% QUESTION 1
% Number: Question 1
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2026, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider the unity negative feedback control system shown in the Figure. <br/>The value of gain [latex]K (&gt;0)[/latex] at which the given system will remain marginally stable is ____. (Answer in integer)<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20261/q49.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20261/q49.webp"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<b>Given:</b><br/>
Open-loop: [latex]\frac{K}{s(s+7)(s+11)}[/latex], unity negative feedback.<br/><br/>
<b>Step 1: Characteristic equation</b><br/><br/>
[latex]
s(s+7)(s+11) + K = 0
[/latex]<br/>
[latex]
s^3 + 18s^2 + 77s + K = 0
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 2: Routh-Hurwitz array</b><br/><br/>
[latex]
\begin{array}{c|cc}
s^3 &amp; 1 &amp; 77 \
s^2 &amp; 18 &amp; K \
s^1 &amp; \frac{18\times77 - K}{18} &amp; 0 \
s^0 &amp; K &amp;
\end{array}
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 3: Marginal stability - [latex]s^1[/latex] row = 0</b><br/><br/>
[latex]
\frac{18\times77 - K}{18} = 0
[/latex]<br/>
[latex]
K = 18\times77 = 1386
[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 2
% Number: Question 2
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2026, Compensators and Controllers
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The state and output equations for a control system are:<br/><br/>[latex]\dot{x} = \begin{bmatrix} -4 &amp; -1.5 \\ 4 &amp; 0 \end{bmatrix} x + \begin{bmatrix} 2 \\ 0 \end{bmatrix} u[/latex]; [latex]y = \begin{bmatrix} 1.5 &amp; 0.625 \end{bmatrix} x[/latex]<br/><br/>Which of the following expressions correctly represents the transfer function [latex]\frac{Y(s)}{U(s)}[/latex] of the system with zero initial conditions?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{3s}{s^2+4s-6}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{3s+5}{s^2+4s-6}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{3s+5}{s^2+4s+6}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{3s}{s^2+4s+6}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<b>Given:</b><br/>
[latex]
\dot{x} = \begin{bmatrix} -4 &amp; -1.5 \\ 4 &amp; 0 \end{bmatrix} x + \begin{bmatrix} 2 \\ 0 \end{bmatrix} u
[/latex]<br/>
[latex]
y = \begin{bmatrix} 1.5 &amp; 0.625 \end{bmatrix} x
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 1: Transfer function formula</b><br/><br/>
[latex]
\frac{Y(s)}{U(s)} = C(sI - A)^{-1}B
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 2: Compute (sI - A)</b><br/><br/>
[latex]
sI - A = \begin{bmatrix} s+4 &amp; 1.5 \\ -4 &amp; s \end{bmatrix}
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 3: Determinant</b><br/><br/>
[latex]
\det(sI-A) = s(s+4) + 6 = s^2 + 4s + 6
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 4: Inverse of (sI - A)</b><br/><br/>
[latex]
(sI-A)^{-1} = \frac{1}{s^2+4s+6}\begin{bmatrix} s &amp; -1.5 \\ 4 &amp; s+4 \end{bmatrix}
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 5: Multiply [latex]
C(sI-A)^{-1} B [/latex]</b><br/><br/>
[latex]
(sI-A)^{-1}B = \frac{1}{s^2+4s+6}\begin{bmatrix} s &amp; -1.5 \\ 4 &amp; s+4 \end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}2\\0\end{bmatrix} = \frac{1}{s^2+4s+6}\begin{bmatrix}2s\\8\end{bmatrix}
[/latex]<br/><br/>
[latex]
\frac{Y(s)}{U(s)} = \begin{bmatrix}1.5 &amp; 0.625\end{bmatrix}\frac{1}{s^2+4s+6}\begin{bmatrix}2s\\8\end{bmatrix}
[/latex]<br/>
[latex]
= \frac{1}{s^2+4s+6}\left(1.5 \times 2s + 0.625 \times 8\right)
[/latex]<br/>
[latex]
= \frac{3s + 5}{s^2 + 4s + 6}
[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 3
% Number: Question 3
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2026, Root Locus
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">For the control system shown in the Figure, the transfer function of a plant,<br/> [latex]G(s) = \frac{1}{(s+1)(s+2)}[/latex] is connected in cascade with a compensator <br/>[latex]C(s) = K(s + \alpha)[/latex], where [latex]K[/latex] and [latex]\alpha[/latex] are positive real valued constants. <br/><br/>Which of the following pairs [latex](K, \alpha)[/latex] represent the correct values for the closed loop system to have poles at [latex](-3 \pm j\sqrt{5})[/latex]?<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20261/q30.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20261/q30.webp"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]2, 3[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]3, 4[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]2, 4[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]3, 3[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<b>Given:</b><br/>
Plant: [latex]G(s) = \dfrac{1}{(s+1)(s+2)}[/latex]<br/>
Compensator: [latex]C(s) = K(s + \alpha)[/latex]<br/>
Desired closed-loop poles: [latex]s = -3 \pm j\sqrt{5}[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 1: Open-loop transfer function</b><br/><br/>
[latex]
L(s) = C(s)G(s) = \frac{K(s+\alpha)}{(s+1)(s+2)}
[/latex]<br/><br/>
Characteristic equation (unity feedback):<br/>
[latex]
1 + L(s) = 0 \Rightarrow (s+1)(s+2) + K(s+\alpha) = 0
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 2: Expand and match with desired polynomial</b><br/><br/>
Desired poles: [latex]s = -3 \pm j\sqrt{5}[/latex]<br/>
Desired characteristic polynomial:<br/>
[latex]
(s+3-j\sqrt{5})(s+3+j\sqrt{5}) = (s+3)^2 + 5 = s^2 + 6s + 14
[/latex]<br/><br/>
Expanding the characteristic equation:<br/>
[latex]
s^2 + 3s + 2 + Ks + K\alpha = 0
[/latex]
[latex]
s^2 + (3+K)s + (2+K\alpha) = 0
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 3: Equate coefficients</b><br/><br/>
[latex]
3 + K = 6 \Rightarrow K = 3
[/latex]<br/>
[latex]
2 + K\alpha = 14 \Rightarrow 2 + 3\alpha = 14 \Rightarrow \alpha = 4
[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 4
% Number: Question 4
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2026, Basics of Control Systems, Block Diagram and SFGs
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A control system is shown in the Figure.<br/> Which option represents the correct transfer function of the system?<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20261/q9.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20261/q9.webp"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{(s+4)^2}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{(s+4)}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{2}{(s+4)}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{(s^2+8s+17)}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<b>Given:</b><br/>
Each block has transfer function<br/>
[latex]
G(s)=\frac{1}{s+4}
[/latex]<br/><br/>
Let the output of the left summing junction be<br/>
[latex]
E(s)
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 1: Output of upper block</b><br/><br/>
The upper block output is the system output:<br/>
[latex]
C(s)=\frac{1}{s+4}E(s)
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 2: Output of lower block</b><br/><br/>
The lower block also receives the same input [latex]E(s)[/latex], hence its output is<br/>
[latex]
\frac{1}{s+4}E(s)
[/latex]<br/><br/>
<b>Step 3: Right summing junction</b><br/><br/>
At the right summing junction,<br/>
- Upper input is positive
- Lower input is negative<br/><br/>
Therefore, [latex]
\frac{1}{s+4}E(s)-\frac{1}{s+4}E(s)=0
[/latex]<br/><br/>
Hence, the feedback signal becomes zero.<br/><br/>
<b>Step 4: Left summing junction</b><br/><br/>
Thus,
[latex]
E(s)=R(s)
[/latex]<br/><br/>
Substituting into the output equation,<br/>
[latex]
C(s)=\frac{1}{s+4}R(s)
[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 5
% Number: Question 5
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2025, Basics of Control Systems, Block Diagram and SFGs
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider a system represented by the block diagram shown below. Which of the following signal flow graphs represent(s) this system? Choose the correct option(s). <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/q46a.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/q46a.webp"/></noscript><br/><br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/q46b.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/q46b.webp"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">A</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">B</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">C</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">D</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Using mason's gain formula, given block diagram<br/>
Overall T.F [latex]=\frac{Y(s)}{R(s)}=\frac{G_{1}+G_{2}}{1+G_{1}}[/latex]<br/>
verify using options
[latex]\frac{\mathrm{Y}(\mathrm{s})}{\mathrm{R}(\mathrm{s})}=\frac{\mathrm{G}_{1}+\mathrm{G}_{2}}{1+\mathrm{G}_{1}+\mathrm{G}_{2}} \rightarrow[/latex] wrong<br/>
[latex]\frac{\mathrm{Y}(\mathrm{s})}{\mathrm{R}(\mathrm{s})}=\frac{\mathrm{G}_{1}+\mathrm{G}_{2}}{1+\mathrm{G}_{1}} \rightarrow[/latex] correct<br/>
[latex]\frac{\mathrm{Y}(\mathrm{s})}{\mathrm{R}(\mathrm{s})}=\frac{\mathrm{G}_{1}+\mathrm{G}_{2}}{1-\mathrm{G}_{1}-\mathrm{G}_{2}} \rightarrow[/latex] wrong<br/>
[latex]\frac{Y(s)}{R(s)}=\frac{G_{1}+G_{2}}{1+G_{1}+G_{2}} \rightarrow[/latex] wrong</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 6
% Number: Question 6
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2025, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider a system where [latex]{x}_{1}({t}), {x}_{2}({t})[/latex], and [latex]{x}_{3}({t})[/latex] are three internal state signals and [latex]u(t)[/latex] is the input signal. The differential equations governing the system are given by<br/><br/>
[latex]\frac{d}{d t}\left[\begin{array}{l}x_{1}(t) \\ x_{2}(t) \\ x_{3}(t)\end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}{ccc}2 &amp; 0 &amp; 0 \\ 0 &amp; -2 &amp; 0 \\ 0 &amp; 0 &amp; 0\end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l}x_{1}(t) \\ x_{2}(t) \\ x_{3}(t)\end{array}\right]+\left[\begin{array}{l}1 \\ 1 \\ 1\end{array}\right] u(t)[/latex].<br/><br/>
Which of the following statements is/are TRUE?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">The signals [latex]x_{1}(t), x_{2}(t)[/latex], and [latex]x_{3}(t)[/latex] are bounded for all bounded inputs</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">There exists a bounded input such that at least one of the signals [latex]x_{1}(t), x_{2}(t)[/latex], and [latex]x_{3}(t)[/latex] is unbounded</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">There exists a bounded input such that the signals [latex]x_{1}(t), x_{2}(t)[/latex], and [latex]x_{3}(t)[/latex] are unbounded</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">The signals [latex]x_{1}(t), x_{2}(t)[/latex], and [latex]x_{3}(t)[/latex] are unbounded for all bounded inputs</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Here given "A" matrix is in the diagonal canonical form [D.C.F] hence<br/>
T.F [latex]=\frac{\mathrm{k}_{1}}{(\mathrm{~s}-2)}+\frac{\mathrm{k}_{2}}{(\mathrm{~s}+2)}+\frac{\mathrm{k}_{3}}{\mathrm{~s}}[/latex]Here poles [latex]\Rightarrow \mathrm{s}=-2,+2,0[/latex]<br/>
<br/> <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/a43.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/a43.webp"/></noscript><br/><br/>
Here one pole is in the right half of s-plane so at least one of the signals [latex]{x}_{1}({t}), {x}_{2}({t})[/latex] and [latex]{x}_{3}({t})[/latex] are unbounded.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 7
% Number: Question 7
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2025, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider the polynomial<br/><br/>
[latex]p(s)=s^{5}+7 s^{4}+3 s^{3}-33 s^{2}+2 s-40[/latex].<br/><br/> Let [latex](L,I,R)[/latex] be defined as follows. <br/>
[latex]L[/latex] is the number of roots of [latex]p(s)[/latex] with negative real parts.<br/>
[latex]I[/latex] is the number of roots of [latex]\mathrm{p}(\mathrm{s})[/latex] that are purely imaginary. <br/>
[latex]R[/latex] is the number of roots of [latex]p(s)[/latex] with positive real parts.<br/><br/>
Which one of the following options is correct?</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\mathrm{L}=2, \mathrm{I}=2[/latex], and [latex]\mathrm{R}=1[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\mathrm{L}=3, \mathrm{I}=2[/latex], and [latex]\mathrm{R}=0[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\mathrm{L}=1, \mathrm{I}=2[/latex], and [latex]\mathrm{R}=2[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\mathrm{L}=0, \mathrm{I}=4[/latex], and [latex]\mathrm{R}=1[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given [latex]p(s)=s^{5}+7 s^{4}+3 s^{3}-33 s^{2}+2 s-40[/latex]
<br/>[latex]
\begin{array}{l|lrl}
s^{5} &amp; 1 &amp; 3 &amp; 2 \\
s^{4} &amp; 7 &amp; -33 &amp; -40 \\
s^{3} &amp; \frac{54}{7} &amp; \frac{54}{7} &amp; 0 \\
s^{2} &amp; -40 &amp; -40 &amp; 0 \\
s^{1} &amp; 0(-80) &amp; 0 &amp; 0 \\
s^{0} &amp; -40 &amp;  &amp;  \\
\end{array}
[/latex]
<br/>
Auxiliary equation [A.E] [latex]-40 \mathrm{~s}^{2}-40=0[/latex]<br/>
A.E [latex]\Rightarrow \mathrm{s}^{2}+1=0 \Rightarrow \mathrm{~s}^{2}=-1 \Rightarrow \mathrm{~s}= \pm 1 \mathrm{j}[/latex]
<br/>
So number of imaginary roots [latex][\mathrm{I}]=2[/latex]<br/>
In the RH tabular, number of sign changes in the first column [latex]=1[/latex], hence number of poles in the right half of s-plane (or) roots with positive real parts [latex][R]=1[/latex], number of poles in the left half of s-plane [latex][\mathrm{L}]=5-2-1=2[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 8
% Number: Question 8
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2025, Compensators and Controllers
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Let [latex]\mathrm{G}(\mathrm{s})=\frac{1}{10 \mathrm{~s}^{2}}[/latex] be the transfer function of a secondorder system. A controller M(s) is connected to the system [latex]\mathrm{G}(\mathrm{s})[/latex] in the configuration shown below.<br/><br/>
Consider the following statements.<br/>
(i) There exists no controller of the form [latex]M(s)=\frac{K_{I}}{s}[/latex], where [latex]K_{I}[/latex] is a positive real number, such that the closed loop system is stable.<br/>
(ii) There exists at least one controller of the form [latex]\mathrm{M}(\mathrm{s})=\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}+\mathrm{s} \mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{D}}[/latex], where [latex]\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{P}}[/latex] and [latex]\mathrm{K}_{\mathrm{D}}[/latex] are positive real numbers, such that the closed loop system is stable.
<br/><br/>
Which one of the following options is correct?<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/q26.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/q26.webp"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">(i) is TRUE and (ii) is FALSE</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">(i) is FALSE and (ii) is TRUE</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Both (i) and (ii) are FALSE</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">Both (i) and (ii) are TRUE</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Statement (i): Given integral controller, integral controller adds pole at origin result system becomes unstable hence for closed loop system stability no integral controller,<br/>
so statement (i) is true.<br/>
Statement (ii): Given PD controller, which adds zero result system becomes stable, hence statement (ii) is also true.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 9
% Number: Question 9
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2025, Root Locus
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider the unity-negative-feedback system shown in Figure (i) below, where gain  [latex]K \geq 0[/latex]. The root locus of this system is shown in Figure (ii) below. <br/><br/>
For what value(s) of  [latex]K[/latex] will the system in Figure (i) have a pole at  [latex]-1+ji[/latex]?  <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/q16.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/q16.webp"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\mathrm{K}=5[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\mathrm{K}=1 / 5[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">For no positive value of [latex]\mathrm{K}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">For all positive values of [latex]\mathrm{K}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
We are given the root locus plot in Figure (ii), and we are to determine whether a closed-loop pole lies at the complex location [latex] -1 + j1 [/latex] for any [latex] K \geq 0 [/latex]. <br/><br/>From control theory, the root locus shows the trajectory of the closed-loop poles as [latex] K [/latex] varies from [latex] 0 [/latex] to [latex] \infty [/latex]. <br/><br/>We inspect the root locus diagram in Figure (ii): <br/>The root locus branches are symmetric about the real axis.
<br/>
The point [latex] -1 + j1 [/latex] lies outside the plotted root locus path.
<br/>
It is not marked or touched by any of the root locus branches. <br/><br/>Hence, the closed-loop poles never pass through the point [latex] -1 + j1 [/latex] for any positive value of [latex] K [/latex].</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 10
% Number: Question 10
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2025, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The Nyquist plot of a system is given in the figure. Let [latex] \omega_P, \omega_Q, \omega_R [/latex] and [latex] \omega_S [/latex] be the positive frequencies at the points [latex]P, Q, R,[/latex] and [latex]S,[/latex] respectively. <br/>
Which one of the following statements is TRUE?<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/q6.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20251/q6.webp"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex] \omega_S [/latex] is the gain crossover frequency and [latex] \omega_P [/latex] is the phase crossover frequency</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex] \omega_Q [/latex] is the gain crossover frequency and [latex] \omega_S [/latex] is the phase crossover frequency</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex] \omega_R [/latex] is the gain crossover frequency and [latex] \omega_S [/latex] is the phase crossover frequency</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex] \omega_S [/latex] is the gain crossover frequency and [latex] \omega_Q [/latex] is the phase crossover frequency</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
From the Nyquist plot: <br/>
The gain crossover frequency is the frequency at which the Nyquist plot crosses the unit circle, i.e., the magnitude of the open-loop transfer function [latex] |G(j\omega)H(j\omega)| = 1 [/latex]. <br/>
The phase crossover frequency is the frequency at which the phase is [latex] -180^\circ [/latex], i.e., the Nyquist plot crosses the negative real axis. <br/><br/>From the diagram: <br/>
Point S lies on the unit circle, so [latex] \omega_S [/latex] is the gain crossover frequency. <br/>
Point P lies on the negative real axis, hence [latex] \omega_P [/latex] is the phase crossover frequency. <br/><br/>Therefore, the correct statement is:
[latex] \omega_S [/latex] is the gain crossover frequency and [latex] \omega_P [/latex] is the phase crossover frequency.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 11
% Number: Question 11
% Type: MSQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2024, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider a system [latex]S[/latex] represented in state space as
<br/>
<br/> [latex]
\frac{d x}{d t}=\left[\begin{array}{ll}
0 &amp; -2 \\
1 &amp; -3
\end{array}\right] x+\left[\begin{array}{l}
1 \\
0
\end{array}\right] r, y=\left[\begin{array}{ll}
2 &amp; -5
\end{array}\right] x
[/latex]<br/>
<br/>
Which of the state space representations given below has/have the same transfer function as that of [latex]S[/latex] ?</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{d x}{d t}=\left[\begin{array}{cc}0 &amp; 1 \\ -2 &amp; -3\end{array}\right] x+\left[\begin{array}{l}1 \\ 0\end{array}\right] r, y=\left[\begin{array}{ll}1 &amp; 2\end{array}\right] x[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{d x}{d t}=\left[\begin{array}{cc}0 &amp; 1 \\ -2 &amp; -3\end{array}\right] x+\left[\begin{array}{l}1 \\ 0\end{array}\right] r, y=\left[\begin{array}{ll}0 &amp; 2\end{array}\right] x[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{d x}{d t}=\left[\begin{array}{cc}-1 &amp; 0 \\ 0 &amp; -2\end{array}\right] x+\left[\begin{array}{c}-1 \\ 3\end{array}\right] r, y=\left[\begin{array}{ll}1 &amp; 1\end{array}\right] x[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{d x}{d t}=\left[\begin{array}{cc}-1 &amp; 0 \\ 0 &amp; -2\end{array}\right] x+\left[\begin{array}{l}1 \\ 1\end{array}\right] r, y=\left[\begin{array}{ll}1 &amp; 2\end{array}\right] x[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
From given state model, (observable form)
<br/> [latex]
T F=C\left[(s I-A)^{-1} B\right]+D
[/latex]<br/>
<br/> [latex]
=\frac{(2 s+1)}{s^{2}+3 s+2}
[/latex]<br/>
From this controllable form can be written as
<br/> [latex]
\begin{aligned}
X^{0} &amp; =\left[\begin{array}{cc}
0 &amp; 1 \\
-2 &amp; -3
\end{array}\right] X+\left[\begin{array}{l}
0 \\
1
\end{array}\right] u \\
Y &amp; =\left[\begin{array}{ll}
1 &amp; 2
\end{array}\right] X+[0] u
\end{aligned}
[/latex]<br/>
Another possibility, [latex]\quad \mathrm{TF}=\frac{2 s+1}{s^{2}+3 s+2}=\frac{-1}{s+1}+\frac{3}{s+2}[/latex]
<br/> [latex]
\begin{aligned}
X^{0} &amp; =\left[\begin{array}{cc}
-1 &amp; 0 \\
0 &amp; -2
\end{array}\right] X+\left[\begin{array}{c}
-1 \\
3
\end{array}\right] v \\
Y &amp; =\left[\begin{array}{ll}
1 &amp; 1
\end{array}\right] X+[0] u
\end{aligned}
[/latex]<br/>
Diagonal form of state mode.</div>

% QUESTION 12
% Number: Question 12
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2024, Compensators and Controllers
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A satellite attitude control system, as shown below, has a plant with transfer function [latex]G(s)=\frac{1}{s^{2}}[/latex] cascaded with a compensator [latex]C(s)=\frac{K(s+\alpha)}{s+4}[/latex], where [latex]K[/latex] and [latex]\alpha[/latex] are positive real constants.
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20241/q28.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20241/q28.webp"/></noscript><br/>
In order for the closed-loop system to have poles at [latex]-1 \pm j \sqrt{3}[/latex], the value of [latex]\alpha[/latex] must be ____</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">1</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">2</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">3</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]
G(S) C(s)=\frac{1}{S^{2}} \frac{k(S+\alpha)}{(S+4)}
[/latex]<br/>
Characteristic equation is
<br/> [latex]
S^{3}+4 S^{2}+k S+\alpha=0
[/latex]<br/>
Poles of the system present at [latex]=-1 \pm j \sqrt{3}[/latex]
<br/>
Characteristic equation is
<br/> [latex]
\begin{aligned}
&amp;(S+a)\left[(S+1)^{2}+3\right]=0 \\
&amp;(S+a)\left(S^{2}+2 S+4\right)=0 \\
&amp; S^{3}+(2+a) S^{2}+(4+2 a) S+4 a=0
\end{aligned}
[/latex]<br/>
Compare equation (I) and (II)
<br/> [latex]
\begin{aligned}
2+a &amp; =4 \\
a &amp; =2 \\
4+2 a &amp; =k \\
k &amp; =8 \\
k \alpha &amp; =4 a \\
\alpha &amp; =\frac{8}{8}=1 \\
\alpha &amp; =1
\end{aligned}
[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 13
% Number: Question 13
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2024, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider a unity negative feedback control system with forward path gain [latex]G(s)=\frac{K}{(s+1)(s+2)(s+3)}[/latex] as shown.
The impulse response of the closed-loop system decays faster than [latex]e^{-t}[/latex] if ______ <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20241/q27.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20241/q27.webp"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]1 \leq K \leq 5[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]7 \leq K \leq 21[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-4 \leq K \leq-1[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-24 \leq K \leq-6[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given,
<br/> [latex]
G(s)=\frac{K}{(S+1)(S+2)(S+3)}
[/latex]<br/>
Impulse response of system is
<br/> [latex]
Y(S)=\frac{K}{(S+1)(S+2)(S+3)+K}
[/latex]<br/>
Given: At impulse response of closed loop system decay faster than [latex]e^{-t}[/latex] Hence, put [latex]s=s-1[/latex],
<br/>
[latex]\therefore[/latex] The new characteristic equation is,
<br/> [latex]
\begin{aligned}
&amp; (s-1+1)(s-1+2)(s-1+3)+K=0 \\
&amp; s(s+1)(s+2)+K=0 \\
&amp; s^{3}+3 s^{2}+2 s+K=0
\end{aligned}
[/latex]<br/>
By [latex]\mathrm{RH}[/latex] criterion,
<br/> [latex]
\begin{array}{c|cc}
s^{3} &amp; 1 &amp; 2 \\
s^{2} &amp; 3 &amp; K \\
s^{1} &amp; \frac{6-K}{3} &amp; \\
s^{0} &amp; K &amp;
\end{array}
[/latex]<br/>
<br/> [latex]
\therefore K \gt 0 ; \quad \frac{6-K}{3} \gt 0 \Rightarrow K \lt 6
[/latex]<br/>
<br/> [latex]
\therefore \quad 0 \lt K \lt 6
[/latex]<br/>
Hence, option satisfies above range of [latex]K[/latex].</div>

% QUESTION 14
% Number: Question 14
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2024, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">In the feedback control system shown in the figure below [latex]G(s)=\frac{6}{s(s+1)(s+2)}[/latex]. <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20241/q3.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20241/q3.webp"/></noscript><br/>
[latex]R(s), Y(s)[/latex], and [latex]E(s)[/latex] are the Laplace transforms of [latex]r(t), y(t)[/latex], and [latex]e(t)[/latex], respectively. If the input [latex]r(t)[/latex] is a unit step function, then _____</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\lim _{t \rightarrow \infty} e(t)=0[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\lim _{t \rightarrow \infty} e(t)=\frac{1}{3}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\lim _{t \rightarrow \infty} e(t)=\frac{1}{4}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\lim _{t \rightarrow \infty} e(t)[/latex] does not exist, [latex]e(t)[/latex] is oscillatory</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given system is<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20241/a3.webp" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20241/a3.webp"/></noscript><br/>
<br/> [latex]
G(s)=\frac{6}{s(s+1)(s+2)}
[/latex]<br/>
The characteristic equation,
<br/> [latex]
\begin{aligned}
1+G(s) &amp; =0 \\
1+\frac{6}{s(s+1)(s+2)} &amp; =0 \\
s(s+1)(s+2)+6 &amp; =0 \\
s^{3}+3 s^{2}+2 s+6 &amp; =0
\end{aligned}
[/latex]<br/>
Clearly, here the internal coefficient product is equal to external coefficient product i.e.,
<br/> [latex]
3 \times 2=6 \times 1
[/latex]<br/>
Hence, the given system is marginally stable system.<br/>
[latex]\therefore e(t)[/latex] not exist because system is oscillatory system.</div>

\end{aligned}
[/latex]</div>

[latex]
\omega_{1} [/latex], system has a zero<br/>
[latex]\therefore[/latex] [latex]a=1[/latex] <br/><br/> and at frequency

% QUESTION 17
% Number: Question 17
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2023, Basics of Control Systems, Block Diagram and SFGs
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">In the following block diagram, [latex]R(s)[/latex] and [latex]D(s)[/latex] are two inputs. The output [latex]Y(s)[/latex] is expressed as [latex]Y(s)=G_{1}(s) R(s)+G_{2}(s) D(s)[/latex].
<br/>
[latex]G_{1}(s)[/latex] and [latex]G_{2}(s)[/latex] are given by
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/q32.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/q32.jpg"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]G_{1}(s)=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)+G(s) H(s)}[/latex] and [latex]G_{2}(s)=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)+G(s) H(s)}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]G_{1}(s)=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)+H(s)}[/latex] and [latex]G_{2}(s)=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)+H(s)}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]G_{1}(s)=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)+H(s)}[/latex] and [latex]G_{2}(s)=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)+G(s) H(s)}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]G_{1}(s)=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)+G(s) H(s)}[/latex] and [latex]G_{2}(s)=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)+H(s)}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a32a.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a32a.jpg"/></noscript><br/>
[latex]
Y(s)=\underbrace{G_{1}(s) R(s)}_{Y_{1}(s)}+\underbrace{G_{2}(s) D(s)}_{Y_{2}(s)}
[/latex]Considering first [latex]R(s)[/latex] only, then [latex]Y(s)[/latex] is [latex]Y_{1}(s)[/latex]<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a32b.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a32b.jpg"/></noscript><br/>
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a32c.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a32c.jpg"/></noscript><br/>[latex]
\begin{aligned}
\frac{Y_{1}(s)}{R(s)}&amp;=\frac{\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s) H(s)}}{1+\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s) H(s)}} \\
\frac{Y_{1}(s)}{R(s)}&amp;=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s) H(s)+G(s)} \\
Y_{1}(s)&amp;=\left[\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)+G(s) H(s)}\right] R(s) \\
G_{1}(s)&amp;=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)+G(s) H(s)}
\end{aligned}
[/latex]
<br/>
Now considering [latex]D(s)[/latex] only, then [latex]Y(s)[/latex] is [latex]Y_{2}(s)[/latex]<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a32d.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/a32d.jpg"/></noscript><br/>[latex]
\begin{aligned}
\frac{Y_{2}(s)}{D(s)} &amp; =\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)[1+H(s)]} \\
Y_{2}(s) &amp; =\left[\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)+G(s) H(s)}\right] D(s) \\
G_{2}(s) &amp; =\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)+G(s) H(s)}
\end{aligned}
[/latex]
<br/>
Hence, [latex]G_{1}(s)[/latex] and [latex]G_{2}(s)[/latex] both are equal.</div>

% QUESTION 18
% Number: Question 18
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2023, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A closed loop system is shown in the figure where [latex]k \gt 0[/latex] and [latex]\alpha \gt 0[/latex]. The steady state error due to a ramp input [latex]\left(R(s)=\alpha / s^{2}\right)[/latex] is given by
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/q31.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20231/q31.jpg"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{2 \alpha}{k}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{\alpha}{k}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{\alpha}{2 k}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{\alpha}{4 k}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given, [latex]\quad[/latex] input is [latex]r(t)=\alpha t u(t)[/latex]
<br/>
[latex]
R(s)=\frac{\alpha}{s^{2}}
[/latex]
<br/><br/>
From the figure,
<br/>
[latex]
G(s) H(s)=\frac{K}{s(s+2)}
[/latex]
<br/>
Now steady state error for Ramp input is
<br/>[latex]e_{s s} =\frac{\alpha}{K_{v}}, \text { where } \alpha \text { is the magnitude of Ramp input } [/latex]<br/>
[latex]
\begin{aligned}K_{v} &amp; =\lim _{s \rightarrow 0}[s G(s) H(s)] \\
K_{v} &amp; =\lim _{s \rightarrow 0}\left[\frac{s \times K}{s(s+2)}\right]=\frac{K}{2}\\
\therefore \quad e_{s s} &amp; =\frac{\alpha \times 2}{K} \\
e_{s s} &amp; =\frac{2 \alpha}{K}
\end{aligned}
[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 19
% Number: Question 19
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2023, Basics of Control Systems, Block Diagram and SFGs
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The open loop transfer function of a unity negative feedback system is [latex]G(s)=\frac{k}{s\left(1+s T_{1}\right)\left(1+s T_{2}\right)}[/latex], where [latex]k, T_{1}[/latex] and [latex]T_{2}[/latex] are positive constants. The phase crossover frequency, in rad/s, is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{\sqrt{T_{1} T_{2}}}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{T_{1} T_{2}}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{T_{1} \sqrt{T_{2}}}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{T_{2} \sqrt{T_{1}}}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
We know phase crossover frequency is that frequency at which phase of the open loop transfer function is [latex]-180^{\circ}[/latex].
<br/><br/>
[latex]
\begin{aligned}
\therefore \quad G(s) &amp; =\frac{K}{s\left(1+s T_{1}\right)\left(1+s T_{2}\right)} \\
G(j \omega) &amp; =\frac{K}{(j \omega)\left(1+j \omega T_{1}\right)\left(1+j \omega T_{2}\right)} \\
\text{Phase of }G(j \omega) &amp; =\phi =-90-\tan ^{-1}\left(\omega T_{1}\right) - \tan ^{-1}\left(\omega T_{2}\right) \\
\therefore \quad \text { At } \omega &amp;=\omega_{p c}, \phi =-180  \\
-180 &amp; =-90-\tan ^{-1}\left(\omega_{p c} T_{1}\right)-\tan ^{-1}\left(\omega_{p c} T_{2}\right) \\
9 \quad 90 &amp; =\tan ^{-1}\left(\omega_{p c} T_{1}\right)+\tan ^{-1}\left(\omega_{p c} T_{2}\right) \\
\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{\omega_{p c} T_{1}+\omega_{p c} T_{2}}{1-\omega_{p c}^{2} T_{1} T_{2}}\right) &amp; =90 \\
1-\omega_{p c}^{2} T_{1} T_{2} &amp; =0 \\
\omega_{p c} &amp; =\frac{1}{\sqrt{T_{1} T_{2}}}
\end{aligned}
[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 20
% Number: Question 20
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2022, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The block diagram of a closed-loop control system is shown in the figure. R(s),Y(s) and D(s) are the Laplace transforms of the time-domain signals [latex]r(t),y(t),[/latex] and [latex]d(t)[/latex], respectively. Let the error signal be defined as [latex]e(t)=r(t)-y(t)[/latex]. Assuming the
reference input [latex]r(t)=0[/latex] for all [latex]t[/latex], the steady-state error [latex]e(\infty )[/latex], due to a unit step
disturbance [latex]d(t)[/latex], is _________ (rounded off to two decimal places).
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20221/q50.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20221/q50.jpg"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]Y(s)=\frac{R(s)\cdot \frac{10}{s(s+10)}}{1+\frac{10}{s(s+10)}} +\frac{D(s)\cdot \frac{10}{s(s+10)}}{1+\frac{10}{s(s+10)}}[/latex]<br/>When,
[latex] r(t)=0\; and \; d(t)=U(t)\underleftrightarrow{L.T.}\frac{1}{s}[/latex]<br/>
[latex] e(\infty )=-\lim_{s \to 0}\frac{s\cdot \frac{1}{s} \times 1}{s(s+10)+10} =-1/10=-0.1\;\;\;(\because e(t)=r(t)-y(t))[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 21
% Number: Question 21
% Type: MSQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2022, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Two linear time-invariant systems with transfer functions<br/> [latex]G_1(s)=\frac{10}{s^2+s+1}, G_2(s)=\frac{10}{s^2+s\sqrt{10}+10} [/latex]<br/>
have unit step responses [latex]y_1(t) [/latex] and [latex]y_2(t) [/latex], respectively. Which of the following
statements is/are true?</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]y_1(t)[/latex] and [latex]y_2(t)[/latex] have the same percentage peak overshoot.</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]y_1(t)[/latex] and [latex]y_2(t)[/latex] have the same steady-state value.</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]y_1(t)[/latex] and [latex]y_2(t)[/latex] have the same damped frequency of oscillation.</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]y_1(t)[/latex] and [latex]y_2(t)[/latex] have the same 2% settling time.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex] \begin{aligned}
G_1(s)&amp;=\frac{10}{s^2+s+1}\\
\omega _{n1}^2&amp;=1\\
2\xi _1 \times 1&amp;=1\\
\xi _1&amp;=1/2\\
G_2(s)&amp;=\frac{10}{s^2+s\sqrt{10}+10}\\
\omega _{n2}^2&amp;=10\\
2\xi _1 \times \sqrt{10}&amp;=\sqrt{10}\\
\xi _2&amp;=1/2\\
\end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>
[latex] \because M_P[/latex]  depends on
[latex]\xi  [/latex] only<br/>
[latex] y_1(t) \; and \; y_2(t)[/latex] have same percentage peak
overshoot.<br/>
[latex]\omega _{d_1}=\omega _{n_1}\sqrt{1-\xi ^2} [/latex]<br/>
[latex]\Rightarrow \omega _{d_1}\neq \omega _{d_2} \;\; \because \omega _{n_1}\neq \omega _{n_2}[/latex]<br/>damped frequency of oscillation is not same.<br/>
[latex] \begin{aligned}
T_s &amp;=\frac{4}{\xi \omega _n}(2%\; setting \; time) \\
T_{s_1} &amp;\neq  T_{s_2} \;\because \omega _{n_1}\neq \omega _{n_2}\\
C_1(s) &amp;= \frac{10 \times 1/s}{s^2+s+1}\\
C_{ss} &amp;=\lim_{s \to 0}s\cdot C_1(s) \\
&amp;= 10\\
C_2(s) &amp;= \frac{10 \times 1/s}{s^2+\sqrt{10}s+10}\\
C_{ss} &amp;=\lim_{s \to 0}s\cdot C_2(s) \\
&amp;= \frac{10}{10}1=1
\end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>Steady - state value is not same.</div>

% QUESTION 22
% Number: Question 22
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2022, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider an even polynomial [latex]p(s)  [/latex] given by<br/> [latex]p(s)=s^4+5s^2+4+K [/latex],<br/>
where [latex]K[/latex] is an unknown real parameter. The complete range of[latex]K[/latex] for which [latex]p(s)  [/latex] has
all its roots on the imaginary axis is ________.</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]-4\leq K\leq \frac{9}{4}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-3\leq K\leq \frac{9}{2}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-6\leq K\leq \frac{5}{4}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-5\leq K\leq 0[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]p(s)=s^4+5s^2+4+k [/latex]<br/>
[latex] \begin{matrix}
s^4 &amp;  1&amp; 5 &amp;(4+k) \\
s^3&amp; 0 &amp; 0 &amp; \\
s^2&amp;  &amp;  &amp; \\
s&amp;  &amp;  &amp; \\
s^0&amp;  &amp;  &amp;
\end{matrix}[/latex]<br/>
[latex] s^4+5s^2+(4+k)=0[/latex]<br/>
[latex] 4s^3+10s=0[/latex]<br/>
[latex](4s^2+10)=0 [/latex]<br/>
[latex]s=\pm j\sqrt{5/2} [/latex]<br/>
[latex] \begin{matrix}
s^4 &amp;  1&amp; 5 &amp;(4+k) \\
s^3&amp; 4 &amp; 10 &amp; \\
s^2&amp; 5/2 &amp; (4+k) &amp; \\
s^1&amp;10-\frac{4(4+k)}{5/2}  &amp;  &amp; \\
s^0&amp; (4+k) &amp;  &amp;
\end{matrix}[/latex]<br/>
[latex] \begin{aligned}
(4+k) &amp; \gt 0\\
k &amp; \gt -4\\
10 \times \frac{5}{2}&amp; \gt 4(4+K)\\
(4+K)&amp; \lt \frac{25}{4}\\
K&amp; \lt 9/4
\end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>
[latex]\Rightarrow  [/latex]  All roots be on imaginary axis
[latex] -4\leq K\leqslant 9/4[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 25
% Number: Question 25
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2021, Compensators and Controllers
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A unity feedback system that uses proportional-integral ([latex]\text{PI}[/latex]
) control is shown in the figure.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20211/q57.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20211/q57.jpg"/></noscript><br/>The stability of the overall system is controlled by tuning the [latex]\text{PI}[/latex] control parameters [latex]K_{P}[/latex]
and [latex]K_{I}[/latex]. The maximum value of [latex]K_{I}[/latex]
that can be chosen so as to keep the overall system stable or, in the worst case, marginally stable (rounded off to three decimal places) is ______</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} G H &amp;=\left(\frac{s K_{p}+K_{I}}{s}\right)\left(\frac{2}{s^{3}+4 s^{2}+5 s+2}\right) \\ q(s) &amp;=s^{4}+4 s^{3}+5 s^{2}+s\left(2+2 k_{p}\right)+2 k_{I} \\ \text{Necessary}:\qquad K_{p} &amp; \gt -1 ; K_{I} \gt 0\\ &amp;\begin{array}{l|ccc} s^{4} &amp; 1 &amp; 5 &amp; 2 K_{I} \\ s^{3} &amp; 4 &amp; 2+2 K_{p} &amp; \\ s^{2} &amp; \frac{18-2 K_{p}}{4} &amp; 2 K_{I} &amp; \\ s^{1} &amp; \left(9-K_{p}\right)\left(1+K_{p}\right)-8 K_{I} &amp; &amp; \\ s^{0} &amp; 2 K_{I} &amp; &amp; \end{array}\\ \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Sufficient:<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \frac{18-2 K_{p}}{4}&amp; \gt 0\\ \Rightarrow \quad K_{p}&amp; \lt 9\\ \therefore \quad-1&amp; \lt K_{p} \lt 9\\ \left(18-2 K_{p}\right)\left(2+2 K_{p}\right)-32 K_{I}&amp; \gt 0\\ 32 K_{I}&amp; \lt 36+32 K_{p}-4 K_{p}^{2}\\ \therefore \qquad \qquad 0&amp; \lt K_{I} \lt \frac{36+32 K_{p}-4 K_{p}^{2}}{32} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \text{If }K_{p}&amp;=-1 \Rightarrow k_{I}=0\\ \text{If }K_{p}&amp;=9 \Rightarrow k_{I}=0\\ \end{aligned}[/latex],<br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} \therefore \qquad \qquad \frac{d K_{I}}{d K_{p}}&amp;=0\\ \Rightarrow \qquad \qquad 32-8 K_{p}&amp;=0=0 \Rightarrow K_{p}=4 \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore [/latex] For [latex]K_{p}=4, K_{I}[/latex] is maximum, which is<br/>[latex] K_{I}=\frac{36+32 \times 4-64}{32}=3.125[/latex]<br/> For [latex]K_{p}=4, K_{I} \lt 3.125[/latex] for stability <br/>[latex] \therefore K_{I \max }=3.125[/latex]<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20211/a57.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20211/a57.jpg"/></noscript><br/></div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 26
% Number: Question 26
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2021, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The electrical system shown in the figure converts input source current [latex]i_{s}(t)[/latex] to output voltage [latex]v_{o}(t)[/latex]<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20211/q41.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20211/q41.jpg"/></noscript><br/>Current i [latex]i_{L}(t)[/latex] in the inductor and voltage [latex]v_{c}(t)[/latex] across the capacitor are taken as the state variables, both assumed to be initially equal to zero, i.e., [latex]i_{L}(0) =0[/latex] and [latex]v_{c}(0)=0[/latex]. The system is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">completely state controllable as well as completely observable</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">completely state controllable but not observable</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">completely observable but not state controllable</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">neither state controllable nor observable</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} i_{s} &amp;=v_{i}+v_{c} \\ \therefore \qquad v_{i} &amp;=-v_{c}+i_{s} \\ v_{L} &amp;=L i_{L} \\ v_{L} &amp;=\left(i_{s}-i_{L}\right) \\ \therefore \qquad L i_{L} &amp;=i_{s}-i_{L} \\ \therefore \qquad i_{L} &amp;=-i_{L}+i_{s} \\ v_{o} &amp;=v_{c} \\ X &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{cc} -1 &amp; 0 \\ 0 &amp; -1 \end{array}\right] X+\left[\begin{array}{ll} 1 \\ 2 \end{array}\right] U \\ Y &amp;=[0 \quad 1] X+[0] \cup \\ A &amp;=-I \\ Q_{C} &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ll} B &amp; A B \end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}{ll} 1 &amp; -1 \\ 1 &amp; -1 \end{array}\right] \Rightarrow\left|Q_{c}\right|=0 \\ Q_{o} &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ll} C^{T} &amp; A^{\top} C^{\top} \end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}{cc} 0 &amp; 0 \\ 1 &amp; -1 \end{array}\right] \Rightarrow\left|Q_{o}\right|=0 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 29
% Number: Question 29
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2020, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider the following closed loop control system <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20201/q55.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20201/q55.jpg"/></noscript><br/> where [latex]G(s)=\frac{1}{s(s+1)}[/latex] and [latex]C(s)=K\frac{s+1}{s+3}[/latex].
If the steady state error for a unit ramp input
is 0.1, then the value of K is _______.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Open loop transfer function for the system [latex]=C(s)\times G(s)=\frac{K(s+1)}{ (s+3)}\times \frac{1}{s(s+1)}[/latex]<br/>Since the system is type-1, so far a given unit ramp input steady state<br/>[latex]e_{ss}=\frac{1}{K_{V}} [/latex]<br/>where,  [latex]K_{V}=\lim_{s\rightarrow 0}S\times \frac{K}{S(S+3)}=\frac{K}{3} [/latex]<br/>so, [latex]\; e_{ss}=\frac{1}{K/3}=\frac{3}{K}[/latex]<br/> Given that,  [latex]\; e_{ss}=0.1 [/latex]<br/>So, [latex]0.1=\frac{3}{K}\Rightarrow K=30[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 30
% Number: Question 30
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2020, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A system with transfer function [latex]G(s)=\frac{1}{(s+1)(s+a)}, a \gt 0[/latex] is subjected an input [latex]5\cos 3t[/latex].
The steady state output of the system is [latex]\frac{1}{\sqrt{10}} \cos (3t-1.892)[/latex]. The value of [latex]a[/latex] is ____.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given that,<br/>[latex]\begin{aligned}G(j\omega )&amp;=\frac{1}{(1+j\omega )(\alpha +j\omega )} \\ \left |G(j\omega ) \right |&amp;=\frac{1}{\sqrt{(\omega ^{2}+1)(\omega ^{2}+\alpha ^{2})}} \\ \text{According to }&amp; \text{question,}\\ \left | G(j\omega ) \right |_{\omega =3}&amp;=\frac{1}{5\sqrt{10}}\\ \Rightarrow \frac{1}{\sqrt{(\omega ^{2}+1)(\omega ^{2}+\alpha ^{2})}}&amp;=\frac{1}{5\sqrt{10}} \\ \Rightarrow \frac{1}{\sqrt{10(a^{2}+9)}}&amp;=\frac{1}{5\sqrt{10}} \alpha ^{2}+9=25 \\ \alpha ^{2}&amp;=16 \\ \alpha &amp;=4\end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 31
% Number: Question 31
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2020, Root Locus
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The characteristic equation of a system is<br/><br/>
[latex]s^3 + 3s^2 + (K + 2)s + 3K = 0[/latex]<br/><br/>
In the root locus plot for the given system, as K varies from 0 to [latex]\infty[/latex], the break-away
or break-in point(s) lie within</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">(-1,0)</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">(-2,-1)</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">(-3,-2)</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">(-[latex]\infty[/latex]
,-3)</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]Q(s)=1+G(s)H(s)=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex]s^{3}+3s^{2}+2s+ks+3k=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex]-k=\frac{s^{3}+3s^{2}+2s}{s+3}[/latex]<br/>[latex] -\frac{\mathrm{d} k}{\mathrm{d} s}=\frac{(s+3)(3s^{2}+6s+2)-(s^{3}+3s^{2}+2s)}{(s+3)^{2}}=0[/latex]<br/>[latex] 3s^{3}+6s^{2}+2s+9s^{2}+18s+6-s^{3}-3s^{2}-2s=0[/latex]<br/>[latex] 2s^{3}+12s^{2}+18s+6=0[/latex]<br/>[latex] s=-0.46,-3.87,-1.65[/latex]<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20201/a37.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20201/a37.jpg"/></noscript><br/>[latex]\therefore[/latex]
Break-away point lies between (0, -1), i.e. (-1, 0)</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 36
% Number: Question 36
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2019, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Let the state-space representation of an LTI system be [latex]\dot{x}(t)=Ax(t)+Bu(t)[/latex],  [latex]\dot{y}(t)=Cx(t)+du(t)[/latex]  where A, B, C are matrices, d is a scalar, u(t) is the input to the system, and y(t) is its output. Let [latex]B=[0\;0\;1]^T[/latex] and d=0. Which one of the following options for A and C will ensure that the transfer function of this LTI system is <br/> [latex]H(s)=\frac{1}{s^3+3s^2+2s+1}[/latex]? <br/><br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20191/q33.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20191/q33.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">A</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">B</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">C</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">D</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} X(t) &amp;=A x(t)+B u(t) \\ y(t) &amp;=C x(t) \\ B &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{l} 0 \\ 0 \\ 1 \end{array}\right] \\ \frac{\gamma(s)}{U(s)} &amp;=\\ \frac{\gamma(s)}{X_{1}(s)} \times \frac{X_{1}(s)}{U(s)}=&amp; 1 \times \frac{1}{s^{3}+3 s^{2}+2 s+1} \\ x_{1}(s)\left(s^{3}+3 s^{2}+2 s+1\right]&amp;=U(s) \\ x_{2} &amp;=\dot{x}_{1}(t) \\ x_{2}(s) &amp;=s x_{1}(s) \\ x_{3} &amp;=\dot{x}_{2}(t) \\ \Rightarrow \quad x_{3}(s) &amp;=s x_{2}(s)=s^{2} X_{1}(s) \\ \text { So, } \quad s x_{3}(s) &amp;=-x_{1}(s)-2 x_{2}(s)-3 x_{3}(s)+u(s) \\ \dot{x}_{3}(t) &amp;=-x_{1}(t)-2 r_{2}(t)-3 x_{3}(t)+u(t) \\ y(t) &amp;=x_{1}(t) \\ \dot{x}(t) &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ccc}0 &amp; 1 &amp; 0 \\ 0 &amp; 0 &amp; 1 \\ -1 &amp; -2 &amp; -3\end{array}\right] x(t)+\left[\begin{array}{l}0 \\ 0 \\ 1\end{array}\right] u(t) \\ y(t) &amp;=[1 \quad 0 \quad 0] x(t) \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

The transfer function [latex]H(s)=\frac{Y(s)}{X(s)}[/latex] is <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20191/q32.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20191/q32.jpg"/></noscript></div>

% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]H(s)=\frac{s+1}{s^2+s+1}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 38
% Number: Question 38
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2019, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider a causal second-order system with the transfer function <br/><br/>[latex]G(s)=\frac{1}{1+2s+s^2}[/latex]<br/><br/> with a unit-step [latex]R(s)=\frac{1}{s}[/latex] as an input. Let C(s) be the corresponding output. The time taken by the system output c(t) to reach 94% of its steady-state value [latex]\lim_{t \to \infty }c(t)[/latex], rounded off to two decimal places, is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">5.25</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">4.5</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">3.89</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">2.81</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} G(s)&amp;=\frac{1}{s^{2}+2 s+1}=\frac{1}{(s+1)^{2}} \\ R(s)&amp;=\frac{1}{s} \\ \alpha (s)&amp;=G(s) R(s)=\frac{1}{s(s+1)^{2}} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Using partial fraction expansion, we get,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} C(s) &amp; =\frac{A}{s}+\frac{B}{(s+1)}+\frac{C}{(s+1)^{2}} \\ A\left(s^{2}+2 s+1\right)&amp;+B\left(s^{2}+s\right)+C s=1 \\ A&amp;=1 \\ A+B &amp; =0 \Rightarrow B=-1 \\ 2 A+B+C &amp; =0 \Rightarrow C=-1 \\ \therefore \quad C(s) &amp; =\frac{1}{s}-\frac{1}{(s+1)}-\frac{1}{(s+1)^{2}} \\ \text{and}\quad c(t) &amp; =\left(1-e^{-t}-t e^{-t}\right) u(t) \\ \lim _{t \rightarrow \infty} c(t) &amp; =1 \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> In order to reach 94 % of its steady-state value,<br/>[latex] \left(1-e^{-t}-t e^{-t}\right)=0.94[/latex]<br/> By trial and error, we get,<br/>[latex] t \approx 4.50 \mathrm{sec}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 40
% Number: Question 40
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2018, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The figure below shows the Bode magnitude and phase plots of a stable transfer function [latex]G(s)=\frac{n_{o}}{s^{2}+d_{2}s^{2}+d_{1}+d_{0}}[/latex]  <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20181/q42.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20181/q42.jpg"/></noscript> Consider the negative unity feedback configuration with gain k in the feedforward path. The closed loop is stable for [latex] k \lt k_{o} [/latex]. The maximum value of [latex]k_{o} [/latex] is ______.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
For G(s) <br/>[latex] M_{\mathrm{dB}}\left(\omega_{p c}\right)=20 \mathrm{dB} [/latex]<br/> When cascaded with k ,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \mathrm{GM}_{\mathrm{dB}} &amp;=-20 \mathrm{dB}-20 \log _{10}(k) \gt 0 \mathrm{dB} \\ 20+20 \log _{10}(k) &amp; \lt 0 \\ 20 \log _{10}(k) &amp; \lt -20 \\ k &amp; \lt 10^{-1}=0.10\\ \text{So,}\quad k_{0}&amp;=0.10 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 41
% Number: Question 41
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2018, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">For a unity feedback control system with the forward path transfer function <br/><br/>[latex]G(s)=\frac{K}{s(s+2)}[/latex] <br/><br/> The peak resonant magnitude [latex]M_{r}[/latex], of the closed-loop frequency response is 2. The corresponding value of the gain K (correct to two decimal places) is _________.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Maximum resonant peak, <br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} M_{r} &amp;=\frac{1}{2 \xi \sqrt{1-\xi^{2}}}=2 \\ 2 \xi \sqrt{1-\xi^{2}} &amp;=\frac{1}{2} \\ \xi^{2}\left(1-\xi^{2}\right) &amp;=\frac{1}{16} \\ \xi^{4}-\xi^{2}+\frac{1}{16} &amp;=0 \\ \xi^{2}&amp;=\frac{1}{2} \pm \sqrt{\frac{1-\frac{1}{4}}{4}}=\frac{1}{2} \pm \frac{\sqrt{3}}{4} \\ \text { As } M_{r}=2 \gt 1, \xi&amp; \lt \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}} \text { and } \xi^{2} \lt \frac{1}{2} \\ \text { So. } \quad\xi^{2}&amp;=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{4}\\ Given, \quad G(s)&amp;=\frac{K}{s(s+2)}=\frac{\omega_{n}^{2}}{s\left(s+2 \xi \omega_{n}\right)} \\ \text{So} \quad\omega_{n} &amp;=\sqrt{K} \\ 2 \xi \sqrt{K} &amp;=2\\ \sqrt{K} &amp;=\frac{1}{\xi} \\ K &amp;=\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}=\frac{1}{\left(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{4}\right)} \\ &amp;=\frac{4}{2-\sqrt{3}}=14.928 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 42
% Number: Question 42
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2018, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The state equation and the output equation of a control system are given below:<br/><br/>[latex]\dot{x}=\begin{bmatrix} -4 &amp; -1.5\\ 4 &amp; 0 \end{bmatrix}x+\begin{bmatrix} 2\\ 0\end{bmatrix}u,[/latex] <br/> [latex]y=\begin{bmatrix} 1.5 &amp; 0.625 \end{bmatrix}x.[/latex]  <br/><br/>The transfer function representation of the system is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{3s+5}{s^{2}+4s+6}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{3s-1.875}{s^{2}+4s+6}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{4s+1.5}{s^{2}+4s+6}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{4s+1.5}{s^{2}+4s+6}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Transfer function<br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} T(s) &amp;=\frac{Y(s)}{U(s)}=\mathrm{C}(s I-A]^{-1} \mathrm{B} \\ A &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{cc} -4 &amp; -1.5 \\ 4 &amp; 0 \end{array}\right] \\ B &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{c} 2 \\ 0 \end{array}\right] \\ C&amp;=[1.5\quad0.625]\\ {[s I-A]} &amp; =\left[\begin{array}{cc}s+4 &amp; 1.5 \\-4 &amp; s\end{array}\right] \\ {[s I-A]^{-1}} &amp; =\frac{1}{\left(s^{2}+4 s+6\right)}\left[\begin{array}{cc}s &amp; -1.5 \\4 &amp; s+4 \end{array}\right] \\ {[s I-A]^{-1} B} &amp; =\frac{1}{s^{2}+4 s+6}\left[\begin{array}{c}2 s \\8 \end{array}\right] &amp; \\ C[s I-A]^{-1} B &amp; =\frac{1}{s^{2}+4 s+6}[1.5 \quad 0.625]\left[\begin{array}{c}2 s \\ 8\end{array}\right] \\ T(s) &amp; =\frac{3 s+5}{s^{2}+4 s+6} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 44
% Number: Question 44
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2017-SET-2, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A second - order LTI system is described by the following state equations, <br/> [latex]\frac{d}{dt}x_{1}(t)- x_{2}(t)=0[/latex]   <br/>        [latex]\frac{d}{dt}x_{2}(t)+2x_{1}(t)+3x_{2}(t)=r(t)[/latex]    <br/>     Where [latex] x_{1}(t)[/latex] and  [latex]x_{2}(t)[/latex] are the two state variables and r(t) denotes the input. The output  [latex] c(t) =x_{1}(t)[/latex]. The system is.</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Undamped (oscillatory)</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Under damped</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Critically damped</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">Over damped</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given state variable equations are as follows:<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} &amp;\frac{d}{d t} x_{1}(t)-x_{2}(t)=0 &amp;\ldots(i)\\ &amp;\frac{d}{d t} x_{2}(t)+2 x_{2}(t)+3 x_{2}(t)=r(t) &amp;\ldots(ii) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Also given that, input [latex]=r(t)[/latex] and output [latex]=x_{1}(t) [/latex]<br/> By applying Laplace transform to equation (i), we<br/> get,<br/>[latex] s X_{1}(s)=X_{2}(s) \qquad \ldots(iii)[/latex]<br/> By applying Laplace transform to equation (ii).<br/> we get,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} s X_{2}(s)+2 X_{1}(s)+3 X_{2}(s)&amp;=R(s) \\ x_{2}(s)&amp;=\frac{R(s)-2 X_{1}(s)}{s+3} &amp;\ldots(iv) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> By substituting equation (iv) in equation (iii), we get,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} s X_{\mathrm{i}}(s)&amp;=\frac{R(s)-2 X_{1}(s)}{s+3}\\ \left(s^{2}+3 s+2\right) X_{1}(s)&amp;=R(s) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>So, the transfer function of the given system is,<br/>[latex]
\begin{aligned}
\frac{X_{1}(s)}{R(s)} &amp;=\frac{1}{s^{2}+3 s+2} \\
v r^{2} &amp;=2 \\
2 \zeta_{n} &amp;=3 \\
\xi &amp;=\frac{3}{2 \omega_{n}}=\frac{3}{2 \sqrt{2}}=1.06
\end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>
As  [latex]\xi \gt 1,[/latex]  the given system is overdamped.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 45
% Number: Question 45
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2017-SET-2, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A unity feedback control system is characterized by the open-loop transfer function   <br/>[latex]G(s)=\frac{2(s+1)}{s^{3}+ks^{2}+2s+1}[/latex] <br/> The value of k for which the system oscillates at 2 rad/s is ________.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
The given open loop transfer function is,<br/>[latex] G(s)=\frac{2(s+1)}{s^{3}+K s^{2}+2 s+1}[/latex]<br/> The closed loop transfer function is,<br/>[latex] T(s)=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)}=\frac{2(s+1)}{s^{3}+K s^{2}+4 s+3}[/latex]<br/> When system oscillates, i.e., when system is marginally stable,<br/>[latex] (1)(3)=K(4) [/latex]<br/>[latex] \mathrm{So}, \quad K=0.75[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 46
% Number: Question 46
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2017-SET-2, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A unity feedback control system is characterized by the open-loop transfer function <br/>[latex]G(s)=\frac{10K(s+2)}{s^{3}+3s^{2}+10}[/latex] <br/>  The Nyquist path and the corresponding Nyquist plot of G(s) are shown in the figures below.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20172/q35.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20172/q35.jpg"/></noscript> <br/> If [latex]0 \lt K \lt 1[/latex], then the number of poles of the closed-loop transfer function that lie in the right half of the s-plane is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">2</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">3</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given that, the open loop transfer function of a  unity feedback system is,<br/>[latex] G(s) =\frac{10 k(s+2)}{s^{3}+3 s^{2}+10} [/latex]<br/> From the given Nyquist plot, for [latex]0 \lt k \lt 1[/latex] the encirclements about the point (-1+j 0) is,<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} N=0 \\ N=P-Z \end{array}[/latex]<br/> P= number of open loop poles in the right half of s-plane<br/> Z= number of closed loop poles in the right half of s-plane<br/> To determine the value of P :<br/> Applying R-H criteria to G(s) <br/>[latex] G(s)=\frac{10 k(s+2)}{s^{3}+3 s^{2}+10}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{c|cc} s^{3} &amp; 1 &amp; 0 \\ s^{2} &amp; 3 &amp; 10 \\ s^{1} &amp; -10 / 3 &amp; 0 \\ s^{0} &amp; 10 &amp; 0 \end{array}[/latex]<br/>Two sign changes are there in the first column of
the RH table. So, two open loop poles are there in
right half of s-plane.<br/>
So,<br/>[latex]
P=2[/latex]<br/>[latex]
\begin{array}{l}
N=P-Z \\
0=2-Z \\
Z=2
\end{array}[/latex]<br/>
Z=2 indicates, there are two closed loop poles
in right half of s-plane.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 49
% Number: Question 49
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2017-SET-2, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider the state space realization <br/> [latex]\begin{bmatrix} x_{1}(t)\\ x_{2}(t) \end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix} 0 &amp; 0\\ 0&amp;-9 \end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix} x_{1}(t)\\ x_{2}(t) \end{bmatrix}+ \begin{bmatrix} 0\\ 45 \end{bmatrix}u(t)[/latex] , with the initial condition [latex]\begin{bmatrix} x_{1}(0)\\ x_{2}(0) \end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix} 0\\ 0 \end{bmatrix}[/latex]  <br/> where u(t) denotes the unit step function. The value of  [latex]\lim_{t\rightarrow \infty }|\sqrt{x_{1}^{2}(t)+x_{2}^{2}(t)}|[/latex]  is _______.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\left[\begin{array}{l} \dot{x}_{1}(t) \\ \dot{x}_{2}(t) \end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}{cc} 0 &amp; 0 \\ 0 &amp; -9 \end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l} x_{1}(t) \\ x_{2}(t) \end{array}\right]+\left[\begin{array}{c} 0 \\ 45 \end{array}\right] u(t)[/latex]<br/> By applying Laplace transform on both sides. get<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} s x_{1}(s)-x_{1}(0)&amp;=0\\ X_{1}(s)&amp;=\frac{x_{1}(0)}{s}=0 \quad \because x_{1}(0)=0 \\ \text{So,} x_{1}(t)&amp;=0 \\ \text{and }\quad s X_{\lambda}(s)-x_{2}(0)&amp;=-9 X_{1}(s)+\frac{45}{s} \\ X_{2}(s) &amp;=\frac{45}{s(s+9)} \quad \because x_{2}(0)=0 \\ \text { Required value } &amp;=\lim _{t \rightarrow \infty}|\sqrt{x_{1}^{2}(t)+x_{2}^{2}(t)}| \\ &amp;=\left|\lim _{t \rightarrow \infty} x_{2}(t)\right| \quad \because x_{1}(t)=0 \\ \lim _{t \rightarrow \infty} x_{2}(t) &amp;=\lim _{s \rightarrow 0} s X_{2}(s)=\frac{45}{9}=5 \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> So,<br/> Required value [latex]= |5| =5[/latex]</div>

Then the closed loop system (having unity gain feedback) is</div>

% QUESTION 56
% Number: Question 56
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-3, Root Locus
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The forward-path transfer function and the feedback-path transfer function of a single loop negative feedback control system are given as <br/><br/>[latex]G(s)=\frac{K(s+2)}{s^{2}+2s+2}[/latex] and H(s)=1, <br/><br/>respectively. If the variable parameter K is real positive, then the location of the breakaway point on
the root locus diagram of the system is __________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} G(s) &amp;=\frac{K(s+2)}{s^{2}+2 s+2} \\ H(s)&amp;=1 \\ 1+G(s) H(s) &amp;=0 \\ 1+\frac{K}{\left(s^{2}\right)+2 s+2}(s+2) &amp;=0\\ K&amp;=-\frac{\left(s^{2}+2 s+2\right)}{(s+2)} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/><br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20163/a48.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20163/a48.jpg"/></noscript><br/>[latex] \frac{d K}{d s}=-\left(\frac{(2 s+2)(s+2)-\left(s^{2}+2 s+2\right)}{(s+2)^{2}}\right) [/latex]<br/> For break away points, [latex] \frac{d K}{d s}=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} (2 s+2)(s+2)-\left(s^{2}+2 s+2\right)=0 \\ 2 s^{2}+6 s+4-s^{2}-2 s-2=0 \\ s^{2}+4 s+2=0 \end{array} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} s&amp;=\frac{-4 \pm \sqrt{16-8}}{2} \\ &amp;=\frac{-4 \pm 2 \sqrt{2}}{2}=-2 \pm \sqrt{2} \\ s&amp;=-0.58 ; s=-3.41 \\ \text{But,}\quad s&amp;=-3.41 \text{ lies on root locus }\\ \text{Hence}\quad s&amp;=-3.41 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 58
% Number: Question 58
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-3, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The first two rows in the Routh table for the characteristic equation of a certain closed-loop control system are given as <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20163/q46.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20163/q46.jpg"/></noscript> <br/>The range of K for which the system is stable is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-2.0 \lt K \lt 0.5[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]0 \lt K \lt 0.5[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]0\lt K\lt \infty[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]0.5\lt K\lt \infty[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{array}{c|cc} s^{3}&amp; 1 &amp; (2 k+3) \\ s^{2}&amp;2 k &amp; 4 \\ s&amp;\frac{4 k^{2}+6 k-4}{2 k} &amp; 0\\ s^{0}&amp;4 \end{array}[/latex]<br/> For stability, [latex]K \gt 0[/latex] <br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} 4 K^{2}+6 K-4&amp; \gt 0 \\ 2 K^{2}+3 K-2&amp; \gt 0 \\ (2 K-1)(K+2)&amp; \gt 0 \\ \Rightarrow \quad K&amp; \gt \frac{1}{2} or, k \lt -2 \\ \text{Hence, }\quad 0.5&amp; \lt K \lt \infty \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 59
% Number: Question 59
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-3, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">For the unity feedback control system shown in the figure, the open-loop transfer function G(s) is given as  <br/><br/>[latex]G(s)=\frac{2}{s(s+1)}[/latex]<br/><br/>. The steady state error [latex]e_{ss}[/latex] due to a unit step input is<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20163/q20.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20163/q20.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">0</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.5</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\infty[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} E(s) &amp;=\frac{R(s)}{1+G(s) H(s)} \\ R(s) &amp;=\frac{1}{s} ; G(s)=\frac{2}{s(s+1)}; \\ H(s) &amp;=1 \\ E(s) &amp;=\frac{\frac{1}{s}}{1+\frac{s}{s(s+1)}}=\frac{s+1}{s^{2}+s+2} \\ e_{s s} &amp;=\lim _{s \rightarrow 0} s E(s) \\ &amp;=\lim _{s \rightarrow 0} \frac{s(s+1)}{s^{2}+s+2} \\ e_{s s} &amp;=0 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 60
% Number: Question 60
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-3, Basics of Control Systems, Block Diagram and SFGs
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The block diagram of a feedback control system is shown in the figure. The overall closed-loop gain G of the system is<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20163/q19.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20163/q19.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]G=\frac{G_{1}G_{2}}{1+G_{1}H_{1}}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]G=\frac{G_{1}G_{2}}{1+G_{1}G_{2}+G_{1}H_{1}}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]G=\frac{G_{1}G_{2}}{1+G_{1}G_{2}H_{1}}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]G=\frac{G_{1}G_{2}}{1+G_{1}G_{2}+G_{1}G_{2}H_{1}}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20163/a19.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20163/a19.jpg"/></noscript><br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} \frac{Y(s)}{X(s)} &amp;=\frac{\frac{G_{2} G_{1}}{1+G_{1} H_{1}}}{1+\frac{G_{2} G_{1}}{1+G_{1} H_{1}}} \\ &amp;=\frac{G_{2} G_{1}}{1+G_{1} G_{2}+G_{1} H_{1}} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 61
% Number: Question 61
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-2, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The asymptotic Bode phase plot of [latex]G(s)=\frac{1}{(s+0.1)(s+10)(s+p_{1})}[/latex], with k and [latex]p_{1}[/latex] both positive, is shown below. The value of [latex]p_{1}[/latex] is ________ <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q47.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q47.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} G(s)=\frac{K}{(s+0.1)(s+10)\left(s+p_{1}\right)} \\ \angle G(s)=-\tan ^{-1} \frac{\omega}{0.1}-\tan ^{-1} \frac{\omega}{10}-\tan ^{-1} \frac{\omega}{p_{1}} \\ \left.\angle G(s)\right|_{\omega=1}=-\tan ^{-1} \frac{1}{0.1}-\tan ^{-1} \frac{1}{10}-\tan ^{-1} \frac{1}{p_{1}} \\ =-135^{\circ} \\ -\tan ^{-1} 10-\tan ^{-1} 0.1-\tan ^{-1} \frac{1}{p_{1}}=-135^{\circ} \\ -84.28^{\circ}-5.71^{\circ}-\tan ^{-1} \frac{1}{p_{1}}=-135^{\circ} \\ -\tan ^{-1} \frac{1}{p_{1}}=-135^{\circ}+90^{\circ}\\ \tan ^{-1} \frac{1}{p_{1}} =45^{\circ} \\ \frac{1}{p_{1}} =1 \Rightarrow p_{1}=1 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 62
% Number: Question 62
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-2, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">In the feedback system shown below [latex]G(s)=\frac{1}{(s+1)(s+2)(s+3)}[/latex]
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q46.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q46.jpg"/></noscript> <br/>The positive value of k for which the gain margin of the loop is exactly 0 dB and the phase margin of the loop is exactly zero degree is ________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]1+G(s) H(s)=1+\frac{K}{(s+1)(s+2)(s+3)}=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex] (s+1)\left(s^{2}+5 s+6\right)+K=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex] s^{3}+5 s^{2}+6 s+s^{2}+5 s+6+k=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow s^{3}+6 s^{2}+11 s+6+K= [/latex]<br/> Gain margin =0dB and phase margin [latex]=0^{\circ}[/latex]<br/> It implies marginal stable system <br/> By Routh Array<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{r|rr} s^{3} &amp; 1 &amp; 11 \\ s^{2} &amp; 6 &amp;(6+k) \\ s &amp; \frac{66-6-K}{6} &amp; 0 \\ s^{\circ} &amp; 6+K &amp; \end{array}[/latex]<br/> For marginal stable system, <br/>[latex] 60-K=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex] K=60[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 63
% Number: Question 63
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-2, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">In the feedback system shown below [latex]G(s)=\frac{1}{(s^{2}+2s)}[/latex]. The step response of the closed-loop system should have minimum settling time and have no overshoot.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q45.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20162/q45.jpg"/></noscript><br/> The required value of gain k to achieve this is ________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} G(s)&amp;=\frac{1}{s^{2}+2 s} \\ \frac{Y(s)}{R(s)}&amp;=\frac{\frac{K}{s^{2}+2 s}}{1+\frac{K}{s^{2}+2 s}}=\frac{K}{s^{2}+2 s+K} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Minimum settling time and no overshoot implies critical damping i.e.<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \xi &amp;=1 \\ \omega_{n} &amp;=\sqrt{K} \\ \therefore \quad 2 \times \xi \cdot \omega_{n} &amp;=2 \\ \omega_{n} &amp;=1 \\ \Rightarrow \quad \sqrt{K} &amp;=1 \text { or } K=1 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 65
% Number: Question 65
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-2, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The response of the system [latex]G(s)=\frac{s-2}{(s+1)(s+3)}[/latex] to the unit step input u(t) is y(t). The value of [latex]\frac{dy}{dt}[/latex] at t=[latex]0^{+}[/latex] is _________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} L\left(\frac{d y}{d t}\right) &amp;=(s Y(s)-y(0)) \\ Y(s) &amp;=G(s) \times \frac{1}{s}=\frac{s-2}{s(s+1)(s+3)} \\ y(0)&amp;=\lim _{s \rightarrow \infty} s Y(s) \\ \text{(Applying } &amp; \text{initial value theorem) } \\ &amp;=\lim _{s \rightarrow \infty} \frac{s-2}{(s+1)(s+3)} \\ &amp;=\frac{\left(1-\frac{2}{s}\right)}{s\left(1+\frac{1}{s}\right)\left(1+\frac{3}{s}\right)} \\ y(0)&amp;=0 \\ L\left(\frac{d y}{d t}\right) &amp;=s Y(s)=\frac{s \times(s-2)}{s(s+1)(s+3)} \\ &amp;=\frac{s-2}{(s+1)(s+3)} \\ \left.\frac{d y}{d t}\right|_{t=0} &amp;=\lim _{s \rightarrow \infty} s L\left(\frac{d y}{d t}\right) \\ &amp;=\lim _{s \rightarrow \infty} \frac{s \times(s-2)}{(s+1)(s+3)}\\ &amp;=\frac{\left(1-\frac{2}{s}\right)}{\left(1+\frac{1}{s}\right)\left(1+\frac{3}{s}\right)}=1 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The transfer function of a linear time invariant system is given by  <br/>[latex]H(s)=2s^{4}-5s^{3}+5s-2[/latex] <br/> The number of zeros in the right half of the s-plane is ________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]2 s^{4}-5 s^{3}+5 s-2=0 [/latex]<br/> By Routh Array, <br/>[latex] \begin{array}{c|ccc} s^{4} &amp; 2 &amp; 0 &amp; -2 \\ s^{3} &amp; -5 &amp; 5 &amp; \\ s^{2} &amp; 2 &amp; -2 &amp; \\ s^{1} &amp; 0(2) &amp; &amp; \\ s^{0} &amp; -2 &amp; &amp; \end{array}[/latex]<br/> Number at sign changes = number at roots (zeros)  in right half of s-plane =3</div>
