% QUESTION 67
% Number: Question 67
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-1, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The open-loop transfer function of a unity-feedback control system is given by <br/> <br/>[latex]G(s)=\frac{K}{s(s+2)}[/latex]<br/> <br/> For the peak overshoot of the closed-loop system to a unit step input to be 10%, the value of K is____________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]G(s)=\frac{K}{s(s+2)} ; H(s)=1 [/latex]<br/> Characteristic equation [latex]=1+G(s) H(s)=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} 1+\frac{K}{s(s+2)} &amp;=0 \\ s^{2}+2 s+K &amp;=0 \Rightarrow \omega_{n}=\sqrt{K} \\ 2 \xi \omega_{n} &amp;=2 ; \xi=\frac{1}{\sqrt{K}} \\ M_{p}&amp;=e^{-\pi\xi/\sqrt{1-\xi^{2}}}-0.1\\ -\frac{\pi \xi}{\sqrt{1-\xi^{2}}} &amp;=\ln (0.1) \\ \Rightarrow \quad\frac{\pi \xi}{\sqrt{1-\xi^{2}}} &amp;=2.3 \\ \pi^{2} \xi^{2} &amp;=(2.3)^{2}\left(1-\xi^{2}\right) \\ 15.16 \xi^{2} &amp;=(2.3)^{2} \\ \Rightarrow \qquad \xi&amp;=0.59 \\ \Rightarrow \quad \text{Also}, \quad K&amp;=\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}=2.8 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 68
% Number: Question 68
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-1, Root Locus
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The open-loop transfer function of a unity-feedback control system is <br/>[latex]G(s)=\frac{K}{s^{2}+5s+5}[/latex] <br/>The value of K at the breakaway point of the feedback control system's root-locus plot is ________</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Characteristic equation  [latex]1+G(s)H(s)=0[/latex]
<br/>[latex]1+\frac{K}{s^2+5s+5}=0[/latex]
<br/>[latex]K=-s^2-5s-5[/latex]<br/>For break away point [latex]\frac{dK}{ds}=0[/latex]<br/>[latex]\frac{dK}{ds}=-2s+5=0\;\Rightarrow \; s=-2.5[/latex]<br/>According to magnitude condition,<br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} |G(s)H(s)|_{s=-2.5} &amp;=1 \\ |G(s)H(s)|_{s=-2.5} &amp;=\frac{|K|}{|(2.5)^2 +5 \times -2.5+5|}=1 \\ |K|&amp;=|(6.25+5-12.5)|=1.25 \\ K&amp;=\pm 1.25 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 69
% Number: Question 69
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-1, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A closed-loop control system is stable if the Nyquist plot of the corresponding open-loop transfer function</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">encircles the s-plane point (-1 + j0) in the counterclockwise direction as many times as the number of right-half s-plane poles.</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">encircles the s-plane point (0 - j1) in the clockwise direction as many times as the number of right-half s-plane poles.</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">encircles the s-plane point (-1 + j0) in the counterclockwise direction as many times as the number of left-half s-plane poles</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">encircles the s-plane point (-1 + j0) in the counterclockwise direction as many times as the number of right-half s-plane zeros.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
N= P-Z<br/>
N = Number of encirclements of (-1 + /J). It is
positive if nyquist plot encircles the point -1 + j0
in counterclockwise direction.<br/>
Z = Number of closed loop poles lying in the right
half of s-plane<br/>
P = Number of open loop poles lying in right half
of s-plane<br/>
For stability Z = 0 [latex]\Rightarrow[/latex] N = P</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 70
% Number: Question 70
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2016-SET-1, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Match the inferences X, Y, and Z, about a system, to the corresponding properties of the elements of first column in Routh?s Table of the system characteristic equation. <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20161/q19.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20161/q19.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">X[latex]\rightarrow[/latex]P, Y[latex]\rightarrow[/latex]Q, Z[latex]\rightarrow[/latex]R</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">X[latex]\rightarrow[/latex]Q, Y[latex]\rightarrow[/latex]P, Z[latex]\rightarrow[/latex]R</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">X[latex]\rightarrow[/latex]R, Y[latex]\rightarrow[/latex]Q, Z[latex]\rightarrow[/latex]P</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">X[latex]\rightarrow[/latex]P, Y[latex]\rightarrow[/latex]R, Z[latex]\rightarrow[/latex]Q</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
When all elements are positive, the system is
stable. When any element is zero, the test breaks
down. When there is change in sign of
coefficients, the system is unstable.</div>

% QUESTION 71
% Number: Question 71
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-3, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The characteristic equation of an LTI system is given by [latex]F(s)=s^{5}+2s^{4}+3s^{3}+6s^{2}-4s-8=0[/latex] The number of roots that lie strictly in the left half s-plane is _________.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Using Routh's tabular or:<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{c|lll} s^{5} &amp; 1 &amp; 3 &amp; -4 \\ s^{4} &amp; 2 &amp; 6 &amp; -8 \\ s^{3} &amp; 0 8 &amp; 0(12) &amp; 0 \\ s^{2} &amp; 3 &amp; -8 &amp; 0 \\ s^{1} &amp; \frac{100}{3} &amp; 0 &amp; 0 \\ s^{0} &amp; -8 &amp; &amp; \end{array}[/latex]<br/> Auxiliary equation:<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} A &amp;=2 s^{4}+6 s^{2}-8=0 \\ \frac{d A}{d s} &amp;=8 s^{3}+12 s=0 \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> As one time row of zero occur in Routh's table therefore a pair of imaginary pole exists, also  number of sign change in Routh's table is one. Hence, two poles lie strictly in the left half of s-plane.</div>

% QUESTION 72
% Number: Question 72
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-3, Root Locus
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">For the system shown in the figure, s = -2.75 lies on the root locus if K is _______.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20153/q47.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20153/q47.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
The open loop transfer function of the system:<br/>[latex] G(s) H(s)=\frac{K(s+3) \times 10}{(s+2)}[/latex]<br/> In order to lie a point s=-2.75, the angle condition must be satisfy and therefore the magnitude condition is<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} &amp;\mid G(s) H(s) \|_{s=-275}=1 \\ &amp;=\left.\frac{10 K \sqrt{s^{2}+3^{2}}}{\sqrt{s^{2}+2^{2}}}\right|_{s=-2.75}=1 \\ &amp;=\frac{10 K \sqrt{(3-2.75)^{2}}}{\sqrt{(2-2.75)^{2}}}=1 \\ &amp;=\frac{0.25 \times 10 K}{0.75} or K=0.3 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 73
% Number: Question 73
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-3, Basics of Control Systems, Block Diagram and SFGs
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The position control of a DC servo-motor is given in the figure. The values of the parameters are [latex]K_{T}=1 N-m/A[/latex], [latex]R_{a}=1 \Omega [/latex], [latex] L_{a}=0.1H,J=5kg-m^{2} [/latex], [latex]B=1 N-m(rad/sec)[/latex] and [latex] K_b=1V/(rad/sec)[/latex] . The steady-state position response (in radians) due to unit impulse disturbance torque [latex]T_{d}[/latex] is_______.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20153/q46.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20153/q46.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
The transfer function due to the disturbance torque [latex] T_{d}(s) \text { is } [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \frac{\theta(s)}{T_{d}(s)}&amp;=\frac{-\frac{1}{(J s+B)} \times \frac{1}{s}}{1+\left(\frac{1}{J s+B}\right)\left(\frac{K_{b} K_{T}}{R_{a}+L_{a} s}\right)} \\ &amp;=\frac{-\left(R_{a}+L_{a} s\right) \cdot \frac{1}{s}}{\left(R_{a}+L_{a} s\right)(J s+B)+K_{b} K_{T}} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> The steady value of response for unit impulse input<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} &amp;=\frac{-s\left(R_{a}+L_{a} s\right) \cdot \frac{1}{s}}{\left(R_{a}+L_{a} s\right)(J s+B)+K_{b} K_{T}}.T_{d}\\ &amp;=-\frac{R_{a}}{R_{a} B+K_{b} K_{T}} \cdot 1\\ \text{Given:}\\ K_{T}&amp;=1 \mathrm{N}-\mathrm{m} / \mathrm{A} R_{a}=1 \Omega \\ B&amp;=1 \mathrm{N}-\mathrm{m} / \mathrm{rad} / \mathrm{sec}\\ \text{and }\quad K_{b}&amp;=1 \mathrm{V} / \mathrm{rad} / \mathrm{sec} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Substituting the given values into above equation,  we get<br/>[latex] \theta(0)=-\frac{1}{2}=-0.5 \mathrm{rad}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 74
% Number: Question 74
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-3, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A network is described by the state model as <br/>[latex]\dot{x_1}=2x_{1}-x_{2}+3u [/latex]<br/>[latex]\dot{x_2}=-4x_{2}-u[/latex]<br/>[latex] y=3x_{1}-2x_{2}[/latex] <br/> The transfer function [latex]H(s)(=\frac{Y(s)}{U(s)})[/latex] is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{11s+35}{(s-2)(s+4)}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{11s-35}{(s-2)(s+4)}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{11s+38}{(s-2)(s+4)}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{11s-38}{(s-2)(s+4)}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
State matrix form:<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} \dot{x}=\left[\begin{array}{cc} 2 &amp; -1 \\ 0 &amp; -4 \end{array}\right] x+\left[\begin{array}{c} 3 \\ -1 \end{array}\right] u \\ y=\left[\begin{array}{ll} 3 &amp; -2 \end{array}\right] x \end{array}[/latex]<br/> The transfer function from tate matrix can be calculated as:<br/>[latex] T(S)=C(s l-A]^{-1} B+D[/latex]<br/> Here,<br/>[latex] [s l-A]^{-1}=\left[\begin{array}{cc} s-2 &amp; 1 \\ 0 &amp; s+4 \end{array}\right]^{-1}[/latex]<br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} &amp;=\frac{1}{(s-2)(s+4)}\left[\begin{array}{cc} (s+4) &amp; -1 \\ 0 &amp; (s-2) \end{array}\right] \\ \Rightarrow &amp;C[s /-A]^{-1} B \\ =&amp; \frac{1}{(s-2)(s+4)}[3-2]\left[\begin{array}{cc} (s+4) &amp; -1 \\ 0 &amp; (s-2) \end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{c} 3 \\ -1 \end{array}\right] \\ =&amp; \frac{1}{(s-2)(s+4)}[3-2]\left[\begin{array}{c} 3 s+12+1 \\ -s+2 \end{array}\right] \\ =&amp; \frac{1}{(s-2)(s+4)}[9 s+39+2 s-4] \\ =&amp; \frac{11 s+35}{(s-2)(s+4)} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 75
% Number: Question 75
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-3, Compensators and Controllers
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The transfer function of a first-order controller is given as <br/><br/>[latex]G_{c}(s)=\frac{K(s+a)}{s+b}[/latex] <br/><br/> where K, a and b are positive real numbers. The condition for this controller to act as a phase lead compensator is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]a \lt b[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]a \gt b[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]K \lt ab[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]K \gt ab[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
For phase lead compensator<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} G_{c}(s)&amp;=\frac{(1+s \tau)}{(1+\alpha s \tau)} \quad ; \quad \alpha \lt 1\\ \text{Here,}\quad \tau&amp;=\frac{1}{a}\\ \text{and}\quad \alpha \tau&amp;=\frac{1}{b}\\ \text{or,}\quad \alpha&amp;=\frac{a}{b} \lt 1\\ \text{or,}\quad a&amp; \lt b \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 76
% Number: Question 76
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-3, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The phase margin (in degrees) of the system [latex]G(s)=\frac{10}{s(s+10)}[/latex] is _______.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} G(s) &amp;=\frac{10}{s(s+10)} \\ P M &amp;=180^{\circ}+\left.\angle G(\omega) H(\omega)\right|_{\omega=\omega_{g c}} \end{aligned} [/latex]<br/> The gain cross over frequency can be calculated by<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} |G(\omega) H(\omega)|&amp;=1 \\ \frac{10}{\omega \sqrt{\omega^{2}+100}}&amp;=1 \\ \omega_{g c}^{4}+100 \omega_{g c}^{2}&amp;-100=0\\ \text { Or } \quad \omega_{g c}^{2}&amp;=0.99, \quad-100.99 \\ \text { Or } \quad \omega_{g c}&amp;=0.99 \text { rad/sec. } \\ \therefore P M &amp;=180^{\circ}+\left(-90^{\circ}-\tan ^{-1} \frac{\omega_{g c}}{10}\right) \\ &amp;=180^{\circ}-90^{\circ}-\tan ^{-1} \frac{0.99}{10} \\ &amp; \simeq 84.364^{\circ} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 77
% Number: Question 77
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-3, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider the Bode plot shown in the figure. Assume that all the poles and zeros are real-valued. <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20153/q19.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20153/q19.jpg"/></noscript><br/> The value of [latex]f_{H}-f_{L}[/latex] (in Hz) is ___________.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
As we know,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \text { Slope } &amp;=\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}} \\ \therefore \quad 40 \mathrm{dB} / \mathrm{dec} &amp;=\frac{40-0}{\log 300-\log _{L}}\\ or, \log \left(\frac{300}{f_{L}}\right)&amp;=\frac{40}{40}=1 f_{L}=30 \mathrm{Hz} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Similarly,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} -40 \mathrm{dB} / \mathrm{dec}&amp;=\frac{40-0}{\log 900-\log f_{H}}\\ \log \left(\frac{900}{f_{H}}\right) &amp;=-1 \\ \text { or, } \quad f_{H}&amp;=9000 \mathrm{Hz} \\ \text { Therefore, } f_{H}-f_{L}&amp;=9000-30 =8970 \mathrm{Hz} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 78
% Number: Question 78
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-2, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The transfer function of a mass-spring-damper system is given by <br/><br/>[latex]G(s)=\frac{1}{Ms^{2}+Bs+K}[/latex]<br/><br/>  The frequency response data for the system are given in the following table. <img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20152/q48.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20152/q48.jpg"/></noscript><br/>The unit step response of the system approaches a steady state value of ________ .</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\frac{\alpha(s)}{R(s)}=G(s)=\frac{1}{M s^{2}+B s+K} [/latex]<br/> Steady state value of response for unit step input<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} C(s)&amp;=\lim _{s \rightarrow 0} s \times \frac{1}{M s^{2}+B s+K} \times \frac{1}{s}=\frac{1}{K} \\ \text{Also}&amp;\\ G(\approx 0)&amp;=\frac{1}{K}=-18.5 \mathrm{dB} \\ &amp;\qquad\text{(as per the given table)}\\ \therefore \frac{1}{K}&amp;=10^{\left(-\frac{18.5}{20}\right)}=0.12 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 79
% Number: Question 79
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-2, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The output of a standard second-order system for a unit step input is given as [latex]y(t)=1-\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}e^{-t}cos(\sqrt{3}t-\frac{\pi }{6})[/latex]. The transfer function of the system is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{2}{(s+2)(s+\sqrt{3})}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{s^2+2s+1}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{3}{s^2+2s+3}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{4}{s^2+2s+4}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
The output of a second order system for unit step <br/> input<br/>[latex] y(t)=1-\frac{e^{-\xi \omega_{n} t}}{\sqrt{1-\xi^{2}}} \sin \left(\omega_{d} t-\theta\right) \qquad\ldots(i)[/latex]<br/> From given data<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} y(t)&amp;=1-\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}} e^{-t} \cos \left(\sqrt{3} t-\frac{\pi}{6}\right)\\ \therefore \quad \alpha&amp;=\xi \omega_{n}=1 \\ \text{and }\quad \omega_{d}&amp;=\sqrt{3} \\ \omega_{n}&amp;=\sqrt{\alpha^{2}+\omega_{d}^{2}}=2 \\ \xi&amp;=\frac{1}{2} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Therefore, the transfer function<br/>[latex] T(s)=\frac{\omega_{n}^{2}}{s^{2}+2 \xi \omega_{n} s+\omega_{n}^{2}}=\frac{4}{s^{2}+2 s+4}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 80
% Number: Question 80
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-2, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The state variable representation of a system is given as [latex]x=\begin{bmatrix} 0 &amp; 1\\ 0&amp;-1 \end{bmatrix}x; \; x(0)=\begin{bmatrix} 1\\ 0 \end{bmatrix} \; y= \begin{bmatrix} 0 &amp;1 \end{bmatrix}x[/latex]<br/> The response y(t) is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">sin(t)</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1-e^{t}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1-cos(t)</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">0</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
The response of the system is given by <br/>[latex] x(t)=L^{-1}\left[(s l-A)^{-1} x(0)+(s /-A)^{-1} B \cdot U(s)\right] [/latex]<br/> and the complete response<br/>[latex] y(t)=[C] x(t) [/latex]<br/> From the given state model,<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} A=\left[\begin{array}{ll}0 &amp; 1 \\ 0 &amp; -1\end{array}\right] ; B=0: C=[0 \quad 1] \; and \;  x(0)=\left[\begin{array}{c}1 \\ 0\end{array}\right] \\ \therefore \quad(s l-A)^{-1}=\left[\begin{array}{cc}s &amp; -1 \\ 0 &amp; s+1\end{array}\right]^{-1} \\ =\frac{1}{s(s+1)}\left[\begin{array}{cc}s+1 &amp; 1 \\ 0 &amp; s\end{array}\right] \\ (s l-A)^{-1} x(0)=\frac{1}{s(s+1)}\left[\begin{array}{cc} s+1 &amp; 1 \\ 0 &amp; s \end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{c} 1 \\ 0 \end{array}\right] \\ =\frac{1}{s(s+1)}\left[\begin{array}{c} s+1 \\ 0 \end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}{c} 1 / s \\ 0 \end{array}\right] \\ \Rightarrow x(t)=L^{-1}\left[(s I-A)^{-1} x(0)\right] \\ =\left[\begin{array}{c} 1 \\ 0 \end{array}\right] u(t) \end{array}[/latex]<br/>and the complete response <br/>[latex] y(t)=\left[\begin{array}{lll}0 &amp; 1\end{array}\right] x(t)=\left[\begin{array}{ll}0 &amp; 1\end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l}1 \\ 0\end{array}\right]=0[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 81
% Number: Question 81
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-2, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A unity negative feedback system has an open-loop transfer function [latex]G(s)=\frac{K}{s(s+10)}[/latex]. The gain for the system to have a damping ratio of 0.25 is ________.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]T(s)=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s) H(s)}=\frac{K}{s^{2}+10 s+K} [/latex]<br/> Comparing with standard second order transfer  function<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} T(s)&amp;=\frac{\omega_{n}^{2}}{s^{2}+2 \xi \omega_{n} s+\omega_{n}^{2}}\\ \text{We have, }\omega_{n}^{2}&amp;=K \\ \text{and }\quad 2 \xi \omega_{n}&amp;=10 \\ \because \quad \xi&amp;=0.25 \quad (given)\\ \therefore \quad \omega_{n}&amp;=\frac{10}{2 \times 0.25}=20 \\ \text{and }\quad k&amp;=\omega_{n}^{2}=(20)^{2}=400 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">For the signal flow graph shown in the figure, the value of [latex]\frac{C(s)}{R(s)}[/latex]  is <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20152/q20.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20152/q20.jpg"/></noscript><br/></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{G_{1}G_{2}G_{3}G_{4}}{1-G_{1}G_{2}H_{1}-G_{3}G_{4}H_{2}-G_{2}G_{3}H_{3}+G_{1}G_{2}G_{3}G_{4}H_{1}H_{2}}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{G_{1}G_{2}G_{3}G_{4}}{1+G_{1}G_{2}H_{1}+G_{3}G_{4}H_{2}+G_{2}G_{3}H_{3}+G_{1}G_{2}G_{3}G_{4}H_{1}H_{2}}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{1+G_{1}G_{2}H_{1}+G_{3}G_{4}H_{2}+G_{2}G_{3}H_{3}+G_{1}G_{2}G_{3}G_{4}H_{1}H_{2}}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{1-G_{1}G_{2}H_{1}-G_{3}G_{4}H_{2}-G_{2}G_{3}H_{3}+G_{1}G_{2}G_{3}G_{4}H_{1}H_{2}}[/latex]</div>

Transfer function<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \frac{C(s)}{R(s)}&amp;=\frac{\Sigma P_{k} \Delta_{k}}{\Delta} \\ =&amp; \frac{G_{1} G_{2} G_{3} G_{4}}{1-\left(-G_{1} G_{2} H_{1}-G_{2} G_{4} H_{2}-G_{2} G_{3} H_{3}\right)+G_{1} G_{2} H_{1} \cdot G_{3} G_{4} H_{2}} \\ \frac{C(s)}{R(s)}&amp;= \frac{G_{1}G_{2}G_{3}G_{4}}{1+G_{1}G_{2}H_{1}+G_{3}G_{4}H_{2}+G_{2}G_{3}H_{3}+G_{1}G_{2}G_{3}G_{4}H_{1}H_{2}} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 83
% Number: Question 83
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-2, Basics of Control Systems, Block Diagram and SFGs
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">By performing cascading and/or summing/differencing operations using transfer function blocks [latex]G_{1}(s) \; and \;  G_{2}(s)[/latex], one CANNOT realize a transfer function of the form</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]{G_{1}(s)}{G_{1}(s)}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{G_{1}(s)}{G_{1}(s)}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]G_{1}(s)(\frac{1}{G_{1}(s)}+G_{2}(s))[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]G_{1}(s)(\frac{1}{G_{1}(s)}-G_{2}(s))[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given blocks has transfer function [latex]G_{1}(s)[/latex] and [latex] G_{2}(s) [/latex]<br/> In cascade connection [latex] G_{1}(s) \cdot G_{2}(s) [/latex]<br/> In parallel connection [latex]G_{1}(s)+G_{2}(s) [/latex]<br/> From the given options:<br/> (For cascading/ summing/differncing)<br/> (A) [latex]G_{1}(s) G_{2}(s) \Rightarrow [/latex]Realization is possible.<br/> (B) [latex]\frac{G_{1}(s)}{G_{2}(s)} \Rightarrow[/latex] Realization is not possible because <br/> the gain of blocks given as [latex]G_{1}(s) [/latex] and [latex]G_{2}(s) [/latex]<br/> (C) [latex]G_{1}(s)\left(\frac{1}{G_{1}(s)}+G_{2}(s)\right)=1+G_{1}(s) G_{2}(s) [/latex]<br/> Realization is possible.<br/> (D) [latex]G_{1}(s)\left(\frac{1}{G_{1}(s)}-G_{2}(s)\right)=1-G_{1}(s) G_{2}(s) [/latex]<br/> Realization is possible.</div>

% QUESTION 84
% Number: Question 84
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-1, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A plant transfer function is given as [latex]G(s)=(K_{P}+\frac{K_{I}}{s})\frac{1}{s(s+2)}[/latex]. When the plant operates in a unity feedback configuration, the condition for the stability of the closed loop system is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]K_{P}\gt \frac{K_{I}}{2} \gt  0[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]2K_{I}\gt K_{P} \gt 0[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]2K_{I} \lt K_{P}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]2K_{I} \gt K_{P}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]G(s)=\left(K_{p}+\frac{K_{I}}{s}\right)\left(\frac{1}{s(s+2)}\right)[/latex]<br/> The closed loop transfer function for unity feedback<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)} &amp;=\frac{\left(K_{p} s+K_{I}\right)}{s^{2}(s+2)+\left(K_{p} s+K_{I}\right)} \\ &amp;=\frac{K_{p} s+K_{I}}{s^{3}+2 s^{2}+K_{p} s+K_{I}} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Using Routh's tabular form:<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{c|cc} s^{3} &amp; 1 &amp; K_{p} \\ s^{2} &amp; 2 &amp; K_{I} \\ s^{1} &amp; \frac{2 K_{p}-K_{I}}{2} &amp; 0 \\ s^{0} &amp; K_{p} &amp; \end{array}[/latex]<br/> For system to be stable;<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} K_{p}&amp; \gt 0\\ \text{and }\frac{2 K_{p}-K_{I}}{2}&amp; \gt 0 \\ \text{or} \quad 2 K_{p}-K_{I}&amp; \gt 0 \\ \text{or} \quad K_{p}&amp; \gt \frac{K_{I}}{2} \gt 0 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 85
% Number: Question 85
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-1, Compensators and Controllers
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">A lead compensator network includes a parallel combination of R and C in the feed-forward path. If the transfer function of the compensator is [latex]G(s)=\frac{s+2}{s+4}[/latex] , the value of RC is ________.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]G_{C}(s)=\frac{s+2}{s+4} \quad\dots(i)[/latex]<br/> For lead compensator,<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20151/a47.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20151/a47.jpg"/></noscript><br/><br/> Transfer function [latex]=\frac{1+s \tau}{1+\alpha s \tau} \quad\dots(ii)[/latex]<br/> Where, <br/>[latex] \tau=[/latex] Lead time constant [latex]=R_{1} C [/latex]<br/> and [latex]\alpha=\frac{R_{2}}{R_{1}+R_{2}} [/latex]<br/> Comparing equation (i) and (ii), we get<br/>[latex] \tau=\frac{1}{2} \quad [/latex] and [latex]\quad \alpha \tau=\frac{1}{4} [/latex]<br/> or, [latex]\alpha=\frac{1}{2} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore [/latex] RC time constant =0.5</div>

% QUESTION 86
% Number: Question 86
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-1, Root Locus
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The open-loop transfer function of a plant in a unity feedback configuration is given as [latex]G(s)=\frac{K(s+4)}{(s+8)(s^{2}-9)}[/latex] . The value of the gain K(&gt; 0) for which -1+ j2 lies on the root locus is_______.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} G(s) &amp;=\frac{K(s+4)}{(s+8)\left(s^{2}-9\right)} \\ &amp;=\frac{K(s+4)}{(s+8)(s+3)(s-3)} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> For the point (-1+2 j) to lie on the root locus the angle condition must be satisfy <br/> i.e. [latex]\angle G(s) H(s)=\pm(2 Q+1) 180^{\circ} [/latex]<br/> Taking LHS:<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} \left.\angle G(s) H(s)\right|_{s=-1+2 j} \\ =\frac{\angle K+\angle(s+4)}{\angle(s+8)+\angle(s+3)+\angle(s-3)} \\ =\frac{\angle K+\angle(-1+2 j+4)}{\angle(-1+2 j+8)+\angle(-1+2 j+3)+\angle(-1+2 j-3)} \\ =\frac{0+\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{3}\right)}{\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{2}{7}\right)+\tan ^{-1}(1)+180^{\circ}-\tan ^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)} \\ =33.69^{\circ}-15.945^{\circ}-45^{\circ}-180^{\circ}+26.565^{\circ} \\ \simeq 180^{\circ} \end{array}[/latex]<br/>As angle condition is satisfied the value of system gain K can be obtained by using magnitude<br/> condition<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} i.e. |G(s) H(s)|_{s=-1+2 j}=1 \\ =\frac{K \sqrt{(-1+4)^{2}+2^{2}}}{\sqrt{(-1+8)^{2}+2^{2}} \cdot \sqrt{(-1+3)^{2}+2^{2}} \cdot \sqrt{(-1-3)^{2}+2^{2}}} \\ =1 \\ =\frac{K \sqrt{9+4}}{\sqrt{49+4} \sqrt{4+4} \sqrt{16+4}}=1 \\ =\frac{K \sqrt{13}}{\sqrt{53} \sqrt{8} \sqrt{20}}=1 \\ K=\frac{\sqrt{53 \times 8 \times 20}}{\sqrt{13}}=25.54 \end{array}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 87
% Number: Question 87
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-1, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The polar plot of the transfer function [latex]G(s)=\frac{10(s+1)}{s+10}[/latex] for [latex]0\leq \omega \lt  \infty[/latex] will be in the</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">first quadrant</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">second quadrant</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">third quadrant</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">fourth quadrant</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]G(s)=\frac{10(s+1)}{(s+10)}[/latex]<br/> For [latex]\omega=0 \Rightarrow M_{1}=1[/latex] and [latex]\phi_{1}=0^{\circ} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \omega=0 \Rightarrow M_{2}=10 [/latex]<br/> and [latex]\phi_{2}=-\tan ^{-1} \infty+\tan ^{-1} \infty=0^{\circ} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore[/latex] Polar plot<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20151/a21.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20151/a21.jpg"/></noscript><br/><br/>As the zero is nearer to the imaginary axis hence
the direction of polar plot is clockwise.</div>

% QUESTION 88
% Number: Question 88
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2015-SET-1, Root Locus
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A unity negative feedback system has the open-loop transfer function [latex]G(s)=\frac{K}{s(s+1)(s+3)}[/latex]. The value of the gain K ([latex]\gt[/latex]0) at which the root locus crosses the imaginary axis is ________.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]G(s)=\frac{K}{s(s+1)(s+3)}[/latex]<br/> The characteristic equation<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} =1+G(s) H(s)=0 \\ =s(s+1)(s+3)+K=0 \\ =s^{3}+4 s^{2}+3 s+K=0 \end{array}[/latex]<br/> Using Routh's tabular form<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{c|cc} s^{3} &amp; 1 &amp; 3 \\ s^{2} &amp; 4 &amp; K \\ s^{1} &amp; \frac{12-K}{4} &amp; 0 \\ s^{0} &amp; K &amp; \end{array}[/latex]<br/> In order to cross the imaginary axis, system <br/> should be marginally stable<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \frac{12-K}{4}&amp;=0\\ \text{or }\quad K&amp;=12 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 90
% Number: Question 90
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-4, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The characteristic equation of a unity negative feedback system is [latex]1 + KG(s) = 0[/latex]. The open loop transfer function G(s) has one pole at 0 and two poles at -1. The root locus of the system for varying K is shown in the figure. <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20144/q48.jpg"/><noscript><img src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20144/q48.jpg"/></noscript><br/> The constant damping ratio line  for [latex]\xi= 0.5[/latex], intersects the root locus at point A. The distance from the origin to point A is given as 0.5. The value of K at point A is ____.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
According to the question <br/>[latex] G(s) H(s)=\frac{K}{s(s+1)^{2}} \qquad \ldots(i)[/latex]<br/><br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20144/a48.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20144/a48.jpg"/></noscript><br/> The damping ratio is given as [latex]\xi=0.5[/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore \quad \theta=\cos ^{-1} \xi=60^{\circ}[/latex]<br/> Let the co-ordinates of point 'A' is (-a+jb) <br/> where, [latex]\cos 60^{\circ}=\frac{O B}{O A} \qquad \ldots(ii)[/latex]<br/> Also, [latex]\quad O A=0.5 [/latex] (Given)<br/>[latex] \therefore O B=0.25[/latex]<br/> Similarly,<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} \sin 60^{\circ}=\frac{B A}{O A} \qquad \ldots(iii)\\ \therefore \quad B A=j b=0.433 j \end{array}[/latex]<br/> Hence, co-ordinates of [latex]A = -0.25 + 0.43j[/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore [/latex] The value of system gain K can be obtained as<br/>[latex] |G(s) H(s)|=1\qquad \ldots(iv)[/latex]<br/> From (i) and (iv) we get [latex]s=-0.25+j 4.33 [/latex]<br/>[latex] K=0.37[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 91
% Number: Question 91
% Type: NAT | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-4, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider a transfer function [latex]G_{p}(s)=\frac{ps^{2}+3ps-2}{s^{2}+(3+p)s+(2-p)}[/latex] with [latex]p[/latex] a positive real parameter. The maximum value of [latex]p[/latex] until which [latex]G_{p}[/latex] remains stable is ____.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given transfer function<br/>[latex] G_{p}(s)=\frac{p s^{2}+3 p s-2}{s^{2}+(3+p) s+(2-p)} \qquad\ldots(i)[/latex]<br/> The characteristic equation is<br/>[latex] =s^{2}+(3+p) s+(2-p)\qquad\ldots(ii)[/latex]<br/> Using Routh Hurtwiz criterion,<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l|ll} s^{2} &amp; 1 &amp; (2-P) \\ s^{1} &amp; (3+p) &amp; 0 \\ s^{0} &amp; (2-p) &amp; 0 \end{array}[/latex]<br/> For system to be remain stable<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} 2-p &amp; \geq 0 \\ p_{\max } &amp;=1.99 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 92
% Number: Question 92
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-4, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The state transition matrix [latex]\phi (t)[/latex] of a system [latex]\begin{bmatrix} \dot{x}_{1}\\ \dot{x}_{2} \end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix} 0 &amp; 1\\ 0&amp;0 \end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix} x_{1}\\ x_{2} \end{bmatrix}[/latex] is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\begin{bmatrix} t &amp; 1\\ 1&amp;0 \end{bmatrix}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\begin{bmatrix} 1 &amp; 0\\ t&amp;1 \end{bmatrix}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\begin{bmatrix} 0 &amp; 1\\ 1&amp;t \end{bmatrix}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\begin{bmatrix} 1 &amp; t\\ 0&amp;1 \end{bmatrix}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \text { Given } &amp;\left[\begin{array}{l} \dot{x}_{1} \\ \dot{x}_{2} \end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}{ll} 0 &amp; 1 \\ 0 &amp; 0 \end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l} x_{1} \\ x_{2} \end{array}\right] \\ A &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ll} 0 &amp; 1 \\ 0 &amp; 0 \end{array}\right] \\ [s I-A] &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ll} s &amp; 0 \\ 0 &amp; s \end{array}\right]-\left[\begin{array}{ll} 0 &amp; 1 \\ 0 &amp; 0 \end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}{ll} s &amp; -1 \\ 0 &amp; s \end{array}\right] \\ [s I-A]=s^{2} &amp; \\ \phi(t) &amp;=\mathcal{L}^{-1}[s I-A]^{-1} \\ &amp;=\frac{1}{s^{2}}\left[\begin{array}{ll} s &amp; 1 \\ 0 &amp; s \end{array}\right] \\ &amp;=L^{-1}\left[\begin{array}{cc} \frac{1}{s} &amp; \frac{1}{s^{2}} \\ 0 &amp; \frac{1}{s} \end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}{ll} 1 &amp; t \\ 0 &amp; 1 \end{array}\right] \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 93
% Number: Question 93
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-4, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">For the second order closed-loop system shown in the figure, the natural frequency (in rad/s) is <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20144/q21.jpg"/><noscript><img src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20144/q21.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">16</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">4</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">2</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
The closed loop transfer function of the given <br/> system is<br/>[latex] \frac{C(s)}{R(s)}=\frac{4}{s(s+4)+4}=\frac{4}{s^{2}+4 s+4} [/latex]<br/> Comparing with standard second order transfer <br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \text{function }\quad &amp;\frac{K \omega_{n}^{2}}{S^{2}+2 \xi \omega_{n}+\omega_{n}^{2}} \\ \text{we get, }\quad \omega_{n}^{2}&amp;=4 \\ \text{or }\quad\omega_{n}&amp;=\sqrt{4}=2 \mathrm{rad} / \mathrm{sec} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 94
% Number: Question 94
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-4, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">In a Bode magnitude plot, which one of the following slopes would be exhibited at high frequencies by a [latex]4^{th}[/latex] order all-pole system ?</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]-80 dB/decade[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-40 dB/decade[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]+40 dB/decade[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]+80 dB/decade[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
A [latex]4^{\text {th }}[/latex]  order all-pole system means that the system
must be having no zero or s-terms in numerator or
and [latex]s^{4}[/latex]  terms in denominator or 4 -poles.<br/>
i.e.  [latex]H(s) \propto \frac{1}{s^{4}} [/latex]<br/>
One pole exhibits -20 dB/decade slope, so 4 pole
exhibits a slope of -80 dB/decade.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 96
% Number: Question 96
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-3, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The state equation of a second-order linear system is given by <br/><br/>
[latex]\dot{x}(t) = Ax(t), \;\; x(0) = x_{0}[/latex]<br/><br/> For  [latex]x_{0}=\begin{bmatrix} 1\\ -1 \end{bmatrix}, \; x(t)=\begin{bmatrix} e^{-t}\\ -e^{-t} \end{bmatrix}[/latex] and  for [latex] x_{0}=\begin{bmatrix} 0\\ 1 \end{bmatrix},[/latex] [latex] x(t)=\begin{bmatrix} e^{-t}-e^{-2t}\\ -e^{-t}+2e^{-2t} \end{bmatrix} [/latex] <br/><br/> when  [latex] x_{0}=\begin{bmatrix} 3\\ 5 \end{bmatrix}[/latex], x(t) is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\begin{bmatrix} -8e^{-t} +11e^{-2t}\\ 8e^{-t} -22e^{-2t} \end{bmatrix}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\begin{bmatrix} 11e^{-t} -8e^{-2t}\\ -11e^{-t} +16e^{-2t} \end{bmatrix}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\begin{bmatrix} 3e^{-t} -5e^{-2t}\\ -3e^{-t} +10e^{-2t} \end{bmatrix}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\begin{bmatrix} 5e^{-t} -3e^{-2t}\\ -5e^{-t} +6e^{-2t} \end{bmatrix}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given [latex]\dot{x}(t)=A x(t), \quad x(0)=x_{0} [/latex]<br/> Taking the Laplace transform<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} s X(s)-x(0) &amp;=A X(s) \\ [s I-A] X(s) &amp;=x(0) \\ X(s) &amp;=[s I-A]^{-1} x(0) \\ x(t) &amp;=\mathcal{L}^{-1}[s I-A]^{-1} x(0) &amp;\ldots(i) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Condition given are <br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} For x_{0}&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{c}1 \\ -1\end{array}\right], x(t)=\left[\begin{array}{c}e^{-t} \\ -e^{-t}\end{array}\right] \\ For x_{0}&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{l}0 \\ 1\end{array}\right], x(t)=\left[\begin{array}{l}e^{-t}-e^{-2 t} \\ -e^{-t}+2 e^{-2 t}\end{array}\right] \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>Using the linearity property in equation (i) <br/>[latex] K_{1} x_{1}(t)=\mathcal{L}^{-1}[s I-A]^{-1} x_{1}(0) K_{1} [/latex]<br/>[latex] K_{2} x_{2}(t)=\mathcal{L}^{-1}[s I-A]^{-1} x_{2}(0) K_{2} [/latex]<br/> Using the linearity property as <br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} K_{1} x_{1}(t)+K_{2} x_{2}(t)&amp;=\mathcal{L}^{-1}[s I-A]^{-1} \\  \left[K_{1} x_{1}(0)+K_{2} x_{2}(0)\right] &amp; \quad \ldots(ii)\\ \text { Also } \quad X_{3}(s)&amp;=[s I-A]^{-1} x_{3}(0) \\ \text{So, } \quad K_{1}\left[\begin{array}{r}1 \\ -1\end{array}\right]+&amp;K_{2}\left[\begin{array}{l}0 \\ 1\end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}{l}3 \\ 5\end{array}\right] \\ \left[\begin{array}{l}K_{1}+O K_{2} \\ -K_{1}+K_{2}\end{array}\right]&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{l}3 \\ 5\end{array}\right] \\ \Rightarrow \quad K_{1}&amp;=3 \\ K_{2}&amp;=8 \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> So, from equation (ii), we get x(t)<br/>
[latex]\begin{aligned} x(t) &amp;=K_{1} x_{1}(t)+K_{2} x_{2}(t) \\ &amp;=3\left[\begin{array}{c} e^{-t} \\ -e^{-t} \end{array}\right]+8\left[\begin{array}{c} e^{-t}-e^{-2 t} \\ -e^{-t}+2 e^{-2 t} \end{array}\right] \\ &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{c} 11 e^{-t}-8 e^{-2 t} \\ -1 t e^{-t}+16 e^{-2 t} \end{array}\right] \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{G_{1}}{1+G_{1}G_{2}}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20143/a20a.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20143/a20a.jpg"/></noscript><br/>Converting the block d iagram into signal flow<br/><br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20143/a20b.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20143/a20b.jpg"/></noscript><br/> graph on <br/> Forward paths, <br/>[latex] P_{1}=G_{1} G_{2} ; P_{2}=G_{2} \cdot 1 ; P_{3}=1 \cdot 1=1 [/latex]<br/> So, the transfer function is<br/>[latex] \frac{C(s)}{R(s)}=G_{1} G_{2}+G_{2}+1[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 99
% Number: Question 99
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-2, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">Consider the state space system expressed by the signal flow diagram shown in
the figure. <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/q48.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/q48.jpg"/></noscript><br/> The corresponding system is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">always controllable</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">always observable</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">always stable</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">always unstable</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
The state equation from the given state diagram is<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \dot{x}_{1} &amp;=x_{2} \\ \dot{x}_{2} &amp;=x_{3} \\ \dot{x}_{3} &amp;=a_{3} x_{3}+a_{2} \cdot x_{2}+a_{1} x_{1}+u \\ \text { Also, } y &amp;=c_{1} x_{1}+c_{2} x_{2}+c_{3} x_{3} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Thus, state matrix <br/>[latex] \left[\begin{array}{c}\dot{x}_{1} \\ \dot{x}_{2} \\ \dot{x}_{3}\end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}{ccc}0 &amp; 1 &amp; 0 \\ 0 &amp; 0 &amp; 1 \\ a_{1} &amp; a_{2} &amp; a_{3}\end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{c}x_{1} \\ x_{2} \\ x_{3}\end{array}\right]+\left[\begin{array}{c}0 \\ 0 \\ 1\end{array}\right] u [/latex]<br/> and [latex]y=\left[\begin{array}{lll}c_{1} &amp; c_{2} &amp; c_{3}\end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l}x_{1} \\ x_{2} \\ x_{3}\end{array}\right] [/latex]<br/> Check for controllability:<br/> The system is said to be controllable if, the rank  of controllability matrix [latex]Q_{c}[/latex] is equal to the rank of the state matrix A . However, if the controllability matrix [latex]Q_{c}[/latex] is a square matrix then the condition for controllability is<br/>[latex] \left|o_{c}\right| \neq 0 [/latex]<br/> where <br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} Q_{c}&amp;=\left[B: A B: A^{2} B\right]\\ \therefore Q_{c}&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ccc}0 &amp; 0 &amp; 1 \\ 0 &amp; 1 &amp; a_{3} \\ 1 &amp; a_{3} &amp; a_{2}+a_{3}^{2}\end{array}\right] \\ \because\left|Q_{C}\right| \neq &amp; 0 \text{ and Rank of } Q_{c}=\text{ Rank of }A=1 \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore[/latex] The system is controllable.<br/> Check for observability:<br/> The system is said to be observable if, the rank of observability matrix [latex]Q_{o}[/latex] is equal to the rank of the state matrix A . However, if the observability matrix [latex]Q_{o}[/latex] is a square matrix then the condition for observability is <br/>[latex] \left|0_{0}\right| \neq 0 [/latex]<br/> where <br/> [latex]\begin{aligned} Q_{0}&amp;=\left[C^{T}: A^{T} C^{T}:\left(A^{T}\right)^{2} C^{T}\right] \\ O_{0}&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ccc}c_{1} &amp; c_{2} &amp; c_{3} \\ s_{3} a_{1} &amp; c_{1}+c_{2} a_{2} &amp; c_{2}+c_{2} a_{3} \\ c_{1}\left(c_{2}+c_{3} a_{3}\right) &amp; c_{3} a_{1}+\left(c_{2}+c_{3} a_{3}\right) a_{2} &amp; c_{1}+c_{2} a_{2}+\left(c_{2}+c_{3} a_{3}\right) a_{3}\end{array}\right] \end{aligned} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \because\left|Q_{0}\right| [/latex] depends on value of unknown. <br/> Hence, not observable always.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">The Bode asymptotic magnitude plot of a minimum phase system is shown in
the figure <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/q47.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/q47.jpg"/></noscript><br/> If the system is connected in a unity negative feedback configuration, the steady state error of the closed loop system, to a unit ramp input, is_____.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/a47.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/a47.jpg"/></noscript><br/>The open loop transfer function of the system <br/>[latex] G(s) H(s)=\frac{K\left(\frac{s}{2}+1\right)}{s\left(\frac{s}{10}+1\right)}\qquad\ldots(i)[/latex]<br/> where,<br/>[latex] \left.26.02\right|_{\omega=0.1}=20 \log K-20 \log \omega\qquad\ldots(ii)[/latex]<br/> Or, [latex]\quad K=1.99 \simeq 2[/latex] From equation (i) and (ii), we get<br/>[latex] G(s)=\frac{20(s+2)}{2 s(s+10)}\qquad\ldots(iii)[/latex]<br/> Steady state error for ramp input is<br/>[latex] e_{s s}=\frac{A}{K_{V}} \text { where } K_{V}=\lim _{s \rightarrow 0} s G(s)[/latex]<br/> From equation (iii),<br/>[latex] K_{V}=\lim _{s \rightarrow 0} s \times \frac{10(s+2)}{s(s+10)}=2[/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore \quad e_{s s}=\frac{1}{2}=0.5[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 101
% Number: Question 101
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-2, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">An unforced linear time invariant (LTI) system is represented by<br/> [latex]\begin{vmatrix} \dot{x}_{1}\\ \dot{x}_{2} \end{vmatrix}=\begin{vmatrix} -1 &amp; 0\\ 0&amp;-2 \end{vmatrix}\begin{vmatrix} x_{1}\\ x_{2} \end{vmatrix}[/latex]<br/> If the initial conditions are [latex]x_{1}(0)=1 \; and \; x_{2}(0)=-1[/latex], the solution of the state equation is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]x_{1}(t)=-1, x_{2}(t)=2[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]x_{1}(t)=-e^{-t}, x_{2}(t)=2e^{-t}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]x_{1}(t)=e^{-t}, x_{2}(t)=-e^{-2t}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]x_{1}(t)=-e^{-t}, x_{2}(t)=-2e^{-t}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned}\left[\begin{array}{l}\dot{x}_{1} \\ \dot{x}_{2}\end{array}\right] &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{cc}-1 &amp; 0 \\ 0 &amp; -2\end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l}x_{1} \\ x_{2}\end{array}\right] \\ \dot{x}_{1} &amp;=-x_{1}+0 x_{2} \\ \dot{x}_{2} &amp;=0 x_{1}-2 x_{2} \end{aligned} [/latex]<br/> Applying Laplace transform on both equations <br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \Rightarrow s X_{1}(s)-x_{1}(0)&amp;=-X_{1}(s) \\ X_{1}(s)[s+1]&amp;=X_{1}(0) \quad &amp;\text{given }x_{1}(0)=1 \\ X_{1}(s)&amp;=\frac{1}{s+1} \\ \Rightarrow \quad e^{-t}&amp;=x_{1}(t)\\ \Rightarrow \quad s X_{2}(s)-X_{2}(0)&amp;=-2 X_{2}(s) \\ (s+2) X_{2}(s)&amp;=X_{2}(0) &amp;X_{2}(0)=-1 \\ X_{2}(s)&amp;=-\frac{1}{s+2} \\ x_{2}(t)&amp;=-e^{-2 t} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% Number: Question 102
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-2, Basics of Control Systems, Block Diagram and SFGs
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">For the following system, <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/q21.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/q21.jpg"/></noscript><br/> when [latex]X_{1}(s) = 0[/latex], the transfer function [latex]\frac{Y(s)}{X_{2}(s)}[/latex]  is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{s+1}{s^{2}}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{s+1}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{s+2}{s(s+1)}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{s+1}{s(s+2)}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Redrawing the block diagram with [latex]X_{1}(s)=0 [/latex]<br/><br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/a21.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20142/a21.jpg"/></noscript><br/> The transfer function<br/>[latex] T(s)=\frac{Y(s)}{X_{2}(s)}=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s) H(s)} \quad\ldots(i)[/latex]<br/> Here, [latex]G(s)=\frac{1}{s}[/latex] and [latex]H(s)=\frac{s}{s+1} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \frac{Y(s)}{X_{2}(s)}=\frac{1 / s}{1+\frac{1}{s} \times \frac{s}{s+1}}=\frac{(s+1)}{s(s+2)}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 103
% Number: Question 103
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-2, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The natural frequency of an undamped second-order system is 40 rad/s. If the
system is damped with a damping ratio 0.3, the damped natural frequency in
rad/s is ______.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \text { Given, } &amp; \omega_{n}=40 \mathrm{rad} / \mathrm{sec} \\ &amp; \xi=0.3 \\ \therefore \quad &amp; \omega_{d}=\omega_{n} \sqrt{1-\xi^{2}}=38.16 \mathrm{rad} / \mathrm{sec} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 104
% Number: Question 104
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 2 Marks
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-1, Compensators and Controllers
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="2">For the following feedback system [latex]G(s)=\frac{1}{(s+1)(s+2)}[/latex]. The 2% settling time of the step response is required to be less than 2 seconds.
Which one of the following compensators C(s) achieves this ? <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20141/q48.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20141/q48.jpg"/></noscript> <br/></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]3 (\frac{1}{s+5})[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]5 (\frac{0.03}{s}+1)[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]2 (s+4)[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]4 (\frac{s+8}{s+3})[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given open loop transfer function is<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} G(s)&amp;=\frac{1}{(s+1)(s+2)} \\ \therefore T(s)&amp;=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)}=\frac{1}{(s+1)(s+2)+1} \\ T(s)&amp;=\frac{1}{s^{2}+3 s+3} &amp;\ldots(i) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Comparing equation (i) with standard transfer function<br/>[latex] \xi \omega_{n}=\frac{3}{2}=1.5 [/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore [/latex] 2% settling time<br/>[latex] \tau_{s}=\frac{4}{\xi \omega_{n}}=\frac{4}{1.5}=2.67&gt;2 \mathrm{sec} [/latex]<br/> Thus, in order to make settling time [latex]\left(\tau_{s}\right)[/latex] less than 2 sec, PD controller should be used. Hence, option (C) is the correct choice. <br/> Where, [latex]C(s)=2(s+4) [/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore [/latex] New transfer function [latex]=T^{\prime}(G)[/latex]<br/>[latex] =\frac{C(s) G(s)}{1+C(s) G(s)}=\frac{2(s+4)}{s^{2}+3 s+2+2 s+8} [/latex]<br/> or [latex]T^{\prime}(s)=\frac{2(s+4)}{s^{2}+5 s+10}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore \quad \tau_{s}=\frac{4}{\xi \omega_{n}}=\frac{4}{5 / 2}=\frac{4}{2.5}=1.6[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

calculated using the asymptotic Bode plot is _____.</div>

% QUESTION 108
% Number: Question 108
% Type: NAT | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2014-SET-1, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The forward path transfer function of a unity negative feedback system is given
by <br/> [latex]G(s)=\frac{K}{(s+2)(s-1)}[/latex] <br/> The value of K which will place both the poles of the closed-loop system at the same location, is ______.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Given that, [latex]\quad G(s)=\frac{K}{(s+2)(s-1)} [/latex]<br/> Using root locus method, the break point can be obtain as<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20141/a20.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20141/a20.jpg"/></noscript><br/><br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \Rightarrow \quad 1+G(s)&amp;=0 \\ 1+\frac{K}{(s+2)(s-1)} &amp;=0 \\ \text{or }\quad K&amp;=-(s+2)(s-1)\\ \frac{d K}{d s} &amp;=-2 s-1=0 \\ \text{or }\quad s &amp;=-0.5 \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> To have, both the poles at the same directions<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} |G(s)|_{s=0.5} &amp;=1 \\ K &amp;=2.25 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

A system is described by the state-variable equations [latex]\dot{X}= AX+Bu[/latex] ; [latex]y=CX+Du[/latex]<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20131/q54.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20131/q54.jpg"/></noscript> <br/>The state transition matrix [latex]e^{At}[/latex] of the system shown in the figure above is</div>

% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\begin{bmatrix} e^{-t} &amp;0 \\ -te^{-t} &amp; e^{-t} \end{bmatrix}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 110

% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2013, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The state diagram of a system is shown below. A system is described by the state-variable equations<br/>[latex]\dot{X}= AX+Bu[/latex];  [latex]y=CX+Du[/latex]<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20131/q54.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20131/q54.jpg"/></noscript> <br/>The state-variable equations of the system shown in the figure above are</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\dot{X}= \begin{bmatrix} -1 &amp;0 \\ 1 &amp; -1 \end{bmatrix}X+\begin{bmatrix} -1\\ 1 \end{bmatrix}u[/latex]
[latex]y=\begin{bmatrix} 1 &amp; -1 \end{bmatrix}X+u[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\dot{X}= \begin{bmatrix} -1 &amp;0 \\ -1 &amp; -1 \end{bmatrix}X+\begin{bmatrix} -1\\ 1 \end{bmatrix}u[/latex] [latex]y=\begin{bmatrix} -1 &amp; -1 \end{bmatrix}X+u[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\dot{X}= \begin{bmatrix} -1 &amp;0 \\ -1 &amp; -1 \end{bmatrix}X+\begin{bmatrix} -1\\ 1 \end{bmatrix}u[/latex] [latex]y=\begin{bmatrix} -1 &amp; -1 \end{bmatrix}X-u[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\dot{X}= \begin{bmatrix} -1 &amp;-1 \\ 0 &amp; -1 \end{bmatrix}X+\begin{bmatrix} -1\\ 1 \end{bmatrix}u[/latex] [latex]y=\begin{bmatrix} 1 &amp; -1 \end{bmatrix}X-u[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20131/a54.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20131/a54.jpg"/></noscript><br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} \mathrm{So}, \dot{x}_{1} &amp;=-x_{1}-u \\ \dot{x}_{2} &amp;=-\left(x_{2}+\dot{x}_{1}\right)=-\left(x_{2}-x_{1}-u\right) \\ \dot{x}_{2} &amp;=x_{1}-x_{2}+u \\ y &amp;=\dot{x}_{2} \\ y &amp;=x_{1}-x_{2}+u \\ \dot{x}_{2} &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{rr}-1 &amp; 0 \\1 &amp; -1\end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{c} x_{1} \\x_{2}\end{array}\right]+\left[\begin{array}{c}-1 \\1 \end{array}\right] u \\ \dot{x} &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{rr} -1 &amp; 0 \\ 1 &amp; -1 \end{array}\right] X+\left[\begin{array}{c} -1 \\ 1 \end{array}\right] u \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 111
% Number: Question 111
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2013, Basics of Control Systems, Block Diagram and SFGs
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The signal flow graph for a system is given below. The transfer function [latex]\frac{Y\left ( s \right )}{U\left ( s \right )}[/latex] for this system is<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20131/q40.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20131/q40.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{s+1}{5s^{2}+6s+2}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{s+1}{s^{2}+6s+2}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{s+1}{s^{2}+4s+2}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{5s^{2}+6s+2}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Using Mason's gain formula,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \Delta &amp;=1-\left[-2 s^{-1}-2 s^{-2}-4-4 s^{-1}\right] \\ \Delta &amp;=1+\frac{2}{s}+\frac{2}{s^{2}}+4+\frac{4}{s} \\ \Delta &amp;=\frac{5 s^{2}+6 s+2}{s^{2}} \\ P_{1} &amp;=s^{2}=\frac{1}{s^{2}} \\ P_{2} &amp;=s^{-1}=\frac{1}{s} \\ \Delta_{1} &amp;=1 \\ \Delta_{2} &amp;=1 \\ \frac{Y(s)}{U(s)} &amp;=\frac{\sum P_{k} \Delta_{k}}{\Delta}=\frac{\frac{1}{s^{2}} \times 1+\frac{1}{s} \times 1}{\frac{5 s^{2}+6 s+2}{s^{2}}} \\ \frac{Y(s)}{U(s)} &amp;=\frac{s+1}{5 s^{2}+6 s+2} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 112
% Number: Question 112
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2013, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A system described by a linear, constant coefficient, ordinary, first order differential equation has an exact solution given by y(t) for t &gt; 0, when the forcing function is x(t) and the initial condition is y(0). If one wishes to modify the system so that the solution becomes -2y(t) for t &gt; 0, we need to</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">change the initial condition to -y(0) and the forcing function to 2x(t)</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">change the initial condition to 2y(0) and the forcing function to -x(t)</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">change the initial condition to [latex]j\sqrt{2}y\left ( 0 \right )[/latex] and the forcing function to [latex]j\sqrt{2}x\left ( t \right )[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">change the initial condition to -2y(0) and the forcing function to -2x(t)</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\frac{d y(t)}{d t}+k y(t)=x(t) [/latex]<br/> Taking Laplace transform of both sides, we have <br/>[latex] s Y(s)-y(0)+k Y(s)=X(s) [/latex]<br/>[latex] Y(s)[s+k]=X(s)+y(0) [/latex]<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow Y(s)=\frac{X(s)}{s+k}+\frac{y(0)}{s+k} [/latex]<br/> Taking inverse Laplace transform, we have <br/>[latex] y(t)=e^{-k t} x(t)+y(0) e^{-k t} [/latex]<br/> So if we want -2y(t) as a solution both x(t) and  y(0) has to be multiplied by -2; hence change x(t) by -2x(t) and y(0) by -2y(0).</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 113
% Number: Question 113
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2013, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The open-loop transfer function of a dc motor is given as [latex]\frac{w\left ( s \right )}{V_{a}\left ( s \right )}= \frac{10}{1+10s}[/latex]. When connected in feedback as shown below, the approximate value of [latex]K_{a}[/latex] that will reduce the time constant of the closed loop system by one hundred times as compared to that of the open-loop system is<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20131/q29.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20131/q29.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]5[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]10[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]100[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \frac{\omega(s)}{V_{a}(s)} &amp;=\frac{10}{1+10 s} \\ 1+T s &amp;=1+10 s \\ T s &amp;=10 s \\ \therefore \quad T &amp;=10\\ T^{\prime}=&amp; \frac{T}{100}=\frac{10}{100}=\frac{1}{10} \\ \frac{\omega(s)}{R(s)}=&amp; \frac{K_{a} \times \frac{10}{1+10 s}}{1+K_{a} \frac{10}{1+10 s}} \\ \frac{\omega(s)}{R(s)}&amp;=\frac{10 K_{a}}{1+10 s+10 K_{a}}=\frac{10 K_{a}}{\left(1+10 K_{a}\right)+10s} \\ \frac{\omega(s)}{R(s)}&amp;=\frac{10k_{a}}{\left(1+10 K_{a}\right)\left[1+\frac{10 s}{1+10 K_{a}}\right]} \\ T^{\prime}&amp;=\frac{10}{1+10K_{a}}=\frac{1}{10}\\ 1+10 K_{a}&amp;=100 \\ 10 K_{a}&amp;=99 \\ K_{a}&amp;=9.9 \\ K_{a} &amp;\cong 10 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

The phase is negative for all [latex]\omega[/latex]. Then G(s) is</div>

% QUESTION 115
% Number: Question 115
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2012, Compensators and Controllers
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The transfer function of a compensator is given as <br/>[latex]G_{c}(s)=\frac{s+a}{s+b}[/latex]<br/>
<br/>The phase of the above lead compensator is maximum at</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\sqrt{2}[/latex] rad/s</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\sqrt{3}[/latex]rad/s</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\sqrt{6}[/latex] rad/s</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1/[latex]\sqrt{3}[/latex] rad/s</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
For phase to be maximum,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \frac{\partial P}{\partial \omega}&amp;=0 \\ \frac{1}{1+\frac{\omega^{2}}{a^{2}}}- \frac{1/b}{1+\frac{\omega^{2}}{b^{2}}}&amp;=0 \\ \frac{1}{1+\omega^{2}}- \frac{1/b}{1+\frac{\omega^{2}}{4}}&amp;=0\\ \frac{1}{1+\omega^{2}}-\frac{2}{\omega^{2}+4} &amp;=0 \\ \omega^{2}+4-2-2 \omega^{2} &amp;=0 \\ \omega^{2} &amp;=2 \\ \omega &amp;=\sqrt{2} \mathrm{rad} / \mathrm{sec} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% Number: Question 116
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2012, Compensators and Controllers
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The transfer function of a compensator is given as <br/>[latex]G_{c}(s)=\frac{s+a}{s+b}[/latex]<br/>
<br/>[latex]G_{c}(S)[/latex] is a lead compensator if</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">a =1, b = 2</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">a = 3, b = 2</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">a = -3, b = -1</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">a = 3, b = 1</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]G_{c}(s)=\left(\frac{s+a}{s+b}\right)[/latex]<br/> Phase [latex]P=\tan ^{-1}(\omega / a)-\tan ^{-1} \omega / b [/latex]<br/> for lead comparators phase must be + ve.<br/> for this [latex]\frac{\omega}{a} \gt \frac{\omega}{b} \Rightarrow a \lt b [/latex]<br/> So [latex]a=1, b=2[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 117
% Number: Question 117
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2012, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The state variable description of an LTI system is given by [latex]\begin{pmatrix} \dot{x}_{1}\\ \dot{x}_{2}\\ \dot{x}_{3} \end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix} 0 &amp; a_{1}&amp; 0\\ 0&amp; 0 &amp; a_{2}\\ a_{3} &amp; 0 &amp; 0 \end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix} x_{1}\\ x_{2}\\ x_{3} \end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix} 0\\ 0\\ 1 \end{pmatrix}u [/latex] <br/>[latex]  y=\begin{pmatrix} 1 &amp;0 &amp;0 \end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix} x_{1}\\ x_{2}\\ x_{3} \end{pmatrix}[/latex] <br/> where y is the output and u is the input. The system is controllable for</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]a_{1}\neq 0, a_{2}=0,a_{3} \neq 0[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]a_{1}= 0, a_{2}\neq 0,a_{3} \neq 0[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]a_{1}= 0, a_{2}\neq 0,a_{3} = 0[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]a_{1}\neq 0, a_{2}\neq 0,a_{3} = 0[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \left[\begin{array}{l}\dot{x}_{1} \\\dot{x}_{2} \\\dot{x}_{3}\end{array}\right]&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{lll} 0 &amp; a_{1} &amp; 0 \\0 &amp; 0 &amp; a_{2} \\a_{3} &amp; 0 &amp; 0\end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l} x_{1} \\x_{2} \\x_{3}\end{array}\right]+\left[\begin{array}{l}0 \\0 \\1 \end{array}\right] u\\ y&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{lll}1 &amp; 0 &amp; 0\end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l} x_{1} \\x_{2} \\x_{3}\end{array}\right]\\ Q_{c}&amp;=\left[B A B A^{2} B\right]\\ A B&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{lll} 0 &amp; a_{1} &amp; 0 \\ 0 &amp; 0 &amp; a_{2} \\ a_{3} &amp; 0 &amp; 0 \end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l} 0 \\ 0 \\ 1 \end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}{l} 0 \\ a_{2} \\ 0 \end{array}\right]\\ A^{2} B&amp;=A A B=\left[\begin{array}{lll} 0 &amp; a_{1} &amp; 0 \\ 0 &amp; 0 &amp; a_{2} \\ a_{3} &amp; 0 &amp; 0 \end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l} 0 \\ a_{2} \\ 0 \end{array}\right]\\ A^{2} B&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{c} a_{1} a_{2} \\ 0 \\ 0 \end{array}\right]\\ Q_{c}&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ccc}0 &amp; 0 &amp; a_{1} a_{2} \\ 0 &amp; a_{2} &amp; 0 \\ 1 &amp; 0 &amp; 0\end{array}\right] \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>For system to be controllable,<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} |Q_{i}| \neq 0 \\ \Rightarrow\left(0-a, a_{i}\right) \neq 0 \\ \Rightarrow a_{1} \neq 0 \\ a_{2} \neq 0 \end{array}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 118
% Number: Question 118
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2012, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The feedback system shown below oscillates at 2 rad/s when <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20121/q30.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20121/q30.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">K=2 and a=0.75</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">K=3 and a=0.75</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">K=4 and a=0.5</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">K=2 and a=0.5</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Characteristic equation is<br/>[latex] 1+G(s)H(s)=0[/latex]<br/>[latex] 1+\frac{K(s+1)}{s^{3}+a s^{2}+2 s+1}=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex] s^{3}+a s^{2}+(2+K) s+(1+K)=0 [/latex]<br/> array for this is Routh<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l|lr} s^{3} &amp; 1 &amp; (2+K) \\ s^{2} &amp; a &amp; (1+K) \\ s^{1} &amp; \frac{a(2+K)-(1+K)}{a} \\ s^{0} &amp; (1+K) &amp; \end{array}[/latex]<br/> For oscillation [latex]\frac{a(2+K)-(1+K)}{a}=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow \quad a=\left(\frac{1+K}{2+K}\right) [/latex]<br/> Now <br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} a s^{2}+(1+K)&amp;=0 \\ -a \omega^{2}+(1+K)&amp;=0 \\ \text{given }\quad \omega&amp;=2 \mathrm{rad} / \mathrm{sec} \\ -4 a+(1+K)&amp;=0 \\ -4 \frac{(1+K)}{(2+K)}+(1+K)&amp;=0 \\ -4(1+K)+(2+K)(1+K)&amp;=0 \\ (1+K)[(2+K)-4]&amp;=0 \\ K=-1,2 \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> but K=-1 is not possible as system will not  oscillate for this as a=0 <br/> So, K=2 <br/>[latex] a=\frac{1+K}{2+K}=\frac{3}{4}=0.75[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 119
% Number: Question 119
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2012, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A system with transfer function<br/> [latex]G(s)=\frac{(s^{2}+9)(s+2)}{(s+1)(s+3)(s+4)}[/latex] <br/>is excited by sin([latex]\omega[/latex] t). The steady-state output of the system is zero at</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\omega[/latex]=1 rad/s</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\omega[/latex]=2 rad/s</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\omega[/latex]=3 rad/s</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\omega[/latex]=4 rad/s</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Transfer function is<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} G(s)&amp;=\frac{\left(s^{2}+9\right)(s+2)}{(s+1)(s+3)(s+4)} \\ \text{Input }\quad X(s)&amp;=L \cdot T[\sin \omega t]\\ X(s)&amp;=\frac{\omega}{s^{2}+\omega^{2}} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> So output<br/>[latex] Y(s)=\frac{\left(s^{2}+9\right)(s+2)}{(s+1)(s+3)(s+4)} \cdot \frac{\omega}{\left(s^{2}+\omega^{2}\right)}[/latex]<br/> Now<br/>[latex] \lim _{t \rightarrow \infty} y(t)=\lim _{s \rightarrow 0} S Y(s)=\lim _{s \rightarrow 0} s \frac{\left(s^{2}+9\right)(s+2)}{(s+1)(s+3)(s+4)} \cdot \frac{\omega}{\left(s^{2}+\omega^{2}\right)}[/latex]<br/> this will be zero if [latex] s^{2}+\omega^{2}[/latex] will cancel [latex]\left(s^{2}+9\right)[/latex] term as only then final value theorem will be applicable. <br/> So at [latex]\omega^{2}=9 \Rightarrow \omega=3 \mathrm{rad} / \mathrm{sec},[/latex] steady state output will be zero.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 121
% Number: Question 121
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2011, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The input-output transfer function of a plant [latex]H(s)=\frac{100}{s(s+10)^2}[/latex].
The plant is
placed in a unity negative feedback configuration as shown in the figure below.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20111/q50.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20111/q50.jpg"/></noscript><br/>The gain margin of the system under closed loop unity negative feedback is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0 dB</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">20 dB</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">26 dB</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">46 dB</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} G(s) H(s) &amp;=\frac{100}{s(s+10)^{2}} \\ \phi &amp;=-90^{\circ}-2 \tan ^{-1}(\omega / 10) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> For phase cross-over frequency, [latex]\phi=-180^{\circ} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \therefore \quad-180 &amp;=-90^{\circ}-2 \tan ^{-1}(\omega / 10) \\ \Rightarrow &amp; \omega=10 \mathrm{rad} / \mathrm{sec} \\ s &amp;=j \omega \\ \text { Put, } \quad G(j \omega) H(j \omega) &amp;=\frac{100}{j \omega(j \omega+100)^{2}} \\ |G(j \omega) H(j \omega)|_{\omega=10} &amp;=\frac{100}{\omega\left(\omega^{2}+100\right)} \\ &amp;=\frac{100}{10(200)}=\frac{1}{20} \\ \text { G.M. } &amp;=\frac{1}{\mid G(j \omega) H(j \omega)}=20 \end{aligned} [/latex]<br/> G.M. in [latex]\mathrm{dB}=20 \log 20=26 \mathrm{dB}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 122
% Number: Question 122
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2011, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The block diagram of a system with one input it and two outputs [latex]y_1 \; and \; y_2[/latex] is
given below. <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20111/q39.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20111/q39.jpg"/></noscript> <br/>A state space model of the above system in terms of the state vector [latex]\underline{x}[/latex] and the output vector [latex]\underline{y}= [y_{1}    \; y_{2}]^{T}[/latex] is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\dot{\underline{x}}=[2]\underline{x} + [1]u; \underline{y}=[1 \; 2]\underline{x}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\dot{\underline{x}}=[-2]\underline{x} + [1]u; \underline{y}=\begin{bmatrix} 1\\ 2 \end{bmatrix}\underline{x}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\dot{\underline{x}}=\begin{bmatrix} -2 &amp; 0\\ 0&amp;-2 \end{bmatrix}\underline{x} +\begin{bmatrix} 1\\ 1 \end{bmatrix}u; \underline{y}=[1 \; 2]\underline{x}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\dot{\underline{x}}=\begin{bmatrix} 2 &amp; 0\\ 0&amp;2 \end{bmatrix}\underline{x} + \begin{bmatrix} 1\\ 1 \end{bmatrix}u; \underline{y}=\begin{bmatrix} 1\\ 2 \end{bmatrix}\underline{x} [/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \frac{Y_{1}(s)}{U(s)} &amp;=\frac{1}{s+2} \\ \frac{Y_{2}(s)}{U(s)} &amp;=\frac{2}{s+2} \\ \frac{Y_{1}(s)}{x_{1}(s)} \frac{x_{1}(s)}{U(s)} &amp;=\frac{1}{s+2} \\ \frac{r_{2}(s)}{x_{2}(s)} \frac{x_{2}(s)}{U(s)} &amp;=\frac{2}{s+2} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \frac{X_{1}(s)}{U(s)} &amp;=\frac{1}{s+2} \text { and } \frac{Y_{1}(s)}{X_{1}(s)}=1 \\ \frac{x_{2}(s)}{U(s)} &amp;=\frac{1}{s+2} \text { and } \frac{Y_{2}(s)}{U(s)}=2 \\ s X_{1}(s)+2 X_{1}(s) &amp;=U(s) \\ \text{and} \quad Y_{1}(s) &amp;=x_{1}(s) \\ s X_{2}(s)+2 X_{2}(s) &amp;=U(s) \\ \text{and }\quad Y_{2}(s)&amp;=2 X_{2}(s) \\ \dot{x}_{1}(t)+2 x_{1}(t)&amp;=U(t) \\ \text{and }y_{1}(t)&amp;=x_{1}(t) \\ \dot{x}_{2}(t)+2 x_{2}(t)&amp;=U(t) \\ y_{2}(t)&amp;=2 x_{2}(t) \end{aligned} [/latex]<br/> From Question,<br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} y &amp;=\left[y_{1} y_{2}\right]^{\top}=[12]^{\top}=\left[\begin{array}{l}1 \\2\end{array}\right] \\ \dot{x}_{1}(t) &amp;=-2 x_{1}(t)+u(t) \\ \dot{x}_{2}(t) &amp;=-2 x_{2}(t)+u(t) \\ \left[\begin{array}{c}\dot{x}_{1} \\\dot{x}_{2}\end{array}\right]&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{cc}-2 &amp; 0 \\-2 &amp; 0\end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l}x_{1} \\x_{2} \end{array}\right]+\left[\begin{array}{l}1 \\1\end{array}\right] u(t) \\ \dot{x} &amp;=[-2] x+[1] u \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> only option (B) is satisfied.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 123

% Tags: GATE EC 2011, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">For the transfer function [latex]G(j\omega)=5+j\omega[/latex], the corresponding Nyquist plot for
positive frequency has the form <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20111/q17.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20111/q17.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">A</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">B</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">C</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">D</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} G(j \omega) &amp;=5+j \omega \\|G(j \omega)| &amp;=\sqrt{25+\omega^{2}} \\ \text { At } \quad\omega&amp;=0\\ At |G(0)|&amp;=\sqrt{25+0}=5 \\ \text { At } \quad\omega&amp;=\infty \\ |G(\infty)| &amp;=\infty \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 124
% Number: Question 124
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2011, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The differential equation [latex]100\frac{d^{2}y}{dt^{2}}-20\frac{dy}{dt}+y=x(t)[/latex] describes a system with an in put x(t) and an output y(t). The system, which is initially relaxed, is excited by a unit step input. The output y(t) can be represented by the waveform <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20111/q16.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20111/q16.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">A</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">B</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">C</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">D</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]100 \frac{d^{2} y}{d t^{2}}-20 \frac{d y}{d t}+y=x(t)[/latex]<br/> Taking Laplace transform of both sides<br/>[latex] 100 s^{2} Y(s)-20 s Y(s)+Y(s)=\frac{X(s)}{s}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \Rightarrow \frac{Y(s)}{X(s)}=\frac{1 / s}{\left(100 s^{2}-20 s+1\right)}=\frac{1}{s(10 s-1)^{2}} [/latex]<br/> Poles are at [latex]s=\frac{1}{10}, \frac{1}{10}, 0 [/latex]<br/> As poles are on the right-hand side of s-plane so given system is unstable system.<br/> Only option (A) represents unstable system.</div>
