% QUESTION 126
% Number: Question 126
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2010, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The signal flow graph of a system is shown below: <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20101/q50.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20101/q50.jpg"/></noscript><br/>The transfer function of the system is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{s+1}{s^{2}+1}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{s-1}{s^{2}+1}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{s+1}{s^{2}+s+1}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{s-1}{s^{2}+s+1}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Forward path gain,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} P_{1} &amp;=2\left(\frac{1}{s}\right)\left(\frac{1}{s}\right)(0.5)=\frac{1}{s^{2}} \\ P_{2} &amp;=2\left(\frac{1}{s}\right)(1)(0.5)=\frac{1}{s} \\ \Delta_{1} &amp;=1 \\ \Delta_{2} &amp;=1 \\ \Delta &amp;=1-\left\{-\frac{1}{s}-\frac{1}{s^{2}}\right\} \\ &amp;=1+\frac{1}{s}+\frac{1}{s^{2}} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Transfer funct ion of the system,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \frac{Y(s)}{U(s)} &amp;=\frac{P_{1} \Delta_{1}+P_{2} \Delta_{2}}{\Delta} \\ &amp;=-\frac{1}{1+\frac{s^{2}}{s}+\frac{1}{s}}=\frac{s+1}{s^{2}+s+1} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 128
% Number: Question 128
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2010, Compensators and Controllers
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A unity negative feedback closed loop system has a plant with the transfer function [latex]G(s)=\frac{1}{s^{2}+2s+2}[/latex] and a controller [latex]G_{c}(s)[/latex] in the
feed forward path. For a unit set input, the transfer function of the controller that
gives minimum steady state error is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]G_{c}(s)=\frac{s+1}{s+2}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]G_{c}(s)=\frac{(s+1)(s+4)}{(s+2)(s+3)}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]G_{c}(s)=\frac{s+2}{s+1}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]G_{c}(s)=1+\frac{2}{s}+3s[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Steady state error,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} e_{s s} &amp;=\lim _{s \rightarrow 0} \frac{s R(s)}{1+G(s) G_{c}(s)} \\ r(t) &amp;=u(t) \\ R(s) &amp;=\frac{1}{s} \\ e_{ss} &amp;=\lim _{s \rightarrow 0} \frac{s \cdot \frac{1}{s}}{1+G(s) G_{c}(s)} \\ e_{ss}&amp;=\lim _{s \rightarrow 0} \frac{1}{1+G(s) G_{c}(s)} \\ \text { Taking, } \quad G_{c}(s) &amp;=\frac{s+1}{s+2}, e_{s s}=\frac{2}{3} \\ \text { Taking, } \quad G_{c}(s) &amp;=\frac{s+2}{s+1}, e_{s s}=\frac{1}{3} \\ \text { Taking, } \quad G_{c}(s) &amp;=\frac{(s+1)(s+4)}{(s+2)(s+3)}, e_{s s}=\frac{3}{5} \\ \text { Taking, } \quad &amp; G_{c}(s)=1+\frac{2}{s}+3 s, e_{s s}=0 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 129

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">For the asymptotic Bode magnitude plot shown below, the system transfer function can be <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20101/q19.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20101/q19.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{10s+1}{0.1s+1}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 130
% Number: Question 130
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2010, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A system with transfer function [latex]\frac{Y(s)}{X(s)}=\frac{s}{s+p}[/latex] has an output [latex]y(t)=cos(2t-\frac{\pi }{3})[/latex] for the input signal [latex]x(t)=pcos(2t-\frac{\pi }{2})[/latex]. Then, the system parameter p is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\sqrt{3}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \phi&amp;=-\frac{\pi}{3}-\left(-\frac{\pi}{2}\right)=\frac{\pi}{6}=30^{\circ}\\ \text{and }\quad\omega&amp;=2 \mathrm{rad} / \mathrm{s} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> From the transfer function,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \phi &amp;=90^{\circ}-\tan ^{-1} \frac{\omega}{p} \\ 90^{\circ}-\tan ^{-1} \frac{2}{p} &amp;=30^{\circ} \\ \tan ^{-1} \frac{2}{p} &amp;=60^{\circ} \\ \frac{2}{p} &amp;=\tan 60^{\circ}=\sqrt{3} \\ p &amp;=\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The transfer function Y(s)/R(s) of the system shown is <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20101/q17.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20101/q17.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{s+1}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{2}{s+1}[/latex]</div>

shown.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20091/q53.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20091/q53.jpg"/></noscript><br/>The gain and phase margins of G(s) for closed loop stability are</div>

% QUESTION 133
% Number: Question 133
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2009, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The Nyquist plot of a stable transfer function G(s) is shown in the figure are
interested in the stability of the closed loop system in the feedback configuration
shown.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20091/q53.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20091/q53.jpg"/></noscript><br/>
Which of the following statements is true ?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">G(s) is an all-pass filter</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">G(s) has a zero in the right-half plane</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">G(s) is the impedance of a passive network</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">G(s) is marginally stable</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Nyquist plot will encirtce the origin as many
number of times as the difference between the
number of right side poles and zeros of open loop
transfer function.<br/>
i.e. [latex]\quad N=P-Z [/latex]<br/>
From the plot,<br/>[latex]
\begin{aligned}
N&amp;=-1\\
\text{and  }P&amp;=0 \quad  (Given)  \\
\therefore \quad Z&amp;=1
\end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>
Thus, one open loop zero in right half of s-Plane</div>

% QUESTION 134
% Number: Question 134
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2009, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The unit step response of an under-damped second order system has steady state value of -2. Which one of the following transfer functions has theses properties ?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{-2.24}{s^{2}+2.59s+1.12}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{-3.82}{s^{2}+1.91s+1.91}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{-2.24}{s^{2}-2.59s+1.12}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{-3.82}{s^{2}-1.91s+1.91}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Steady state Value =-2 <br/> denominator:<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} 2 \xi \omega_{n} &amp;=1.91, \omega_{n}^{2}=1.91 \\ \Rightarrow \quad \omega_{n} &amp; \cong 1.4 \\ \xi &amp;=\frac{1.91}{2 \omega_{n}}=\frac{1.91}{2.8} \lt 1\\ &amp;...\text{underdamped} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 136
% Number: Question 136
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2009, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider the system [latex]\frac{dx}{dt}=Ax+Bu [/latex] with [latex]  A=\begin{bmatrix} 1 &amp; 0\\ 0&amp;1 \end{bmatrix} [/latex] and [latex] B=\begin{bmatrix} p\\ q \end{bmatrix}[/latex] where p and q are arbitrary real numbers. Which of the following statements about the controllability of the system is true ?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">The system is completely state controllable for any nonzero values of p and q</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Only p = 0 and q = 0 result in controllability</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">The system is uncontrollable for all values of p and q</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">We cannot conclude about controllability from the given data</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} A&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ll}1 &amp; 0 \\0 &amp; 1\end{array}\right] \\ B&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{l}p \\q\end{array}\right] \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> For controllabil itycondition is<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} Q_{C}&amp;=\left[B, A B, \ldots A^{n-1} B\right] \neq 0\\ A \cdot B&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ll}1 &amp; 0 \\0 &amp; 1\end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l}p \\q\end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}{l}p+0 \\0+q\end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}{l}p \\q\end{array}\right]\\ \text{So}\quad Q_{c}&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ll}p &amp; p \\ q &amp; q\end{array}\right]=0 \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> So, the system is uncontrollable for all values of p and q</div>

% QUESTION 138
% Number: Question 138
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2008, Compensators and Controllers
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Group I gives two possible choices for the impedance Z in the diagram. The
circuit elements in Z satisfy the conditions [latex]R_{2}C_{2} \gt R_{1}C_{1}[/latex]. The transfer functions [latex]\frac{V_{o}}{V_{i}}[/latex] represents a kind of controller. Match the impedances in Group I with the type of controllers in Group II <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20081/q43a.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20081/q43a.jpg"/></noscript><br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20081/q43b.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20081/q43b.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Q - 1,R - 2</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">Q - 1,R - 3</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Q - 2,R - 3</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Q - 3,R - 2</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{array}{c} \frac{V_{i}\left(R_{1} C_{1} S+1\right) 1}{R_{1}}=-\frac{V_{0}}{Z} \\ \Rightarrow \frac{V_{0}}{V_{i}}=-\frac{Z\left(R_{1} C_{1} S+1\right)}{R_{1}} \end{array}[/latex]<br/> In case of Q, [latex]\quad Z=\frac{R_{2} C_{2} s+1}{C_{2} S+1} [/latex]<br/> In case of R, [latex]\quad Z=\frac{R_{2}}{R_{2} C_{2} s+1} [/latex]<br/> Considering Q <br/>[latex] \frac{V_{0}}{V_{i}}=-\frac{\left(R_{1} C_{1} S+1\right)}{R_{1}} \cdot \frac{\left(R_{2} C_{2} S+1\right)}{C_{2} S}[/latex]<br/> Considering R ,<br/>[latex] \frac{V_{0}}{V_{i}}=-\frac{\left(R_{1} C_{1} S+1\right)}{R_{1}} \cdot \frac{R_{2}}{\left(R_{2} C_{2} s+1\right)}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \because [/latex] Given that [latex]R_{2} C_{2}&gt;R_{1} C_{1} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore \quad [/latex] Considering R, controller is lag compensator. <br/> and Considering Q , Controller is PID controller.</div>

% QUESTION 142
% Number: Question 142
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2008, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A certain system has transfer function [latex]G(s)=\frac{s+8}{s^{2}+\alpha s-4}[/latex], where [latex]\alpha[/latex] is a parameter. Consider the standard negative unity feedback configuration as shown below <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20081/q39.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20081/q39.jpg"/></noscript> <br/> Which of the following statements is true?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">The closed loop systems is never stable for any value of [latex]\alpha[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">For some positive value of [latex]\alpha[/latex], the closed loop system is stable, but not for all positive values.</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">For all positive values of [latex]\alpha[/latex], the closed loop system is stable.</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">The closed loop system stable for all values of [latex]\alpha[/latex], both positive and negative.</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Closed loop gain is<br/>[latex]
\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)}=\frac{s+8}{s^{2}+\alpha s-4+s+8} [/latex]<br/>
Characteristic equation <br/>[latex]
q(s)=s^{2}+(\alpha+1) s+4 [/latex]<br/>
Closed loop system is stable only for [latex]
\alpha \gt -1.[/latex]. Therefore, for all positive of [latex]\alpha[/latex], the closed
loop system is stable.</div>

functions.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20081/q38.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20081/q38.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">P - 3,Q - 1,R - 4,S - 2</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">P - 3,Q - 2,R - 4,S - 1</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">P - 2,Q - 1,R - 4,S - 2</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">P - 3,Q - 4,R - 1,S - 2</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Comparing the given transfer functions with<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{c} \frac{\omega_{n}^{2}}{s^{2}+2 \xi \omega_{n} s+\omega_{n}^{2}} \\ P=\frac{25}{s^{2}+25} \Rightarrow \xi=0 \end{array}[/latex]<br/> So, P is undamped.<br/>[latex] Q=\frac{36}{s^{2}+20 s+36} \Rightarrow \xi=\frac{20}{2 \times 6}=1.67[/latex]<br/> So, Q is overdamped.<br/>[latex] R=\frac{36}{s^{2}+12 s+36} \Rightarrow \xi=\frac{12}{2 \times 6}=1[/latex]<br/> So, R is critically damped.<br/>[latex] S=\frac{49}{s^{2}+7 s+49} \Rightarrow \xi=\frac{7}{2 \times 7}=0.5[/latex]<br/> So, S is underdamped.</div>

% QUESTION 144
% Number: Question 144
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2008, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A linear, time - invariant, causal continuous time system has a rational transfer
function with simple poles at s =- 2 and s =- 4 and one simple zero at s =- 1
. A unit step u(t) is applied at the input of the system. At steady state, the
output has constant value of 1. The impulse response of this system is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[exp(- 2t) + exp(- 4t)]u(t)</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[- 4 exp(- 2t) - 12 exp(- 4t) - exp(- t)]u(t)</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[- 4 exp(- 2t) + 12 exp(- 4t)]u(t)</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[- 0.5 exp(- 2t) + 1.5 exp(- 4t)]u(t)</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Transfer functions can be written as,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} H(s)&amp;=\frac{K(s+1)}{(s+2)(s+4)} \\ \text{Input,}\quad R(s)&amp;=\frac{1}{s}\\ C(s)&amp;=R(s) H(s) \\ \text{Given:} \quad \lim _{s \rightarrow 0} s C(s)&amp;=1 \\ \text{or,} \quad\lim _{s \rightarrow 0} \frac{s \cdot K(s+1)}{s(s+2)(s+4)}&amp;=1 \\ \text{Or }\quad \frac{K}{8}&amp;=1 \\ \Rightarrow \quad K &amp;=8 \\ H(s) &amp;=\frac{8(s+1)}{(s+2)(s+4)} \\ &amp;=-\frac{4}{s+2}+\frac{12}{s+4} \\ h(t) &amp;=\left(-4 e^{-2 t}+12 e^{-4 t}\right) u(t) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>Which is also the required impulse response of the system.</div>

% Tags: GATE EC 2007, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider a linear system whose state space representation is [latex]x(t)=Ax(t)[/latex]. If the initial state vector of the system is [latex]\dot{x}(0)=\begin{bmatrix} 1\\ -2 \end{bmatrix}[/latex], then the system response is  [latex]x(t)=\begin{bmatrix} e^{-2t}\\ -2e^{-2t} \end{bmatrix}[/latex] <br/>If the initial state vector of the system changes to [latex]x(0)=\begin{bmatrix} 1\\ -1 \end{bmatrix}[/latex], then the system response becomes  [latex]x(t)=\begin{bmatrix} e^{-t}\\ -e^{-t} \end{bmatrix}[/latex]. <br/><br/>
The eigenvalue and eigenvector pairs ([latex]\lambda _{i},v_{i}[/latex]) for the system are</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex](-1, \begin{bmatrix} 1\\ -1 \end{bmatrix})\; and \;(-2,\begin{bmatrix} 1\\ -2 \end{bmatrix})[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex](-2, \begin{bmatrix} 1\\ -1 \end{bmatrix})\; and \;(-1,\begin{bmatrix} 1\\ -2 \end{bmatrix})[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex](-1, \begin{bmatrix} 1\\ -1 \end{bmatrix})\; and \; (2, \begin{bmatrix} 1\\ -2 \end{bmatrix})[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex](-2, \begin{bmatrix} 1\\ -1 \end{bmatrix})\; and \;(1,\begin{bmatrix} 1\\ -2 \end{bmatrix})[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Sum of the eigen value = Trace of the principle
diagonal matrix<br/>
Sum= -3. Only option (A) satisfies both conditions</div>

% QUESTION 149
% Number: Question 149
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2007, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The state space representation of a separately excited DC servo motor dynamics
is given as <br/>[latex]\begin{bmatrix} \frac{d\omega }{dt}\\ \frac{di_{a}}{dt} \end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix} -1 &amp; 1\\ -1&amp;-10 \end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix} \omega \\ i_{a} \end{bmatrix}+\begin{bmatrix} 0\\ 10 \end{bmatrix}u[/latex]<br/>  where [latex]\omega[/latex] is the speed of the motor, [latex]i_{a}[/latex] is the armature current and u is the armature voltage. The transfer function [latex]\frac{\omega (s)}{U(s)}[/latex] of the motor is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{10}{s^{2}+11s+11}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{s^{2}+11s+11}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{10s+10}{s^{2}+11s+11}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{s^{2}+s+11}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \left[\begin{array}{c}\frac{d \omega}{d t} \\\frac{d i_{a}}{d t}\end{array}\right]&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{cc} -1 &amp; 1 \\ -1 &amp; -10 \end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l} \omega \\ i_{a} \end{array}\right]+\left[\begin{array}{c} 0 \\ 10 \end{array}\right] u\\ \Rightarrow \frac{d \omega}{d t}&amp;=-\omega+i_{\mathrm{a}} &amp;\ldots(i)\\ \Rightarrow \frac{d i_{\mathrm{a}}}{d t}&amp;=-\omega-10 i_{\mathrm{a}}+10 u &amp;\ldots(ii) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Taking Laplace transform (i) \&amp; (ii) <br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} \Rightarrow s \omega(s)=-\omega(s)+I_{a}(s) \\ \Rightarrow(s+1) \omega(s)=I_{a}(s) \quad\ldots(iii)\\ \Rightarrow s I_{e}(s)=-\omega(s)-10 I_{a}(s)+10 U(s) \\ \Rightarrow \omega(s)=(-10-s) I_{a}(s)+10 U(s) \\ =(-10-s)(s+1) \omega(s)+10 U(s) \\ =-\left[s^{2}+11 s+10\right] \omega(s)+10 U(s) \\ \Rightarrow\left[s^{2}+11 s+11\right] \omega(s)=10 U(s) \\ \Rightarrow \frac{\omega(s)}{U(s)}=\frac{10}{\left(s^{2}+11 s+11\right)} \end{array}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 150
% Number: Question 150
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2007, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The asymptotic Bode plot of a transfer function is as shown in the figure. The
transfer function G(s) corresponding to this Bode plot is <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20071/q56.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20071/q56.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{(s+1)(s+20)}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{s(s+1)(s+20)}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{100}{s(s+1)(s+20)}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{100}{s(s+1)(1+0.05s)}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]G(s)=\frac{K}{s(s+1)(s+20)}=\frac{K / 20}{s(1+s)\left(1+\frac{s}{20}\right)} [/latex]<br/> Bode plot is in (1+sT) form <br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} -20 \log \omega+20 \log K&amp;=|60 d B|_{\omega=0.1} \\ -20 \log 0.1+20 \log K&amp;=60 \mathrm{dB} \\ 20 \log K&amp;=40 \mathrm{dB} \\ \Rightarrow \quad K&amp;=100 \\ \therefore \quad G(s)&amp;=\frac{100}{s(s+1)(1+.05 s)} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 151
% Number: Question 151
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2007, Root Locus
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A unity feedback control system has an open-loop transfer function [latex]G(s)=\frac{K}{s(s^{2}+7s+12)}[/latex]
The gain K for which [latex]s=-1+j1[/latex]  will lie on the root locus of this system is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">4</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">5.5</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">6.5</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">10</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} T \cdot F \cdot &amp;=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s)} \\ A s H(s) &amp;=1 \end{aligned} [/latex]<br/> For the point s=-1+j 1 to lie on root locus <br/>[latex] \begin{array} {l} 1+G(s)=0 \\ \Rightarrow 1+\frac{K}{s\left(s^{2}+7 s+12\right)}=0 \\ \text{Putting }\left.s^{2}+7 s+12\right)+K=0 \\ \Rightarrow \quad(-1+j)(1-2 j-1-7+7 j+12)+K=0 \\ \Rightarrow \quad K=+10 \end{array}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 152
% Number: Question 152
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2007, Compensators and Controllers
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The open-loop transfer function of a plant is given as [latex]G(s)=\frac{1}{s^{2}-1}[/latex] . If the plant is operated in a unity feedback configuration, then the lead compensator that an stabilize this control system is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{10(s-1)}{s+2}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{10(s+4)}{s+2}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{10(s+2)}{s+10}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{2(s+2)}{s+10}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Lead compensator is required for stability.<br/>
Controllers given in option (A) and (B) are not lead
compensator. From option (C) and (d), with
option (D) system becomes unstable, hence
option (C) is correct.</div>

% QUESTION 153
% Number: Question 153
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2007, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The transfer function of a plant is [latex]T(s)=\frac{5}{(s+5)(s^{2}+s+1)}[/latex] The second-order approximation of T(s)  using dominant pole concept is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{(s+5)(s+1)}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{5}{(s+5)(s+1)}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{5}{s^{2}+s+1}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{s^{2}+s+1}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
In dominant pole concept, the factor that has to <br/> be eliminated should be in time constant form.<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \frac{5}{(s+5)\left(s^{2}+s+1\right)}=&amp; \frac{5}{5\left(1+\frac{s}{5}\right)\left(s^{2}+s+1\right)} \\ =&amp; \frac{1}{s^{2}+s+1} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 154
% Number: Question 154
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2007, Compensators and Controllers
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A control system with PD controller is shown in the figure. If the velocity error
constant [latex]K_{V}[/latex]=1000 and the damping ratio [latex]\varsigma[/latex]=0.5, then the value of [latex]K_{P}[/latex] and [latex]K_{D}[/latex] are <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20071/q52.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20071/q52.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]K_{P}=100,K_{D}=0.09[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]K_{P}=100,K_{D}=0.9[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]K_{P}=10,K_{D}=0.09[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]K_{P}=10,K_{D}=0.9[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} K_{v} &amp;=\lim _{s \rightarrow 0} s G(s) H(s) \\ 1000 &amp;=\lim _{s \rightarrow 0} s \times \frac{\left(K_{P}+K_{D} s\right) 100}{s(s+10)} \\ \Rightarrow \quad K_{P} &amp;=100 \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Now characteristics eq. [latex](1+G(s) H(s))=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} 1+\frac{\left(K_{P}+K_{D}\right) 100}{s(s+10)}&amp;=0 \\ \text { Putting } \quad K_{P}&amp;=100 \\ s^{2}+10 s+10^{4}+100 K_{0} s&amp;=0 \\ s^{2}+\left(10+100 K_{0}\right) s+10^{4}&amp;=0 \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Comparing with standard second order eq.<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} \text{i.e.}\quad s^{2}+2 \xi \omega_{n} s+\omega_{n}^{2}=0\\ \text{So,} \quad \omega_{n}=100 ; 2 \xi \omega_{n}=10+100 K_{0} \\ \text{Given,}\quad \xi=0.5 \\ 2 \times 0.5 \times 100=10+100 K_{0} \\ K_{D}=0.9 \end{array}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 155
% Number: Question 155
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2007, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The frequency response of a linear, time-invariant system is given by [latex]H(f)=\frac{5}{1+j10 \pi f}[/latex]. The step response of the system is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]5(1-e^{-5t})u(t)[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]5(1-e^{-\frac{t}{5}})u(t)[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1/5(1-e^{-5t})u(t)[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1/5(1-e^{-t/5})u(t)[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} H(f) &amp;=\frac{5}{1+j 10 \pi f} \\ H(s) &amp;=\frac{5}{1+5 s} \\ &amp;=\frac{5}{5\left(s+\frac{1}{5}\right)}=\frac{1}{s+\frac{1}{5}} \\ \text { Step response } &amp;=\frac{1}{S} \frac{1}{\left(s+\frac{1}{5}\right)} \\ \frac{1}{s} \times \frac{1}{\left(s+\frac{1}{5}\right)} &amp;=\frac{A}{s}+\frac{B}{s+\frac{1}{5}} \\ \Rightarrow A\left(s+\frac{1}{5}\right)&amp;+B s=1 \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> when [latex]s=0, A=5 [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \text{when }\quad s&amp;=\frac{-1}{5}, B=-5 \\ Y(s) &amp;=\frac{5}{s}-\frac{5}{s+\frac{1}{5}} \\ \Rightarrow y(t)&amp;=5\left[1-e^{-t / 5}\right] u(t) \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 157
% Number: Question 157
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2006, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider a unity-gain feedback control system whose open-loop transfer function is: <br/>[latex]G(s)=\frac{as+1}{s^{2}}[/latex] <br/>
With the value of "a" set for a phase - margin of [latex]\frac{\pi }{4}[/latex], the value of unit-impulse response of the open-loop system at t=1 second is equal to</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">3.4</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">2.4</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">1.84</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1.74</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} G(s)&amp;=\frac{0.84 s+1}{s^{2}}\\ H(s) &amp;=1, R(s)=1 \\ c(s) &amp;=G(s) \cdot R(s) \\ \therefore \quad C(s) &amp;=\frac{0.84 s+1}{s^{2}} \\ c(t) &amp;=L^{-1}\left[\frac{1+0.84 s}{s^{2}}\right] \\ &amp;=L^{-1}\left[\frac{1}{s^{2}}+\frac{0.84}{s}\right] \\ c(t) &amp;=[t+0.84] U(t) \\ \text{At}\quad t &amp;=1, c(t)=1+0.84=1.84 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 158
% Number: Question 158
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2006, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider a unity-gain feedback control system whose open-loop transfer function is: <br/>[latex]G(s)=\frac{as+1}{s^{2}}[/latex] <br/>
The value of "a" so that the system has a phase-margin equal to [latex]\frac{\pi }{4}[/latex] is approximately equal to</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">2.4</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1.4</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">0.84</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.74</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \mathrm{PM}&amp;=\frac{\pi}{4} \\ \Rightarrow 180+\tan ^{-1} a \omega-180^{\circ}&amp;=\frac{\pi}{4} \\ \tan \frac{\pi}{4}&amp;=\mathrm{a} \omega \\ \Rightarrow\quad a \omega&amp;=1 \end{aligned} [/latex]<br/> Now for gain crossover frequency,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} |G(s)|&amp;=1 \\ \Rightarrow \quad \frac{\sqrt{1+a^{2} \omega^{2}}}{\omega^{2}}&amp;=1 \\ \sqrt{1+1}&amp;=\omega^{2} \quad(\text { as } a \omega=1) \\ f^{2}&amp;=\sqrt{2} \\ \omega&amp;=(2)^{1 / 4} \\ a&amp;=\frac{1}{2^{1 / 4}}=0.84 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2006, Compensators and Controllers
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The transfer function of a phase lead compensator is given by  <br/>[latex]G_{c}(s)=\frac{1+3Ts}{1+Ts}[/latex] where [latex]T \gt 0 [/latex] <br/>The maximum phase shift provide by such a compensator is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{\pi }{2}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{\pi }{3}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{\pi }{4}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{\pi }{6}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Maximum phase shift<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \phi_{m} &amp;=\angle G_{\theta}(s) \\ \phi &amp;=\tan ^{-1} 3 \omega T-\tan ^{-1} \omega T \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> For maximum phase shift<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \frac{d \phi}{d \omega} &amp;=0 \\ \Rightarrow \frac{3 T}{1+(3 T \omega)^{2}} &amp;=\frac{T}{1+(T \omega)^{2}} \\ 3\left[1+(T \omega)^{2}\right] &amp;=1+(3 T \omega)^{2} \\ 3+3 T^{2} \omega^{2} &amp;=1+9 T^{2} \omega^{2} \\ 2 &amp;=6(\omega T)^{2} \\ (\omega T)^{2} &amp;=\frac{1}{3} \\ \omega T=&amp; \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}} \\\phi_{\max } &amp;=\tan ^{-1} 3 \times \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}-\tan ^{-1} \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}} \\
&amp;=\frac{\pi}{3}-\frac{\pi}{6}=\frac{\pi}{6}\end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 161
% Number: Question 161
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2006, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The unit impulse response of a system is [latex]h(t) =  e^{-t},t\geq 0[/latex]. For this system the steady-state value of the output for unit step input is equal to</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">-1</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">1</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\infty[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} h(t) &amp;=e^{-t} \\ H(s) &amp;=\frac{1}{s+1} \\ R(s) &amp;=\frac{1}{s} \\ \text { Output } &amp;=H(s) \cdot R(s)=\frac{1}{(s+1)} \cdot \frac{1}{s} \\ \frac{1}{s(s+1)} &amp;=\frac{A}{s}+\frac{B}{s+1} \\ 1 &amp;=A(s+1)+B s \\ s &amp;=0,1=A \\ s &amp;=-1,1=-B \\ \therefore \quad \text{ Output }&amp;=\frac{1}{s}-\frac{1}{s+1} \\ &amp; =\left(1-e^{-t}\right) u(t) \end{aligned} [/latex]<br/> When [latex]t=\infty[/latex] at steady state. <br/> Output = 1</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 162
% Number: Question 162
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2006, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The positive values of "K" and "a"  so that the system shown in the figures below
oscillates at a frequency of 2 rad/sec respectively are <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20061/q56.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20061/q56.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1, 0.75</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">2, 0.75</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1, 1</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">2, 2</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{array}{l} \text { 1+ } G(s) H(s)=1+\frac{K(s+1)}{s^{3}+a s^{2}+2 s+1}=0 \\ \Rightarrow \frac{s^{3}+a s^{2}+2 s+1+K s+K}{s^{3}+a s^{2}+2 s+1}=0 \end{array}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{c|cc} s^{3}&amp; 1 &amp;2+K\\ s^{2}&amp;a &amp; K+1\\ s&amp;\frac{a(2+K)-(K+1)}{a} \end{array}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} \text { For oscillation, } \frac{a(2+K)-(K+1)}{a}=0 \\ \qquad \begin{array}{l} a=\frac{K+1}{K+2} \\ a s^{2}+K+1=0 \\ s=j \omega, s^{2}=-\omega^{2}=-4 \\ \Rightarrow-4 a+K+1=0 \\ a=\frac{K+1}{4} \Rightarrow \frac{K+1}{4}=\frac{K+1}{K+2} \Rightarrow K=2 \\ a=0.75 \end{array} \end{array}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 163
% Number: Question 163
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2006, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The Nyquist plot of [latex]G(j\omega )[/latex] [latex]H(j\omega )[/latex] for a closed loop control system, passes through
(- 1, j0) point in the GH plane. The gain margin of the system in dB is equal to</div>

[latex]\begin{array}{l} \mathrm{G.M}=20 \log \frac{1}{a} \mathrm{dB} \\ a=1 \quad \therefore \mathrm{G} \cdot \mathrm{M}=0 \end{array}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 164
% Number: Question 164
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2006, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The unit step response of a system starting from rest is given by <br/> [latex]c(t)=1-e^{-2t} \; for \;  t\geq 0[/latex] <br/>The transfer function of the system is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{1+2s}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{2}{2+s}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{2+s}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{2s}{1+2s}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} C(s)&amp;=\frac{1}{s}-\frac{1}{s+2}=\frac{2}{s(s+2)} \\ R(s)&amp;=\frac{1}{s} \quad H(s)=\frac{C(s)}{R(s)}=\frac{2}{s+2} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">In the system shown below, x(t) = (sint)u(t). In steady-state, the response y(t)
will be <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20061/q18.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20061/q18.jpg"/></noscript></div>

% QUESTION 168
% Number: Question 168
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2005, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The open loop transfer function of a unity feedback is given by [latex]G(s)=\frac{3e^{-2s}}{s(s+2)}[/latex]. <br/> The gain and phase crossover frequencies in rad/sec are, respectively</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.632 and 1.26</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.632 and 0.485</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.485 and 0.632</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">1.26 and 0.632</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Gain cross over frequency where gain is 1. <br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} |G(s)| &amp;=1 \\ \Rightarrow \frac{3}{\omega\left(\omega^{2}+4\right)^{12}} &amp;=1 \\ \Rightarrow \frac{9}{\omega^{2}\left(\omega^{2}+4\right)} &amp;=1 \\ \Rightarrow \omega_{g}&amp;=1.26 \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Phase cross over frequency <br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \text{Where }\angle G H&amp;=-180^{\circ} \\ \Rightarrow \quad \omega_{p c}&amp;=0.632\left(\frac{\omega_{\phi}}{2}=\cot 2 \omega_{\phi}\right) \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 169
% Number: Question 169
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2005, Root Locus
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">An unity feedback system is given as<br/> [latex]G(s)=\frac{K(1-s)}{s(s+3)}[/latex]<br/> Indicate the correct root locus diagram.<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20051/q68.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20051/q68.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">A</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">B</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">C</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">D</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} 1+G(s) H(s) &amp;=0 \\ K &amp;=\frac{s^{2}+3 s}{1-s} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> For break away and break in point<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l} \quad \frac{d K}{d s}=(1-s)(2 s+3)+s^{2}+3 s=0 \\ =-s^{2}+2 s+3=0 \\ \Rightarrow s^{2}-2 s-3=0 \\ \Rightarrow(s-3)(s+1)=0 \\ s=3,-1 \end{array}[/latex]<br/> -1 is the breakaway point and 3 is the break in point.</div>

% Number: Question 171
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2005, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A ramp input applied to an unity feedback system results in 5% steady state

% QUESTION 176
% Number: Question 176
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2005, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A linear system is equivalently represented by two sets of state equations : <br/>[latex]\dot{X}=AX+BU \; and \;  W=CW+DU[/latex] <br/>The eigenvalues of the representations are also computed as [latex][\lambda][/latex] and [latex][\mu][/latex]. Which one of the following statements is true ?</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex][\lambda ]=[\mu ]  \; and \;  X=W[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex][\lambda ]=[\mu ]  \; and \;  X\neq W[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex][\lambda ]\neq [\mu ]  \; and \;  X= W[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex][\lambda ]= [\mu ]  \; and \;  X\neq W[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Eigen vlues of [latex]A=[\lambda][/latex]<br/>
Eigen values of  [latex]W=[\mu] [/latex]<br/>
The eigen values of a system are always unique. <br/>[latex]
\mathrm{So},[\lambda]=[\mu] [/latex]<br/>
But a system can be represented by different state
models having different set of state variables.<br/>[latex]
\begin{array}{l}
X=W \\
X \neq W
\end{array}[/latex]<br/>
Both are possible conditions.</div>

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Given [latex]A=\begin{bmatrix} 1 &amp; 0\\ 0&amp;1 \end{bmatrix}[/latex], the state transition matrix [latex]e^{At}[/latex] is given by</div>

% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} [s I-A] &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ll}s &amp; 0 \\0 &amp; s\end{array}\right]-\left[\begin{array}{ll}1 &amp; 0 \\0 &amp; 1\end{array}\right] \\ s I-A] &amp; =\left[\begin{array}{cc}s-1 &amp; 0 \\0 &amp; s-1\end{array}\right] \\ e^{A t} &amp; =[s I-A]^{-1}\\ &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{cc}-\frac{1}{s-1} &amp; 0 \\ 0 &amp; \frac{1}{s-1}\end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}{ll}e^{t} &amp; 0 \\ 0 &amp; e^{t}\end{array}\right] \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 178
% Number: Question 178
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2004, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The state variable equations of a system are : <br/> 1.  [latex]\dot{x_{1}}=-3x_{1}-x_{2}+u[/latex]
<br/>  2. [latex]\dot{x_{2}}=2x_{1}[/latex]
<br/>  [latex]y=x_{1}+u[/latex]
<br/> The system is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">controllable but not observable</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">observable but not controllable</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">neither controllable nor observable</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">controllable and observable</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \left[\begin{array}{c}\dot{x}_{1} \\\dot{x}_{2}\end{array}\right]&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{cc}-3 &amp; -1 \\2 &amp; 0\end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l}x_{1} \\x_{2}\end{array}\right]+\left[\begin{array}{l}1 \\0\end{array}\right] u \\ y&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ll}1 &amp; 0\end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l}x_{1} \\x_{2}\end{array}\right]+\left[\begin{array}{l}1 \\0\end{array}\right] u \\ Q_{C}&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ll}B &amp; A B\end{array}\right]\\ \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>[latex]\begin{aligned} A B&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{c}-3 \\ 2\end{array}\right] \\ \therefore \quad a_{c}&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{cc}1 &amp; -3 \\ 0 &amp; 2\end{array}\right] \neq 0 \\ \therefore \text{Controllable } \\ Q_{0}&amp;=\left[C^{\top} \quad A^{\top} C^{\prime}\right] \\ C^{T}&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{l}1 \\ 0\end{array}\right], \quad A^{T}&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ll}-3 &amp; 2 \\ -1 &amp; 0\end{array}\right] \\ A^{\top} C^{T}&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{l}-3 \\ -1\end{array}\right] \\ \therefore \quad Q_{0}&amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ll}1 &amp; -3 \\ 0 &amp; -1\end{array}\right] \neq 0 \\ \therefore \text{ Observable} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 179
% Number: Question 179
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2004, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">For the polynomial [latex]P(s)=s^{5}+s^{4}+2s^{3}+2s^{2}+3s+15[/latex], the number of roots
which lie in the right half of the s -plane is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">4</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">2</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">3</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]P(s)=s^{5}+s^{4}+2 s^{3}+2 s^{2}+3 s+15 [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{c|ccc} s^{5}&amp; 1 &amp; 2 &amp; 3 \\ s^{4} &amp; 1 &amp; 2 &amp; 15 \\ s^{3} &amp; 0(\epsilon) &amp; -12 &amp; 0 \\ s^{2} &amp; \frac{2 \epsilon+12}{\epsilon} &amp; 15 &amp; 0 \\ s&amp;\frac{\frac{\frac{-12(2 \epsilon+12)}{\epsilon}}{\epsilon }-15 \epsilon }{\frac{\epsilon}{\left(\frac{2 \epsilon+12}{\epsilon}\right)}}&amp;&amp; \end{array}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \frac{2 \epsilon+12}{\epsilon}=t [/latex]<br/> Let [latex]\in[/latex] be a small positive no. <br/>[latex] \frac{-12 t-15 \epsilon}{t} [/latex]<br/>[latex] s \Rightarrow-12-\frac{15 \epsilon}{t} [/latex]<br/>[latex] s ^{0}\quad 15[/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore [/latex] Two sign change from [latex]s^{2}[/latex] to s and s to [latex]s^{0} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore [/latex] 2 roots on RHS of s plane.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 180
% Number: Question 180
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2004, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The open-loop transfer function of a unity feedback system is [latex]G(s)=\frac{K}{s(s^{2}+s+2)(s+3)}[/latex]. The range of K for which the system is stable is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{21}{4} \gt K \gt 0[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]13 \gt K \gt 0[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{21}{4} \lt K \lt \infty[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]-6 \lt K \lt \infty[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{array}{l} G(s)=\frac{K}{s\left(s^{2}+s+2\right)(s+3)} \\ H(s)=1\\ 1+G(s) H(s)=1+\frac{K}{s\left(s^{3}+3 s^{2}+s^{2}+3 s+2 s+6\right)} \\ =\frac{s^{4}+4 s^{3}+5 s^{2}+6 s+K}{s\left(s^{3}+4 s^{2}+5 s+6\right)} \\ 1+G(s) H(s)=0 \end{array}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l|ll}s^{4} &amp; 1 &amp; 5\;K \\ s^{3} &amp; 4 &amp; 6 \\ s^{2} &amp; \frac{7}{2} &amp; k \\ s &amp; \frac{\frac{7}{2} \times 6-4 K}{\frac{7}{2}} &amp; 0 \\ s^{0} &amp; k &amp; \frac{7}{2}\end{array} [/latex]<br/> For system to be stable, [latex]K \gt 0[/latex] <br/>[latex] (21-4 K) \cdot \frac{2}{7}\gt 0 [/latex]<br/>[latex] \frac{21}{4} \gt K \Rightarrow K\lt \frac{21}{4} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore \quad \frac{21}{4} \gt K \gt 0[/latex]</div>

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider the signal flow graph shown in Fig. The gain [latex]\frac{X_{5}}{X_{1}}[/latex] is<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20041/q68.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20041/q68.jpg"/></noscript></div>

% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1-(be+cf+dg)+bedg}{abcd}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{array}{l} P_{1}=a b c d, \quad \Delta_{1}=1 \\ L_{1}=b e, L_{2}=c f, L_{3}=d g \\ \text { Non-touching loops are } L_{1} \&amp; L_{3}=b e d g \\ \therefore \quad \frac{X_{5}}{X_{1}}=\frac{a b c d}{1-(b e+c f+d g)+b e d g} \end{array}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 183
% Number: Question 183
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2004, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A causal system having the transfer function H(s)=1/(s+2) is excited with
10u(t). The time at which the output reaches 99% of its steady state value is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">2.7 sec</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">2.5 sec</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">2.3 sec</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">2.1 sec</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} H(s) &amp;=\frac{1}{s+2} \\ r(t) &amp;=10 u(t) \\ R(s) &amp;=\frac{10}{s} \\ C(s) &amp;=H(s) \cdot R(s)=\frac{1}{s+2} \cdot \frac{10}{s} \\ \Rightarrow \quad \frac{10}{s(s+2)} &amp;=\frac{A}{s}+\frac{B}{s+2} \\ 10 &amp;=A(s+2)+B s \\ s &amp;=0,10=2 A \\ \Rightarrow \quad A &amp;=5 \\ s &amp;=-2,10=-2 B \\ \Rightarrow \quad B &amp;=-5 \\ \therefore \quad C(s) &amp;=\frac{5}{s}-\frac{5}{s+2} \\ c(t) &amp;=5\left[1-e^{2 t}\right] u(t) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Steady state value when t=0 is 5.99% of steady<br/> state value reaches at<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} &amp; 5\left[1-e^{2 t}\right]=0.99 \times 5 \\ \Rightarrow \quad &amp; 1-e^{-2 t}=0.99 \\ &amp; e^{2 t}=0.1 \\ \Rightarrow \quad &amp;-2 t=\ln 0.1 \\ \Rightarrow \quad &amp; t=2.3 \mathrm{sec} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 184
% Number: Question 184
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2004, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A system described by the following differential equation [latex]\frac{d^{2}y}{dt^{2}}+3\frac{dy}{dt}+2y=x(t)[/latex] is initially at rest. For input x(t)=2u(t), the output y(t) is</div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex](1-2e^{-t}+e^{-2t})u(t)[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex](1+2e^{-t}-2e^{-2t})u(t)[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex](0.5+2e^{-t}+1.5e^{-2t})u(t)[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex](0.5+2e^{-t}+2e^{-2t})u(t)[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \frac{d^{2} y}{d t^{2}}+\frac{3 d y}{d t}+2 y &amp;=X(t) \\ s^{2} Y(s)+3 s Y(s)+2 Y(s) &amp;=X(s) \\ x(t) &amp;=2 u(t) \\ X(s) &amp;=\frac{2}{s} \\ \therefore \quad\left(s^{2}+3 s+2\right) Y(s) &amp;=\frac{2}{s} \\ r(s) &amp;=\frac{2}{s(s+2)(s+1)} \\ \frac{2}{s(s+2)(s+1)} &amp;=\frac{A}{s}+\frac{B}{s+2}+\frac{C}{s+1} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} 2&amp;=A(s+2)(s+1)+B s(s+1)+C(s+2) s \\ s&amp;=0,2=2 A \Rightarrow A=1 \\ s&amp;=-1,2=-C \Rightarrow C=-2 \\ s&amp;=-2,2=2 B \Rightarrow B=1 \\ Y(s)&amp;=\frac{1}{s}+\frac{1}{s+2}-\frac{2}{s+1} \\ y(t)&amp;=(1+e^{-2 t}-2 e^{-t}) u(t)  \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 186
% Number: Question 186
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2004, Root Locus
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Given [latex]G(s)H(s)=\frac{K}{s(s+1)(s+3)}[/latex],  the point of intersection of the asymptotes of

% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">-4</div>

[latex]\text { Centroid }=\frac{\Sigma P-\Sigma Z}{P-Z}=\frac{-1-3}{3}=-1.33[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 188
% Number: Question 188
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2003, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The zero-input response of a system given by the state-space equation <br/>[latex]\begin{bmatrix} \dot{X_1}\\\dot{X_2} \end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix} 1 &amp;0 \\ 1&amp; 1 \end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix} X_1\\X_2 \end{bmatrix}[/latex] and [latex]\begin{bmatrix} X_1(0)\\X_2(0) \end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix} 1\\0 \end{bmatrix}[/latex] is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\begin{bmatrix} te^{t}\\t \end{bmatrix}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\begin{bmatrix} e^{t}\\t \end{bmatrix}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\begin{bmatrix} e^{t}\\t e^{t} \end{bmatrix}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\begin{bmatrix} t\\t e^{t} \end{bmatrix}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} (s I-A) &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{ll} s &amp; 0 \\ 0 &amp; s \end{array}\right]-\left[\begin{array}{ll} 1 &amp; 0 \\ 1 &amp; 1 \end{array}\right] \\ &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{cc} s-1 &amp; 0 \\ -1 &amp; s-1 \end{array}\right] \\ (s I-A)^{-1} &amp;=\frac{\left[\begin{array}{cc} (s-1) &amp; 0 \\ +1 &amp; (s-1) \end{array}\right]}{(s-1)^{2}}\\ &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{cc}\frac{1}{s-1} &amp; 0 \\ \frac{+1}{(s-1)^{2}} &amp; \frac{1}{s-1}\end{array}\right] \\ L^{-1}[s I-A]^{-1}&amp;=e^{A t}=\left[\begin{array}{cc}e^{t} &amp; 0 \\ t e^{t} &amp; e^{t}\end{array}\right] \\ &amp;=\left[\begin{array}{cc}e^{t} &amp; 0 \\ t e^{t} &amp; e^{t}\end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}{l}1 \\ 0\end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}{c}e^{t} \\ t e^{t}\end{array}\right] \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">D</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">

% QUESTION 192
% Number: Question 192
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2003, Root Locus
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The root locus of system [latex]G(s)H(s)=\frac{K}{s(s+2)(s+3)}[/latex] has the break-away point
located at</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">(- 0.5,0)</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">(- 2.548,0)</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">(- 4,0)</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">(- 0.784,0)</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{array}{l} 1+G(s) H(s)=0 \\ \Rightarrow 1+\frac{K}{s(s+2)(s+3)}=0 \\ \Rightarrow 1+\frac{K}{s\left(s^{2}+5 s+6\right)}=0 \\ \Rightarrow \quad K=-1 \cdot\left(s^{3}+5 s^{2}+6 s\right) \\ \Rightarrow \frac{d K}{d s}=-\left(3 s^{2}+10 s+6\right) \\ \text{For break away point  } \frac{d K}{d s}=0 \\ \Rightarrow 3 s^{2}+10 s+6=0 \\ s=\frac{-10 \pm \sqrt{100-72}}{6} \\ =\frac{-10 \pm 5.3}{6}=-0.784,-2.55 \\ \end{array}[/latex]<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20031/a69.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20031/a69.jpg"/></noscript><br/>
Centroid  [latex]=\frac{-2-3}{3}=\frac{-5}{3}=-1.66 [/latex]<br/>
angle of asymtotes  [latex]=(2 K+1) \frac{\pi}{P-Z}   [/latex]<br/>[latex]
=(2 K+1) \frac{\pi}{3} [/latex]<br/>[latex]
\frac{\pi}{3}, \pi, \frac{5 \pi}{3} [/latex]<br/>[latex]
\therefore  [/latex] Break away point is (-0.784,0)
as -2.55 does not lie on RL.</div>

% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The signal flow graph of a system is shown in Fig. below. The transfer function C(s)/R(s) of the system is  <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20031/q68.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20031/q68.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{6}{s^{2}+29s+6}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{6s}{s^{2}+29s+6}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{s(s+2)}{s^{2}+29s+6}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{s(s+27)}{s^{2}+29s+6}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} P_{1}&amp;=1 \\ \Delta_{1}&amp;=1+\frac{3}{s}+\frac{24}{s}=\frac{s+27}{s}\\ G(s) &amp;=\frac{P_{1} \Delta_{1}}{1-(\text { loop gain })+\text { pair of non- touching loops }} \\ L_{1}&amp;=\frac{-3}{s}, L_{2} =\frac{-24}{s}, L_{3}=\frac{-2}{s} \end{aligned} [/latex]<br/>[latex] L_{1}[/latex] and [latex]L_{3}[/latex] are non-touching.<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \therefore G(s)&amp;=\frac{\frac{(s+27)}{s}}{1-\left(\frac{-3}{s}-\frac{24}{s}-\frac{2}{s}\right)+\frac{-2}{s} \times \frac{-3}{s}} \\ &amp;=\frac{\frac{(s+27)}{s}}{1+\frac{29}{s}+\frac{6}{s^{2}}} \\ \frac{C(s)}{R(s)} &amp;=\frac{s(s+27)}{s^{2}+29 s+6} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 194
% Number: Question 194
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2003, Compensators and Controllers
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">A PD controller is used to compensate a system. Compared to the uncompensated system, the compensated system has</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">a higher type number</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">reduced damping</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">higher noise amplification</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">larger transient overshoot</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
A PD controller does not effect the type of the
system.<br/>
It reduces the overshoot and increases the
damping. <br/>
It increases the bandwidth therefor SNR
decreases,
System becomes more prone to noise</div>

% QUESTION 196
% Number: Question 196
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2002, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The system with the open loop transfer function [latex]G(s)H(s)=\frac{1}{s(s^{2}+s+1)}[/latex] has a gain margin of</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">- 6 dB</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">0dB</div>

% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">6dB</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]G(s) H(s)=\frac{1}{s\left(s^{2}+s+1\right)}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \omega [/latex] when [latex] \angle G(s) H(s)=-180^{\circ} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} -180 &amp;=-90-\tan ^{-1} \frac{\omega}{1-\omega^{2}} \\ -90 &amp;=-\tan ^{-1} \frac{\omega}{1-\omega^{2}} \\ 1-\omega^{2} &amp;=0 \\ \omega_{\alpha c} &amp;=1 \mathrm{rad} / \mathrm{sec} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Value of gain at [latex]\omega_{\rho c}=1 [/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} |G(s) H(s)| &amp;=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\left(\left(1-\omega^{2}\right)^{2}+|j \omega|^{2}\right.}} \\ \therefore \quad G \cdot M \cdot &amp;=-20 \log 1=0 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 197
% Number: Question 197
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2002, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The characteristic polynomial of a system is <br/>[latex]q (s) = 2s^{5}+s^{4}+4s^{3}+2s^{2}+2s+1[/latex]. <br/>The system is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">stable</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">marginally stable</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">unstable</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">oscillatory</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
Routh table is<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{c|ccc} s^{5} &amp; 2(1) &amp; 4(2) &amp; 2(1) \\ s^{4} &amp; 1 &amp; 2 &amp; 1 \\ s^{3} &amp; 0 &amp; 0 &amp; 0 \\ s^{2} &amp; &amp; &amp; \\ s^{1} &amp; &amp; &amp; \\ s^{0} &amp; &amp; &amp; \end{array}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \therefore \frac{d}{d s}\left(s^{4}+2 s^{2}+1\right) &amp;=0 \\ 4 s^{3}+4 s &amp;=0 \\ s &amp;=\pm j, s=\pm j \\ \frac{d}{d s}\left(s^{2}+1\right) &amp;=0 \\ 2 s &amp;=0 \\ \Rightarrow s &amp;=0 \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Double roots on imaginary axis so system is unstable</div>

% QUESTION 198
% Number: Question 198
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2002, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The transfer function of a system is [latex]G(s)=\frac{100}{(s+1)(s+100)}[/latex]. For a unit step input to the system the approximate settling time for 2% criterion is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">100sec</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">4sec</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1sec</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">0.01sec</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]G(s)=\frac{100}{(s+1)(s+100)}[/latex]<br/> Taking dominant pole consideration, <br/>[latex] s=-100[/latex] pole is not taken.<br/>[latex] \therefore \quad G(s)=\frac{100}{s+1} [/latex]<br/> Now it is 1 st order system <br/>[latex] \therefore \quad t_{s}=4 T=4 \times 1=4 \mathrm{s}[/latex]</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 199
% Number: Question 199
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2002, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The system shown in figure remains stable when <br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20021/q2.14.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20021/q2.14.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]K \lt -1[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]- 1\lt K \lt 1[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]1\lt K \lt 3[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]K \lt -3[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\frac{Y(s)}{R(s)}=\frac{\frac{K}{s}}{1-\left(\frac{3}{s}+\frac{K}{s}\right)}=\frac{K}{s-(3+K)} [/latex]<br/> For system to be stable,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} 3+K&amp; \lt 0 \\ \Rightarrow \quad K&amp; \lt -3 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 200
% Number: Question 200
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2002, State Space Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The transfer function Y(s)U(s) of a system described by the state equations
x(t) = -2x(t) + 2u(t) and y(t) = 0.5x(t) is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{0.5}{(s-2)}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{(s-2)}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{0.5}{(s+2)}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{(s+2)}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \dot{x}(t)=-2 x(t)+2 u(t) &amp;\ldots(i)\\ y(t)=0.5 x(t)&amp;\ldots(ii) \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> From (i), Taking Laplace transform of (i)<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} s X(s)&amp;=-2 X(s)+2 U(s)\\ X(s)[s+2]&amp;=2 U(s)\\ \Rightarrow \quad X(s)&amp;=\frac{2 U(s)}{(s+2)} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> Taking Laplace transform of (ii)<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} Y(s)&amp;=0.5 X(s) \\ Y(s)&amp;=\frac{0.5 \times 2 U(s)}{s+2} \\ \therefore \quad \frac{Y(s)}{U(s)}&amp;=\frac{1}{(s+2)} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 203
% Number: Question 203
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2002, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">Consider a system with the transfer function [latex]G(s)=\frac{s+6}{ks^{2}+s+6}[/latex]. Its damping ratio will be 0.5 when the value of k is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{2}{6}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">3</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{1}{6}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">6</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{array}{l} \frac{s+6}{k\left(s^{2}+\frac{s}{k}+\frac{6}{k}\right)} \\ \text { Comparing with } s^{2}+2 \xi \omega_{n}+\omega_{n}^{2} \\ \begin{aligned} \omega_{n}&amp;=\sqrt{\frac{6}{k}} \\ 2 \xi \omega_{n}&amp;=\frac{1}{k} \\ 2 \times 0.5 \times \sqrt{\frac{6}{k}}&amp;=\frac{1}{k} \\ \Rightarrow \qquad \frac{6}{k}&amp;=\frac{1}{k^{2}} \\ k&amp;=\frac{1}{6} \end{aligned} \end{array}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 204
% Number: Question 204
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2001, Stability Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The feedback control system in figure is stable<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20011/q42.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20011/q42.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">For all [latex]K\geq 0[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Only If [latex]K\geq 1[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">Only If [latex]0\leq K \lt 1[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">Only If [latex]0\leq K\leq 2[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} T . F . &amp;=\frac{G_{1} G_{2}}{1+G_{1} G_{2} H} \\ &amp;=\frac{\frac{K(s-2)}{(s+2)^{2}}}{1+\frac{K(s-2)(s-2)}{(s+2)^{2}}} \\ &amp;=\frac{K(s-2)}{(s+2)^{2}+K(s-2)^{2}} \end{aligned} [/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore [/latex] Characteristic equation<br/>[latex] =s^{2}+4+4 s+K s^{2}-4 K s+4 K [/latex]<br/>[latex] (1+K) s^{2}+K(4-4 K) s+4 K+4=0 [/latex]<br/> Routh table is<br/>[latex] \begin{array}{l|ll} s^{2} &amp; (1+K) &amp; (4 K+4) \\ s^{1} &amp; K(4-4 K) &amp; 0 \\ s^{0} &amp; 4(K+1) &amp; \end{array}[/latex]<br/> For system to be stable,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} 1-K&amp; \gt 0\\ 1&amp; \gt K \text{ and } K \geq 0 \quad \text{ (given in question)} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/> From third row,<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} K&amp; \gt -1 \\ \therefore \quad 0 &amp;\leq K \lt 1 \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 205
% Number: Question 205
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2001, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The open-loop DC gain of a unity negative feedback system with closed-loop
transfer function [latex]\frac{s+4}{s^{2}+7s+13}[/latex] is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{4}{13}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{4}{9}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">4</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">13</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} \mathrm{CLTF} &amp;=\frac{G(s)}{1+G(s) H(s)}=\frac{s+4}{s^{2}+7 s+13} \\ \frac{1+G(s) H(s)}{G(s)} &amp;=\frac{s^{2}+7 s+13}{s+4} \end{aligned} [/latex]<br/>[latex] H(s)=1[/latex] for unity feedback.<br/>[latex] \begin{aligned} \frac{1}{G(s)} &amp;=\frac{s^{2}+7 s+13}{s+4}-1 \\ \frac{1}{G(s)} &amp;=\frac{s^{2}+6 s+9}{s+4} \\ \therefore \quad G(s) &amp;=\frac{s+4}{s^{2}+6 s+9} \\ \text { For D.C. } \quad s&amp;=0\\ \therefore \quad G(s) &amp;=\text{open loop gain} \\ &amp;=\frac{4}{9} \end{aligned}[/latex]</div>

% QUESTION 206
% Number: Question 206
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2001, Basics of Control Systems, Block Diagram and SFGs
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">An electrical system and its signal-flow graph representations are shown in figure
(a) and (b) respectively. The values of [latex]G_{2}[/latex] and H, respectively are<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20011/q40.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20011/q40.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{Z_{3}(s)}{Z_{2}(s)+Z_{3}(s)+Z_{4}(s)},\frac{-Z_{3}(s)}{Z_{1}(s)+Z_{3}(s)}[/latex]</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{-Z_{3}(s)}{Z_{2}(s)-Z_{3}(s)+Z_{4}(s)},\frac{-Z_{3}(s)}{Z_{1}(s)+Z_{3}(s)}[/latex]</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{Z_{3}(s)}{Z_{2}(s)+Z_{3}(s)+Z_{4}(s)},\frac{Z_{3}(s)}{Z_{1}(s)+Z_{3}(s)}[/latex]</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">[latex]\frac{-Z_{3}(s)}{Z_{2}(s)-Z_{3}(s)+Z_{4}(s)},\frac{Z_{3}(s)}{Z_{1}(s)+Z_{3}(s)}[/latex]</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
From KVL in both loops In first loop, <br/>[latex] V_{i}(s)=I_{1}(s) Z_{1}(s)+\left[I_{1}(s)-I_{2}(s)\right] Z_{3}(s) [/latex]<br/>[latex] V_{i}(s)=I_{1}(s)\left[Z_{1}(s)+Z_{3}(s)\right]-I_{2}(s) Z_{3}(s) [/latex]<br/>[latex] \frac{V_{i}(s)}{Z_{1}(s)+Z_{3}(s)}=I_{1}(s)-\frac{I_{2}(s) \cdot Z_{3}(s)}{Z_{1}(s)+Z_{3}(s)} \; \; ...(i)[/latex]<br/> In second loop, <br/>[latex] \left[I_{2}(s)-I_{1}(s)\right] Z_{3}(s)+I_{2}(s) Z_{2}(s)+I_{2}(s) Z_{4}(s)=0 [/latex]<br/>[latex] I_{2}(s)\left(Z_{2}(s)+Z_{3}(s)+Z_{4}(s)\right)=I_{1}(s) \cdot Z_{3}(s) [/latex]<br/>[latex] G_{2}=\frac{I_{2}(s)}{I_{1}(s)}=\frac{Z_{3}(s)}{Z_{2}(s)+Z_{3}(s)+Z_{4}(s)} [/latex]<br/> From SFG, [latex]I_{1}(s)=V_{i} G_{1}(s)+I_{2}(s) H(s) [/latex]<br/>[latex] I_{1}(s)=V_{i}(s) \frac{1}{Z_{1}(s)+Z_{3}(s)}+I_{2}(s) \frac{Z_{3}(s)}{Z_{1}(s)+Z_{3}(s)} [/latex]<br/> [latex] \therefore \quad H(s)=\frac{Z_{3}(s)}{Z_{1}(s)+Z_{3}(s)} [/latex]<br/> (comparing above two equations)</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

% QUESTION 207
% Number: Question 207
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2001, Frequency Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">The Nyquist plot for the open-loop transfer function G(s) of a unity negative
feedback system is shown in figure. If G(s) has no pole in the right half of splane, the number of roots of the system characteristic equation in the right half
of s-plane is<br/><img data-lazyloaded="1" data-src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20011/q17.jpg" decoding="async" src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODdhAQABAPAAAMPDwwAAACwAAAAAAQABAAACAkQBADs="/><noscript><img decoding="async" src="/wp-content/uploads/GATE/EC/20011/q17.jpg"/></noscript></div>
% Option A (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">0</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">1</div>
% Option C (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">2</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">3</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]N=0[/latex]<br/> (1 encirclement in CW direction and other in ACW)<br/>[latex] P=0 [/latex](no pole in right half)<br/> So, [latex]N=P-Z[/latex]<br/>[latex] Z=P-N=0[/latex]<br/>[latex] \therefore \quad [/latex] No roots on RH of s-plane.</div>

% QUESTION 209
% Number: Question 209
% Type: MCQ | Marks: 1 Mark
% Tags: GATE EC 2001, Time Response Analysis
% Text HTML: <div class="question_text" data-value="1">If the characteristic equation of a closed-loop system is [latex]s^{2}+2s+2=0[/latex], then the system is</div>
% Option A (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">over damped</div>
% Option B (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">critically damped</div>
% Option C (Correct=True): <div class="option_data">underdamped</div>
% Option D (Correct=False): <div class="option_data">undamped</div>
% Explanation HTML: <div class="mtq_explanation-text">
[latex]\begin{aligned} s^{2}+2 \xi \omega_{n}+\omega_{n}^{2} &amp;=0 \\ 2 \xi\omega{n} &amp;=2, \xi=\frac{1}{\omega_{n}} \\ \omega_{n} &amp;=\sqrt{2}\\ \therefore \quad \xi&amp;=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}} \end{aligned}[/latex]<br/>[latex] \because \xi \lt 1,[/latex] hence response will be underdamped.</div>

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
